## Snapshot
The only scaled IDM left that straddles both sides of the chip value chain: it **designs** x86 CPUs / accelerators / custom silicon (sold through the **Intel Products** business — segments **CCG** client PCs and **DCAI** data-center & AI) and **manufactures** them (plus, increasingly, chips for external customers) through **Intel Foundry** — leading-edge nodes (Intel 3, 18A, 14A) and advanced packaging (EMIB/EMIB-T, Foveros). Three reportable segments — CCG, DCAI, Intel Foundry — plus an "all other" bucket (Mobileye; Altera deconsolidated after the 51% divestiture closed 2025-09-12) (10-K FY25, 2026-01-23). FY25 revenue $52.9B, roughly flat y/y on supply constraints (Q4 FY25 call, 2026-01-22). The entire thesis is the turnaround under CEO Lip-Bu Tan (in seat ~15 months): get **18A** to yield, win a **first major external 14A/foundry customer**, and turn the foundry P&L from a ~$2.4B/qtr loss toward break-even by exit-2027 — against a near-breakeven product P&L and a heavily restructured, government- and partner-recapitalized balance sheet.

## At a glance — product · buyer · supplier
| | |
|---|---|
| **Sells (top 3)** | 1) x86 client PC CPUs (CCG) · 2) data-center/server CPUs + AI accelerators (DCAI) · 3) leading-edge foundry wafers + advanced packaging (Intel Foundry) |
| **Main buyer(s)** | PC OEMs (Dell/HP/Lenovo) + data-center/hyperscalers for server; external foundry customers still tiny (3rd-party foundry+A&T just $174M/qtr) |
| **Key suppliers** | WFE/materials: ASML, AMAT, LRCX, KLAC; substrates and EUV photoresist |

## Position in the value chain
Intel is the rare name that occupies the **middle box and reaches into both neighbors**: it buys WFE/materials from the same suppliers as TSMC, designs its own CPUs/accelerators, fabs them in-house, and now sells leading-edge wafers + advanced packaging to *external* customers — i.e. it competes with its own foundry suppliers' biggest customer (TSMC) while remaining their customer. The swing factors are the **18A ramp** (yields, Panther Lake → Nova Lake → server) and **landing the first big external foundry/14A customer** plus the **packaging-as-trojan-horse** wins (EMIB/EMIB-T).

## Current state (latest quarter)

**🆕🔴 2026-08-25 — INTEL'S HOT CHIPS SHOWING IS THE MOST SUBSTANTIVE PRODUCT DISCLOSURE ON THIS PAGE THIS QUARTER, AND EVERY PART OF IT RUNS ON INTEL'S OWN NODES AND PACKAGING.** Three architectures presented (Hot Chips 2026, recapped by TMT Breakout, Jefferies Asia TMT sales and TrendForce via JPM, all 2026-08-25):
- **Diamond Rapids (Xeon 7, now slated for 2027)** — a new **"fan-out" architecture separating scalable compute building blocks from centralised memory and I/O hubs**. **Intel 18A-P**, **up to 256 P-cores**, **1.28 GB of shared last-level cache**, **16 memory channels at 12,800 MT/s**, **128 lanes of PCIe Gen6 with CXL 3.0**, core chiplets stacked on a base die via **Foveros Direct 3D bonding**, hubs connected over **UCIe-S**. *"Uniform memory latency was the central architectural priority"* — every compute block connects directly to every memory/IO hub, avoiding hops through neighbouring tiles.
- **Crescent Island** — a **350W air-cooled PCIe datacenter GPU tuned for inference economics rather than peak training throughput**, with **up to 480GB of LPDDR5X** and **FP4** support, framed around *"larger models, longer context windows and more concurrent agents inside existing data centers."* ⚠️ **"No independent pricing or total-cost comparison against [[NVDA]] or [[AMD]] parts has been published, so the advantage remains to be demonstrated."**
- **Wildcat Lake / Intel Core Series 3** — **Intel 18A**, NPU rated **up to 17 TOPS (40 platform TOPS)**, **the FIRST use of UCIe in an Intel processor**, and it **drops Foveros for a simpler organic multi-chip package** to cut cost.
🔴 **The framing that matters for the foundry thesis:** *"all three lean on **in-house nodes (18A/18A-P) and packaging** — evidence Intel is trying to **prove out its foundry roadmap on its own products before winning external customers**"* (TrendForce via JPM · Joshua Meyers, 2026-08-25).

**🆕🔴 2026-08-25 — AND THE PACKAGING ON-RAMP JUST GOT ITS SECOND NAMED EXTERNAL WIN.** *"**SK Hynix confirmed it will use Intel's EMIB advanced packaging for next-gen HBM memory chips**, joining Apple, MediaTek and other top [[TSM]] clients in a win for Intel… the victory is **mainly due to the continued shortage of TSMC's CoWoS capacity**"* (@dnystedt, corpus, relaying Taiwan media, 2026-08-25). **JPM's Asia Tech Tour reaches the same place from the other side:** *"we believe **Intel may secure some project wins in PACKAGING (e.g. EMIB-T for TPU v9)**; however, **at the front end we still do not see any major project wins**, given the technology lag — **Intel 18A is comparable to TSMC N3E**, and by the time **Intel 14A enters mass production in 2028 we expect the TSMC N2 family to already be approaching a $50-60bn revenue run rate** — and the typical **3-5 year engagement cycle** from initial design to mass production"* (JPM · Gokul Hariharan, 2026-08-25).
⚠️ **Two independent constraints on the EMIB story from the substrate side, same day:** Unimicron told JPM that **EMIB-T substrate is MORE COMPLEX than CoWoS** *"as the workload of IC package is increasingly reliant on substrate in the absence of interposer,"* that **three substrate makers are involved**, and that **"satisfying yields are still a work in progress"** (JPM · Jerry Tsai, 2026-08-25). And AT&S's CTO — a direct beneficiary — is **"bullish on the opportunities for EMIB and embedding power in the substrate" while "skeptical on COWOP"** (Jefferies Asia TMT sales · Conor O'Mara + Lucy Rowe, call with AT&S IR Gebhardt Philipp, 2026-08-24). ➤ **Net: the packaging on-ramp is now corroborated by a memory customer, a covering analyst, a substrate supplier and a substrate customer — while the FRONT-END funnel is explicitly rejected by the same analyst. Those are separable claims and this page should keep them separate.**

**🆕 2026-08-18 (/run-inbox) — INTEL'S SILICON-PHOTONICS/CPO GROUP IS NOT DEAD: IT PUBLISHED A WORKING 16-WAVELENGTH CPO LINK. AN ENGINEER-LED TEARDOWN OF THE PAPER GRADES IT "C+, MAYBE B−" AGAINST NVIDIA'S "A+" — AND THE REASON IS AN ARCHITECTURAL CHOICE, NOT A PROCESS GAP.** (Irrational Analysis — "Intel CPO Group is Alive? + Very Interesting NVDA/LITE/AEVA Bonus Papers", 2026-08-17. ⚠️ **Independent engineering substack, NOT a broker — no rating, no PT, no model, and self-declared "heavily invested in the semiconductor industry." Read as a technical referee, not as a recommendation.**)
- **WHY THIS IS ON THE PAGE AT ALL: the author's stated prior was that the group was finished — "who cares, they suffered massive attrition, got re-org-ed like 5 times and are dead" — and he is publicly revising it on the evidence of the papers.** ➤ **A negative prior being updated by a hostile reader is a stronger signal than a positive claim from a friendly one. The optionality this page carries on Intel photonics should be marked up from ~zero, but only to "alive," not to "competitive."**
- **WHAT INTEL DEMONSTRATED, on the reviewer's own read: a hybrid-integrated laser platform (gratings etched into silicon, architecturally closer to Scintil than to the OpenLight/[[TSEM]] approach) delivering MULTI-WAVELENGTH output at lower power, ±10 GHz wavelength-spacing accuracy ("insanely good"), a 16-wavelength laser array with "excellent flatness," ~5 dB extinction ratio across all rings ("usually rings give 3-4 dB"), 75 dB TIA gain, and ~1.3 dB average coupling loss through a two-lens attachable connector.** ➤ **The physical/process execution is genuinely praised. That is the part that took the attrition years to rebuild, and it appears rebuilt.**
- 🔴 **WHERE IT FAILS, AND IT IS THE SAME ROOT CAUSE THREE TIMES: SOA four-wave mixing. Intel amplifies all 8 modulated wavelengths through a single SOA, and every pair of wavelengths spawns two FWM tones that land in-band as RIN.** The reviewer's specific charges: **the claimed 50 dB SMSR is "a lie… real number is 30 dB," achieved by clipping the spectrum to hide out-of-band FWM tones**; the claimed BER of <1e-12 across all channels is not supported because it is quoted at PERFECT timing rather than across a sampling window; and the Rx design compounds it — **three cascaded MZIs to de-mux "is stupid," kills link budget, forces a second SOA stage and mandatory polarization control, adding "even more FWM crap polluting RIN."**
- 🔴 **THE COMPETITIVE BENCHMARK, WHICH IS THE ACTIONABLE PART: [[NVDA]] specifies BER of 1e-12 across a 0.47 UI sampling offset WITH clock forwarding (so Tx jitter is tracked and there is no PPM offset). Intel is not clock-forwarding. At a 0.47 UI total sampling offset "all of the Intel CPO lanes would catastrophically fail"; generously granting Intel a 0.2 UI window still puts it at ~1e-5 BER.** ➤ **Translate for the thesis: this is not a "one node behind" gap that a fab advantage closes. Clock forwarding plus avoiding shared-SOA amplification are ARCHITECTURAL decisions, and NVIDIA made both. Intel can improve the silicon and still lose the link budget. Anyone underwriting Intel photonics as a 2027-28 CPO share story needs a specific answer on the SOA and clocking architecture, not on yield.** ⚠️ **Single independent source, no second technical opinion, and the reviewer describes his own write-up as "lazy low-quality stream-of-consciousness" produced ahead of Hot Chips. Logged as a technical flag to re-test at Hot Chips rather than as a settled finding.** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/IA_18_ago.html)

**NEW / BULL-CATALYST 2026-08-18 — "SCARCITY IS WHAT ULTIMATELY SAVED INTEL."** Ben Thompson's foundry argument is that no rational hyperscaler would pay the insurance cost of qualifying Intel while TSMC was ample and easier to use. Acute leading-edge scarcity changes that arithmetic: foregone AI revenue can exceed the engineering pain of making Intel a real foundry partner, and the customer itself supplies the process/culture learning Intel lacks. Thompson expects a major partner announcement in the near term; this is an analyst expectation, not a disclosed customer win. The risk is unchanged: the thesis only works if that scarcity bridge lasts long enough for yield, packaging, IP and service culture to become competitive (Ben Thompson, *Invest Like the Best*, 2026-08-18).

Packaging EMIB-T/CoPoS Ramps", 2026-08-16 — the note flags that MTK is covered by Mizuho's Kevin Wang in HK). **Keep the hedges: "potentially" on TPUv9, and "first high-volume customer" is dated 2028E, not now.**
- 🔴⚠️ **THIS IS A DIRECT COLLISION WITH THREE THINGS ALREADY ON THIS PAGE AND ON [[MEDIATEK]], AND THE NUMBERS ARE NOT NETTED — BECAUSE THE MISSING VARIABLE IS *WHICH LEVEL* THE YIELD IS MEASURED AT.** The wiki's existing marks: **(a) Fubon (2026-07-08): [[GOOG]] asked Intel to lift EMIB-T yield to 95%+ before production, and Fubon judged hitting that by mid-2027 "very challenging," concluding Google would fall back on TSMC's 2028 CoWoS expansion; (b) DIGITIMES/@dnystedt (2026-08-03): package yield "now exceed 90%" but "a 50% SUBSTRATE YIELD REMAINS THE BOTTLENECK"; (c) JPM via [[MEDIATEK]] (2026-08-16): "~60% at SUBSTRATE level and ~90% at CHIP-ON-SUBSTRATE level, per our checks," with Unimicron's own ramp target ~50% at substrate and Intel providing commercial protection to substrate vendors; (d) UBS (2026-07-26): "packaging validation yields ~90-92%," substrate fabrication "possibly ~50% vs >80% for HPC ABF substrates."** ➤ **🔴 MIZUHO DOES NOT SAY WHICH LEVEL ITS >97% REFERS TO. That omission is the whole story: if >97% is the BRIDGE or chip-on-substrate step, it is a plausible improvement on the ~90-92% cluster and says nothing about the substrate bottleneck every other source names as binding. If it is the SUBSTRATE step, it contradicts four independent sources (~50-60%) and would be the single most important datapoint on this page. Until the basis is stated, >97% CANNOT be compared with Fubon's 95%+ requirement, and must not be used to declare the Google yield gate cleared.** All five marks are retained side by side; none supersedes another.
- ⚠️ **The other live counter-evidence stays on the page unchanged: a competitor's IR, quoted by an analyst on 07-28, reportedly said EMIB "is struggling" / "doesn't work"** (BofA · Vivek Arya relay, see [themes/cowos-packaging](themes/cowos-packaging.md)), and **Unimicron said Intel's EMIB-T is unlikely to reach full mass production before 2027**. ➤ **So the current state of the evidence is: one house at >97% with no basis, four sources bracketing substrate yield at ~50-60%, a rival's IR saying it does not work, and the CUSTOMER (MediaTek management, 07-31) saying on the record "we are more committed to EMIB-T." The customer's own commitment is the strongest single piece of evidence here and it is unchanged.**

- **As relayed:** *"Tim has fielded several investor Qs about INTC's $20b equity offering ever since it was announced. He does think it **REMOVES AN OVERHANG** and he **remains BULLISH ON FOUNDRY**. Tim feels INTC's **$20B+ equity offering this week should provide enough of a 'BRIDGE' THROUGH C2027 AND C2028**."* And on positioning: *"Investors are increasingly **constructive on the foundry story**, and **upon successful 14A EXECUTION it could equate to INCREMENTAL DISCOVERY VALUE** in the names (interesting to see **INTC stock +7% since the $20b offering**)."* **INTC appears on UBS's semis LONG-BIASED positioning list — and also on its DEBATED line.** ⚠️ **That double-listing is an internal inconsistency in the note, flagged wiki-wide this run; take the prose.**
- ➜ **The one genuinely incremental thing here is the SEQUENCING it implies: the raise removed the balance-sheet question, so the equity now trades on 14A execution rather than on funding. That makes 14A the single scoreable variable — the same conclusion the page's 08-12 block reaches from the primary note, now with the market having voted +7% on it.**
- **The demand backdrop moved the right way in the same week's data:** **CQ2'26 total SERVER CPU shipments +~9% q/q (+27% y/y) versus a normal seasonal of +1%; total PC CPU shipments +10% q/q versus a 5-yr median of −2%, "a sharp rebound after declining 13% q/q in 1Q"; and "INTC's REVENUE SHARE IMPROVES Q/Q"** (UBS · Arcuri, CPU Industry Update, via Ruple 2026-08-15). ⚠️ **The same update keeps the structural offset intact and this page should not lose it: "AMD continues to gain share in Client DIRECTLY AT INTEL'S EXPENSE." A q/q revenue-share improvement inside a continuing unit-share loss is not a reversal.**
- ⚠️🔴 **AND THE UNIT BACKDROP IS DETERIORATING, WHICH IS THE HALF THAT CUTS AGAINST THE ABOVE: UBS forecasts 2026 global PC units −~11% y/y (desktop −13% / notebook −10%) to ~242MM, with 2027 recovering only +2% y/y** — because *"**MEMORY PRICE INFLATION and mix allocation towards higher-end products will likely constrain demand for LOW-TO-MID TIER PCs**, while pass-through of some costs will also pressure unit volume,"* and 2027 stays capped by *"continued elevated memory pricing, potential DESPECING, and a more normalized replacement cycle following the transition to Windows 11."* ➜ **Memory cost is now the binding constraint on Intel's largest unit market, and it is entirely outside Intel's control. Note the direct read-through to the memory pages: the same tightness that is re-rating [[MU]]/[[SNDK]] is what is shrinking Intel's client TAM.**
- ⚠️ **AN INTERNAL INCONSISTENCY BETWEEN TWO UBS DESKS IN THE SAME 48 HOURS, LOGGED NOT RESOLVED: Arcuri's global model has 2026 NOTEBOOK units −10% y/y, while UBS's Taiwan team (Randy Abrams, 08-14/15) cut its Taiwan-ODM notebook forecast to −13% y/y for 2026 versus "our global PC model −11% y/y." Different bases (global units vs Taiwan ODM shipments) and Abrams flags the gap himself — but the two houses' notebook numbers are 3pts apart in notes published a day apart. Both retained as published.**

**🆕🔴 2026-08-12 — UBS PUTS THE RAISE IN A CAPEX/FCF FRAME AND ANSWERS THE QUESTION DIRECTLY: "DOES THE $20B+ EQUITY RAISE REMOVE AN OVERHANG? WE THINK SO." AND IT NAMES THE FOUNDRY CUSTOMERS IT EXPECTS TO FOLLOW.** (UBS / Timothy Arcuri, **Neutral, PT $112 ← $121**, 2026-08-12.)

- **🔴 THE FUNDING BRIDGE, and it is the first place on this page the raise is sized against the actual capex path:** *"We feel INTC's $20B+ equity offering this week should provide **enough of a 'bridge' through C2027 and C2028**. When combined with **pre-payments and financial commitments that we expect to accompany several forthcoming foundry deals ([[GOOG]] for EMIB-T, [[AAPL]] for M-Series, [[AMD]], [[SPCX]], and potentially a few more)**, we believe the raise will allow INTC to fund its foundry buildout. Overall we view the raise as **a strong endorsement of INTC's confidence in its foundry roadmap.**"* ⚠️ **The named list — Google (EMIB-T), Apple (M-Series), AMD, SpaceX — is UBS's expectation, not announced business. Log it as an analyst forecast of the customer pipeline, not as wins.**
- **🔴 THE CAPEX LADDER, which is the number this page did not have: gross capex ~$20B in FY26 ➜ ~$28–30B in FY27 (management said FY27 capex will increase "significantly") ➜ "another step-up into the $40B range for C2028 and C2029."** ➜ **On UBS's own numbers the raise (~$19.7bn net) covers roughly one incremental year of the step-up. That is what "bridge through C2027 and C2028" means, and it implies the funding question returns for C2029 unless the foundry pre-payments arrive.**
- **🔴 FCF — and management is hedging:** *"INTC sounds **fairly noncommittal to being FCF positive in C2027**"*; UBS models **~$1B FCF burn in C27** (on $26B net / $28.5B gross capex), **~$4B burn in C28**, **turning positive to ~+$8B in C29** and growing thereafter. ⚠️ **Note the sequencing: FCF turns positive in the same year the capex ladder tops out. The entire equity story is a bet on that crossover happening on schedule.**
- **Dilution is modest on UBS's math:** assuming the deal closes in the Sept quarter, **Sept-Q EPS $0.39 ➜ $0.37**; **Dec-Q ~unchanged at $0.45**; **C26 EPS $1.56 ➜ $1.55**; **C27 $2.17 ➜ $2.19**; **C28 $2.82 ➜ $2.94** (i.e. accretive in the out-years as the funded capacity earns). **Deal mechanics as priced: ~211MM shares at $95, plus a 30-day underwriter option on ~32MM more; net proceeds ~$19.7bn.**
- **🔴 UBS'S SPLIT VERDICT IS THE USEFUL PART, AND IT IS THE SAME SPLIT THIS PAGE CARRIES: bullish foundry, unconvinced on products.** *"We have long been bullish on the company's process metrics and **yield curve progress for 14A (which, unlike 18A, is really just a process node shrink)**, and we believe it has **a much wider process window which should make it much more attractive to external customers**. This bullish foundry view is offset, however, by **our relative lack of confidence in INTC's current product roadmap.**"* PT cut **$121 ➜ $112** — and note **the cut is entirely a PRODUCTS multiple compression, not a foundry markdown**: SOTP values Products at **26% normalised EBIT margin on 28x EV/EBIT (vs 31x prior)**, Foundry on **EV/k-wpm at $10/share (vs $11 prior), applying a ~45% discount to [[TSM]]** for technology, yield, utilisation and customer-adoption risk.
- **⚠️ THE SHARE DATA CUTS AGAINST THE PRODUCT ROADMAP, AND IT IS THE SAME ANALYST SAYING IT (UBS / Arcuri, "CPU Industry Update — CQ2'26", 2026-08-12, Mercury Research data):**
  - **Server CPU units +~9% q/q (+27% y/y) vs normal seasonal +1%.** ⚠️ **INTC ceded 140bps of unit share q/q to 53.9%**; [[AMD]] **+90bps to 28.4%**; [[ARM]] **+40bps to 17.6%**. **Y/Y the trend is brutal: INTC 64.0% ➜ 53.9%, AMD 24.0% ➜ 28.4%, ARM 12.0% ➜ 17.6%.**
  - **✅ The offset, and it is real: on x86 REVENUE share INTC gained 160bps q/q to 55.4% (−340bps y/y), as Mercury data suggests INTC ASPs were +22% q/q vs AMD +7%.** ➜ **Intel is losing units and winning price. In a memory-inflated, allocation-driven market that is a defensible near-term outcome; it is not a roadmap fix.**
  - **PC: total CPU units +10% q/q vs a 5-yr median of −2%** (desktop −7%, notebook **+16%** vs −2% seasonal). **INTC lost 110bps of total PC unit share to 59.1%** (AMD +30bps to 25.6%, ARM +90bps to 15.3%) **while gaining 210bps of notebook revenue share to 73.2% and 240bps of desktop to 64.9%**; **INTC PC ASPs +9% q/q vs AMD −6%.** Same pattern: **units down, price up.**
  - **⚠️ The competitive set gets more crowded exactly when 14A needs to land: ARM's AGI platform launches with 136 cores in CY27; [[QCOM]] targets a 250+ core CPU in CY28**; UBS expects **Intel's server roadmap to improve with Coral Rapids and the reintroduction of multithreading (absent from Diamond Rapids)** — *"That said, the market is likely to be materially more crowded by then."*
  - **UBS's 2026 PC unit view is a memory-cost call: "memory price inflation and mix allocation towards higher-end products will likely constrain demand for low-to-mid tier PCs, while pass-through of some costs will also pressure unit volume."** Cross-ref [[MU]], [[SAMSUNG]], [[SKHYNIX]].
- **Desk colour on the day: the INTC equity offering was the lead item in UBS's index/flow note** (UBS / Jason Irukulapati, "The Daily Catch — INTC Equity Offering / ICESEMI Consultation", 2026-08-12), and BofA's one-liners flagged **"INTEL foundry ambitions"** alongside the AI-earnings tape (BofA / Nicholas Villani and Claudio Franco desk notes, 2026-08-12 — sales commentary, not Research).

**🆕🔴 2026-08-11 — THE RAISE WAS UPSIZED TO ~$20BN AGAINST $100BN+ OF DEMAND, PRICED AT $95. THESIS DRIFT: the deal logged yesterday as "approximately $15bn" is now ~$20bn with the overallotment, and it was 5x+ oversubscribed.**
- **Terms as executed:** **$15bn base upsized to ~$20bn** (if the overallotment is exercised), **priced at $95/share — a −6.5% discount to the prior Friday's close — with Bloomberg reporting $100bn+ of demand.** *"The first capital raise since 1971."* The stock was **down on the initial announcement and flat after hours on the incremental news** (Jefferies / Conor O'Mara, 2026-08-10 PM; Jefferies / Rankie Wong, 2026-08-11). Intel also appeared in **Russell/S&P index share-count changes** on the increased shares outstanding (2026-08-11).
- **Management's stated rationale, verbatim:** *"**Customers continue to signal a strong and sustainable demand environment, driven by unprecedented investment in AI compute.** Progress in emerging areas including **physical AI, purpose-built silicon, advanced packaging and external wafers** represent significant growth opportunities for Intel… Intel intends to use the net proceeds for general corporate purposes, which may include… **capital expenditures and working capital**… to pursue the growth opportunities ahead **while maintaining a strong balance sheet and its commitment to an investment-grade rating**."*
- **🔴 The read-through the market actually paid for was in the supply chain, not in Intel.** On 08-11 **[[ASML]] +4% / [[KLAC]] +4% / [[LRCX]] +3% / [[AMAT]] +1%**, with TMTB's reasoning: *"Semi cap generally stronger on the back of **INTC's raise, which ended up at an upsized $20B today at $95 vs initial $15B**: … **ASML has most direct leverage since Intel is the anchor customer for High-NA EUV on 14A**, and litho is the biggest, longest-lead-time line item, ie it gets ordered first when a fab tools out. **INTC also a big customer of KLAC**"* (TMTB EOD Wrap, 2026-08-11). ⚠️ **Sequencing matters: on 08-10, the day of the announcement, "none of the US semicap equipment names reacted positively with LAM KLA and AMAT all down 2-3%"** (Jefferies / Conor O'Mara). **The complex re-rated only once the size and the demand number were known — which is the market saying the raise is credible capex, not a rescue.**
- **🔴 AND THE STRUCTURAL PRIZE GOT MORE EXCLUSIVE ON THE SAME DAY: [[SAMSUNG]] pushed High-NA EUV out to ~1nm (≈2030), abandoning the earlier 2nm/1.4nm plan, on "technical hurdles and cost pressures"; [[TSM]] has bought High-NA for R&D only and says its 2029 A12/A13 nodes won't need it. TrendForce: "there remains only ONE OUTLIER in early High-NA EUV adoption"** (TrendForce, via JPM / Joshua Meyers, 2026-08-11). ➜ **Intel is that outlier. The bull reading is that 14A/High-NA is a genuine process differentiator no one else will have this decade; the bear reading is that Intel is funding a $20bn bet on tooling its two largest competitors have both explicitly decided they can skip. This page should hold both — it is the single most consequential open question on the foundry thesis.**
- **Demand-side corroboration for the capex: Bernstein raised logic/foundry WFE to +20% (2027) and +19% (2028) "as TSMC, INTC and Samsung invest in leading edge nodes"** (Rasgon / Dai, 2026-08-11), inside a WFE raise to **$148bn/$204bn/$259bn for 2026/27/28**. **JPM/Harlan Sur separately names INTC among the best-positioned in compute on June WSTS data, with the industry forecast raised to $1.68T CY26 / $2.25T CY27** (2026-08-11).

**🆕 2026-08-10 — Intel launches a ~$15bn follow-on equity offering.** US-registered follow-on of **approximately $15bn**, bookrunners **J.P. Morgan, Goldman Sachs, Morgan Stanley, Citi**; management roadshow team **Lip-Bu Tan (CEO), David Zinsner (CFO), John Pitzer (Head of IR)** (JPM ECM / Joshua Meyers, 2026-08-10; GS / Isadora Turcheti, 2026-08-10 — both marked approved for external distribution). Company framing: *"intended to further enable Intel to pursue the growth opportunities ahead while maintaining a strong balance sheet and its commitment to an investment-grade rating."*

**The sell-side read (Vital Knowledge / Adam Crisafulli, 2026-08-10):** *"Many people thought Intel would look to raise additional equity given the stock's huge rally over the last several quarters and the fact it needed additional cash to execute on its turnaround/growth plans. At the current market cap, a ~$15B stock sale would only be **~3% dilutive** (whereas a year ago it would have been nearly **50% dilutive**). Since most investors are sanguine on Intel's fundamental outlook, this stock sale should be absorbed fairly easily by the market."* The caveat he attaches is a sector point, not an Intel point: **"the $15B Intel raise is the latest example of how the AI industry, at least at the present time, is demanding capital far above what companies are generating via operating cash flow, requiring markets (via debt and equity) to plug the gap, a dynamic that creates risks for the sustainability of the boom."**

**🆕🔴 2026-08-10 (PM) — THE ROADSHOW CALL WITH CFO ZINSNER IS THE RICHEST INTEL SOURCE SINCE THE Q2 PRINT, AND IT RE-FRAMES THE RAISE FROM OPPORTUNISTIC TO MECHANICAL.** Takeaways from the broadly-distributed syndicate investor call held after the offering launched; active joint bookrunners JPM / GS / MS / Citi; stock trading **−3%** on the day (TMT Breakout, 2026-08-10).
- **Why now — management's own framing:** the raise is *"the moment both of **Lip-Bu Tan's preconditions for a capex step-up — process proof and demand conviction — have been met**,"* and the urgency is **mechanical rather than opportunistic**: Intel *"won't place purchase orders without identified funding,"* plans to **issue POs across multiple equipment vendors within the next few weeks**, and faces spending (tools, shell acceleration, **billions in substrate prepayments**) landing **within ~18 months** while offsets like the **investment tax credit arrive mostly later**. Equity-only **to protect the investment-grade rating** — which Zinsner noted *"matters to foundry customers, not just rating agencies"* — while preserving the **$20B+ cash practice**. Read-through to the semicap names: **new tool POs from Intel inside weeks** ([AMAT.md](AMAT.md), [LRCX.md](LRCX.md), [KLAC.md](KLAC.md), [ASML.md](ASML.md), [TOKYOELEC.md](TOKYOELEC.md)).
- **🔴 Process — 14A drew the most confident language of the session, with a NAMED, DATED CATALYST.** 18A is *"meeting or beating its **~7% monthly defect-density improvement target**,"* with **Panther Lake volume pipe-cleaning the node**. 14A characterised as *"a **straightforward shrink** with the hardest engineering (gate-all-around, backside power) already done,"* and its **defect-density trajectory "the best Intel has seen since 22nm."** ⚠ **The bogey: management expects to confirm the 0.9 PDK on the LATE-OCTOBER EARNINGS CALL**, ahead of **risk production in 2027** and **volume in 2028**. That is a falsifiable, dated test of the whole foundry thesis — logged in Catalysts.
- **Demand — 10+ CPU LTAs, and the CPU:GPU ratio argument gets a second airing.** *"**10+ CPU LTAs** (three-to-five-year terms, some with **share and pricing components**, with pricing deliberately left partly unlocked to capture momentum),"* plus the structural claim that *"the **CPU-to-GPU ratio is near parity** and could tilt further in CPUs' favor."* **IR head John Pitzer sized edge/physical AI — just ~10% of client CPU revenue today — as potentially rivaling the entire traditional PC market.** ⚠ Note this is the *opposite* side of the argument BofA's Vivek Arya called *"pure BS"* on the [NVDA.md](NVDA.md) page (agentic CPU racks are a separate room; unit ratios are meaningless at $2-4k CPU vs 10-20x accelerator ASPs). **Both framings now live on the wiki; they are irreconcilable and that is the debate.**
- **Foundry — the conversation has changed shape.** *"Customer conversations have moved from **monthly yield reviews to weekly commercial negotiations over terms and capacity corridors**"* — and **the raise itself doubles as a signal to those customers, who management suggested could also contribute capital in some form.** **[[SPCX]]'s interest in 14A was cast as an endorsement of node maturity** (cross-ref [SPCX.md](SPCX.md)). ⚠ **Foundry break-even remains exit-2027 but "could slip into 2028," which management PRE-FRAMED as a positive, demand-driven signal rather than an execution miss** — file that framing as management's, not as fact.
- **Model rules of thumb given on the call:** **$5 of front-end capex per $1 of back-end**; **billions in substrate prepayments hitting working capital**; **ASICs at a $2B run-rate heading to $4B in a $100B+ market**; **Mobileye (~78%) and Altera (~48%) held as future monetization levers rather than near-term sales.**
- **The closing pitch:** *"the **old Intel was a Rule of 30 company; the new Intel targets a Rule of 45** on a **~15x larger TAM**,"* with management stressing the story *"has multiple ways to work beyond near-term foundry alone."*

**🆕 2026-08-10 — the demand-side counterweight the raise does not address: PCs (Barclays / Tom O'Malley, 2026-08-10).** Barclays' PC Pulse **cut CY26 to −14% (from −10%)** and **CY27 to −3% (from −2%)**, modelling **Q3 and Q4 both −11% q/q vs +5% / −3% historical seasonality**. *"July came in well below our estimates following a large beat in June which cited **pull-ins**… **a couple more ODMs are now pointing to DD% declines for the year**,"* with **component tightness into 2H "the one consistent message."** ⚠ This is the clearest read that **memory/component cost inflation is now showing up as PC UNIT destruction**, not just margin pressure — a direct headwind to CCG that the LTA/foundry story does not offset. Cross-ref [themes/macro-cycle.md](themes/macro-cycle.md), [AMD.md](AMD.md), [MCHP.md](MCHP.md).

**Roadmap datapoint:** **Diamond Rapids (Oak Stream) may kick off in 2H27**, versus AMD's Venice (Zen 6) ramping from 3Q26 into mass volume in 2027 — both moving to **PCIe 6.0**, which raises PCB content ~50% (M6 → M7/8, 22L) (Jefferies / Conor O'Mara, 2026-08-09). Also carried on BofA's TMT weekly idea-generation list this week alongside CSCO/NVDA/ORCL (BofA / Brian Fenske, 2026-08-09).

**Q2 FY26 print — big beat & raise, stock +8-9% (reported 2026-07-23 AMC):** Revenue **$16.1B** (vs Street ~$14.4B / GS $14.3B — a ~$1.7B beat), **non-GAAP GM 41.8%** (vs Street ~39.2%, GS 39.3%), **OPM 17.2%** (vs Street ~11.2%), **non-GAAP EPS $0.42** (vs Street ~$0.22, GS $0.23) — one of the cleanest INTC prints in years, execution finally catching up to the narrative. Segments: **CCG $8.9B** (vs Street ~$8.0B, +11% beat), **DCAI $6.3B** (vs Street ~$5.6B, +13% beat), **Intel Foundry $5.8B** (+5.3% beat) — but **external foundry revenue still only ~$300M**. **Q3 FY26 guide well above: revenue $15.8-16.8B** (mid $16.3B vs Street ~$15.06B), **GM ~42%** (vs Street ~40.2%), **EPS $0.38** (vs Street ~$0.27). Capex: **2026 raised to $18-20B** (a second consecutive guide-up), with a "significant increase" flagged for 2027. **CFO David Zinsner (press interview): Intel has signed several multi-year CPU and XPU supply agreements** — an incremental positive on demand visibility. Roadmap tells: **18A-P entered risk production (as guided); Panther Lake in high-volume on High-NA; Foundry now committed to 14A HVM in 2028 (pulled in from ~2029)**; "Purpose Built Silicon revenue nearly tripled YoY" (possible ASIC re-labeling). Management (Zinsner/Tan): "AI is driving unprecedented demand for compute… well-positioned to capture sustainable growth across our CPU franchise, ASICs, advanced packaging and vast wafer foundry network… meaningfully increasing our investments in equipment, clean room space, and substrates." Sell-side first-takes all constructive: **GS (Schneider): "strong quarter across the board," expect stock higher** (Neutral $150, likely under review); **JPM (Meyers): "strong beat & guide… aggressive commentary on capex that bodes well for WFE"**; **UBS (Ruple): "one of the better prints from INTC in quite some time… execution now starting to catch up with the narrative… still has incremental discovery value."** Read-through on the print: **AMD +3.1%, KLAC +2.8%, ASML +2.4%** (WFE-positive) (GS · James Schneider "First Take"; JPM · Joshua Meyers "First Blush"; UBS · Robert Ruple "Earnings Reads", all 2026-07-23). Watch on the call: advanced-packaging outlook, external-foundry progress, server-CPU demand/share, GM progression.

**Post-call detail + the day-after sell-side (2026-07-24) — the beat is not in dispute; the capex/FCF funding question is the new fault line.** Refined segment figures from the full notes: **DCAI $6.26B (+24% q/q, +59% y/y — best growth in ~15 years)** at **~39.5-40% op margin**, with **DCAI op income $2.5B surpassing Client for the first time** — DCAI is now Intel's largest profit pool (JPM · Harlan Sur, 2026-07-24). **CCG $8.88B (+15% q/q)** but the upside was **ASP/mix, not units** — Intel again **under-shipped PC consumption** (last call it flagged >$1B/qtr of under-shipment and now says it will **still not have caught up exiting this year**); **AI PC is now two-thirds of client mix**, **18A is at full scale supporting 400+ Core Series 3 designs**, and CCG op margin fell 620bps q/q to 26.4% on stranded-inventory charges as output pivoted to server (JPM · Sur; UBS · Timothy Arcuri, both 2026-07-24). **External foundry revenue $293M (from $174M)** — up $93M q/q, mostly early advanced-packaging, against a **~$2.1B/qtr foundry op loss** (JPM · Sur, 2026-07-24). **Custom ASIC now ~$2B run-rate (~3x y/y, ~8% of DCAI, vs ~$1B run-rate in 4Q25), guided to $4B+ "in the not-too-distant future," framed against a $100B+ TAM** (UBS · Arcuri; JPM · Sur, 2026-07-24) — this is what the PR called "Purpose Built Silicon nearly tripled YoY."
- **Capex is the headline revision: FY26 >$20B (from $18B prior), tooling +40% y/y; CY27 "significantly above" that** — formal 2027 number comes in January. Street modelling of CY27: **MS $30bn**, **JPM $26bn ("may still prove light")**, **UBS ~$30bn** (MS · Joseph Moore; JPM · Sur; UBS · Arcuri, all 2026-07-24). Mgmt framed the step-up as **demand-backed** — supported by LTAs and **customer pre-pays** — and said it is "aggressively locking in tool purchase orders," accelerating clean-room build, and securing substrate + memory supply. Note the space/shell constraint is gone: prior-years' investment means **the bulk of incremental capex is tooling, not greenfield** (MS · van Putten/Simpson, 2026-07-24).
- **…and the funding question is now explicit.** With capex ~$8B above depreciation, MS sees Intel **modestly FCF-negative this year and next**, and management **conceded on the call that positive 2027 FCF is "more challenged"** and that it **"may need to tap the capital markets"** (MS · Moore; JPM · Sur, 2026-07-24). Liquidity is ample today — **~$30B cash + $10B revolver + ~$10B of monetizable non-core assets** — but JPM flags that **share count is already ~5.1B vs ~4.4B a year ago**, so an equity raise would be dilution landing exactly as EPS momentum builds. Brookfield proceeds also **start being paid back next year** (MS · Moore, 2026-07-24).
- **14A is now fully committed — and at least one analyst reads a customer behind it.** 14A committed to **HVM in 2028** with **risk production 2H27** and the **0.9 PDK in October**, after which customer decisions are possible "relatively quickly" (JPM · Sur, 2026-07-24). UBS's read is the sharper one: committing to 14A HVM "implies it must finally have some sort of firm commitment from a customer interest group including (we think) **AAPL or Google**"; UBS believes the main 14A interest is **Apple (compute processor), a gaming console, and perhaps Google (though mostly EMIB-related)** (UBS · Arcuri, 2026-07-24). Management would not confirm whether it has customer commitments or prepayments funding the capex. Corroborating tape: **"14A CUSTOMER CONFIRMED… he said he wouldn't ramp capex unless customer confirmed"** (@bubbleboi, 2026-07-23) — a retail read of the same inference, not a company confirmation.
- **New EMIB-T customer datapoint: UBS's checks suggest MediaTek may be among the EMIB-T customers, for its v9-gen TPU chip** (UBS · Arcuri, 2026-07-24) — a second independent corroboration of the [[MEDIATEK]]/Google-TPU-v9-on-EMIB-T thread already on the page, and Intel highlighted **growing EMIB-T backlog** as a differentiator, with **billions in EMIB-T revenue next year** per MS (MS · Moore, 2026-07-24).
- **Advanced-packaging leadership hire:** Intel hired **former SK Hynix chief Seok-Hee Lee to lead Intel Foundry advanced packaging** — BofA counts this alongside the 14A commit and the capex raise as one of three actions signalling coming external-foundry success (BofA · Vivek Arya, 2026-07-24). Note the adjacency to the unresolved SK Hynix–Ohio thread in Catalysts; on 07-23 TrendForce reported **both Intel and SK hynix dismissed the Ohio fab acquisition rumour "but the story may not end there — new collaboration paths are reportedly emerging beyond a potential sale"** (@trendforce, 2026-07-23).
- **Mgmt guidance colour on the shape of 2H:** the **GM floor is set ("comfortably in the 40s")**; from here the drags are dilutive Panther Lake/Granite Rapids mix (a "few quarters" issue) plus 2H input-cost inflation, against yield gains, pricing and the **40-60% y/y fall-through framework** (JPM · Sur, 2026-07-24). Q3 revenue is **flat-ish q/q on supply linearity, not demand** — capacity adds are **back-end loaded into Q4 and gated by substrates / T-glass / memory** — and supply "will not catch up" this year. Client is guided flattish in Q3 (channel CPU builds; edge ~10% of segment revenue drives the modest CCPG growth) with **PC softness hitting the P&L in Q4** and the **PC market down low-double-digit % in CY26 on memory inflation** (JPM · Sur; UBS · Arcuri, 2026-07-24).
- **CEO on the call (via @TheTranscript_, 2026-07-23/24):** "Our Q2 results represent our **strongest revenue growth in more than fifteen years**, enabled by greater speed, accountability, and customer focus"; and on demand — "Our outlook for **server CPU demand has improved again** since our last earnings report. We are forecasting **strong double-digit unit growth for the industry this year and next, with momentum extending into 2028**." Mgmt also flagged a **rising CPU-to-GPU ratio now approaching parity**, expected to skew further toward CPUs as inference/agentic workloads proliferate (UBS · Arcuri, 2026-07-24) — direct confirmation of the ratio thesis in Debate/Bull.

**Q1 FY26 (call 2026-04-23; quarter ended 2026-03-28):** Revenue **$13.6B**, +7% y/y, beating the guide midpoint by **$1.4B** — sixth straight beat. **Non-GAAP GM 41%** (~650bps above guide); **non-GAAP EPS $0.29**. But GAAP printed an operating loss of **$(3.1B)** and **EPS $(0.73)** on a **$4.07B restructuring charge** — the headline that auto-injected financials will show as near-breakeven once normalized (10-Q, 2026-04-24).
- **CCG $7.7B** (-6% q/q, above plan); FY26 PC unit TAM guided down low-double-digit %, supply-constrained, sell-in holding up better than sell-through on historically low channel inventory (StoneX, 2026-05-12).
- **DCAI $5.1B** (+7% q/q, +22% y/y); server prioritized over client given scarcity; server CPU guided to grow double-digits through year-end. Intel Products op income $4.06B (CCG $2.5B + DCAI $1.5B) (10-Q). Underneath the dollar growth, x86 server **share keeps bleeding to AMD**: BofA's 4Q25 tracker shows INTC server units +9% q/q (~5.0M) but unit share down ~1.5pts q/q and AMD ASP now at a **~70% premium to Intel** in server (BofA CPU deep-dive, 2026-02-23); UBS likewise has AMD continuing to take x86 server unit and revenue share into 2026 (UBS 2026 overview, 2025-12-22).
- **Intel Foundry $5.4B** (+20% q/q) but **op loss $(2.4B)**; external third-party foundry + A&T revenue just **$174M** (10-Q) — the external book is still tiny. Management targets quarterly loss reduction through FY26 toward exit-2027 break-even.
- **Contrarian share read — Intel *gaining* x86 server share, and less constrained than AMD:** an expert from one of the largest ODMs (a CPU buyer) put Intel's x86 server share at **50% (2024) → 55% (2025) → ~60% (2026)** — a surprise vs the consensus "bleeding to AMD" narrative. The mechanism: Intel moved **laptop/desktop designs to 18A**, which lifted 18A utilization/yields *and* freed existing-node capacity for Xeon servers — explaining the surprise margin beat last quarter (even selling low-spec chips); on the supply side Intel "seems **less capacity-constrained than AMD**" (own fabs in Ireland/Israel, no plant issue) (Redburn "Conor O'Mara Asia Trip Takeaways", 2026-06-23). Server CPU pricing: both Intel and AMD raised general server CPU pricing ~15-20% earlier this year and are expected to **raise another +15-20% in H2** (Intel constrained by in-house yield, AMD by TSMC N3 utilization) (Redburn "Conor O'Mara Asia Trip Takeaways", 2026-06-23). **Confirmation (2026-07-07):** Intel has reportedly **raised prices across a range of CPUs, with data-center processors seeing the largest increases — the flagship 128-core Xeon 6980P now US$13,955, up ~12%** (TrendForce, 2026-07-07) — a concrete data point on the H2 server-CPU hike, supportive of DCAI ASP/margin.
- **Server-CPU strength offsets the PC headwind — BofA flips to Buy (BofA "Core Wars Q2 Preview", Vivek Arya, 2026-07-13):** BofA now carries INTC at **Buy, PO $160** (31x ~$6.24 CY30E EPS power, disc. 2yr) — stronger PC/server-CPU pricing (industry server ASPs +~20% YoY) and AI-server-CPU demand more than offset PC units down 10-15%+ YoY in 2026; **Products operating margin up ~600bps in Q1 vs the 2025 average**; multiple 18A-P/14A external engagements; **18A server Diamond/Coral Rapids** the roadmap gate. BofA models **INTC server-CPU sales ~$40bn+ by CY30E (~25% of a $170bn server-CPU TAM)** with **value share sliding to ~24% by 2030 (from ~41% in 2025)** — dollar revenue still grows +20% CAGR CY26-30 on ASP expansion even as unit/value share erodes to AMD/ARM. Mizuho (Vijay Rakesh, 2026-07-11) stays **Neutral, PT $135** (~10x F27E P/S) — a technology-enabler of the >$1.4T-by-2028E AI-DC-capex ramp but "on the sidelines" on a weak foundry roadmap + AI-accelerator lag + wafer/memory-supply tightness capping 2026. [BofA source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Vivek_on_CPUs.html)
  - **⚠ UPDATED 2026-08-12 — same house, bigger TAM, SMALLER PO: BofA cuts INTC to PO $145 (from $160) while raising the CY30E server-CPU TAM to $210.6bn (from ~$170bn). Buy maintained. Old marks in the Changelog** (BofA · Vivek Arya, "Rise of the agents: raising CPU TAM (again) to $210bn", 2026-08-12; price $100.95). **The INTC line now reads: server-CPU revenue $14.1bn CY25 → $20.8bn CY26E → $27.8bn CY27E → $33.6bn CY28E → $46.3bn CY30E — a +22.2% CAGR CY26-30 — on units 18.5mn → 20.2mn → 29.3mn (only +9.7% CAGR) and ASP $761 → $1,028 → $1,579 (+11.3% CAGR).** ★ **Read the split before reading the share: HALF the growth is ASP, and BofA is explicit that Intel grows dollars while ceding share — "we see share declining to just ~22% of total server CPU value by CY30 from ~34% in CY26, though we expect INTC's continued relative strength in traditional enterprise workloads. However, we see continued $ revenue growth of +20% CAGR between CY26-30, particularly as INTC navigates through the industry-wide supply shortages, benefits from ASP expansion."** ⚠️ **The one forward-looking product sentence, and it is the Coral Rapids gate this page already tracks: "By CY28, 18A-P-based Coral Rapids should ramp and could begin to narrow the performance gap vs competition" — i.e. BofA's own model does NOT assume Intel closes the gap before CY28.** **On unit share Intel still ends the decade largest: ~36% unit share CY30 (vs AMD ~30%, ARM merchant ~22%, ARM custom ~12%) — the value/unit-share divergence IS the low-ASP problem the page's $800-vs-$1,400 bullet describes.** By workload, Intel's CY30E $46.3bn splits **traditional/IaaS $19.1bn (+12.4% CAGR), AI compute/head node $13.6bn, agentic AI node $13.6bn** — so **~59% of Intel's CY30 server-CPU dollars come from AI workloads that were ~28% of its book in CY25.** **Why BofA still likes it, verbatim: "We also like INTC given its foundry optionality, incremental Q3 supply ramping into Q4, and gen-on-gen ASP uplift."** PO basis and risks: **29x CY30E EPS power of $6+, discounted back two years, = 8.9x total-company 2028E EV/S; upside risks are external foundry packaging/wafer deals, better 18A/14A yields, a stronger PC market (Win10 refresh/AI uplift) and geopolitics favouring domestic manufacturing; downside risks are lower yield/ramp at Intel Foundry on 18A/14A, no material external wafer customer, a weak mature PC market, and accelerated share loss to CPU competitors.**
- **CXL 3.1 CPU roadmap slips (2026-07-07):** Samsung delayed mass production of its CXL 3.1 memory module (CMM-D 3.0) **following the postponed launches of CXL 3.1-supporting CPUs from Intel and AMD** (@jukan05, 2026-07-07) — i.e., Intel's CXL 3.1-capable server CPU has pushed out, a modest negative for the CXL-memory-fabric attach story (host-CPU-gated). Read-through to [SAMSUNG](SAMSUNG.md) / [cxl-memory-fabric](themes/cxl-memory-fabric.md).
- **18A yields tracking ~2-3 months ahead of internal benchmarks**; "fastest new-product ramps in 5 years" (Xeon 6, Core Series 3 / Panther Lake) (Q1 FY26 call). **Capex flat y/y but equipment spend +25% y/y**; shell capacity already built (Arizona Fab 62, Ireland), so adds are facilitization not greenfield (StoneX, 2026-05-12). Asia channel cross-read is more cautious: US fab investment "remains weak," not increasing next year, with construction sites hit by severe labor shortages (Daifuku via Jefferies Techknowledge, 2025-12-17).
- **Guidance Q2 FY26:** revenue **$13.8–14.8B** (+2-9% q/q). BBG consensus Q2-26E (auto-injected below): ~$14.4B rev, ~39% GM, EPS ~$0.21.
- **Q4 FY25 DCAI beat, in hindsight the first sign of the inflection:** DCAI segment revenue **$4.7B** (up from **$4.1B** in Q3'25), op margin **26.4%** (up from 23.4% Q3'25 and just 8.6% Q4'24) — Intel's own reported figures. SemiAnalysis frames this as "Intel saw an unexpected uptick in datacenter CPU demand in late 2025" and is **raising 2026 capex guidance on foundry tools and prioritizing wafers to server from PC** to alleviate the supply constraint — consistent with the wafer-reallocation mechanism Redburn's ODM-expert channel-check flagged independently for Q1 FY26 (SemiAnalysis/Wong & Patel "CPUs are Back," 2026-02-09, p.2-3).
- **Diamond Rapids (2026 flagship) architecture detail — new platform "Oak Stream":** 8ch and 16ch memory variants, PCIe Gen6, built on **Intel 18A**, up to 4 compute tiles/CPU, **192 cores max, 500W TDP**, 1S/2S/4S configs, native TF32/FP8 support in AMX. Architecturally it moves to **4 Core Building Block (CBB) dies flanking two I/O and Memory Hub (IMH) dies** — a multi-NUMA-node design similar to AMD's, abandoning the large single mesh after Granite Rapids' 10×19 mesh hit scaling limits. Each CBB packs 32 Dual Core Modules on Intel 18A-P hybrid-bonded onto an Intel 3-PT base die (2 cores/L2); the full die has 256 cores but **only up to 192 are enabled for mainline SKUs** — a yield tell (see Risks). Die-to-die interconnect drops EMIB in favor of long substrate traces from each CBB to both IMH dies (SemiAnalysis "CPUs are Back," 2026-02-09, p.31-33,39).
- **Rare Intel ISA lead — Advanced Performance Extensions (APX):** Diamond Rapids supports APX, which AMD's Zen7/Venice does not. AMD CTO Mark Papermaster has confirmed Zen7's new matrix-multiplication engine and AVX10/FRED/ChkTag will have debuted first on Intel Diamond Rapids — a rare case of Intel leading on an ISA feature, a counterweight to the otherwise bearish Diamond Rapids framing below (SemiAnalysis "CPUs are Back," 2026-02-09, p.64).
- **Intel now reporting gains from its OpenAI/Mistral investments** — "having languished for decades, [Intel] has recovered toward valuation levels last seen at the height of the dot-com boom" and "has reported making money from its investments in OpenAI and Mistral" (Barron's "Other Voices," 2026-06-08).
- **18A yield jumps to >85%, capacity expansion + a broad claimed design-win list (KeyBanc via @firstadopter, 2026-07-14):** KeyBanc says **18A yields have improved to over 85%, from 65% last quarter** — a large step-up on the single most-watched execution metric on the page (vs the "7-8%/month" / "6-7pp/month" prior reads); if accurate it materially de-risks the 18A ramp. KeyBanc sees Intel able to **expand INTC-3 capacity to support +25-30% server-CPU unit growth this year and >50% next year**, plans to "significantly expand 18A capacity" on improving yields + design wins, and — the most aggressive claim — believes that **beyond Apple, Intel has secured design wins with AMD, NVDA, MRVL, MSFT, MU, and OpenAI** (this is KeyBanc's belief, not an Intel confirmation — treat as an analyst channel claim); also raising **client-CPU pricing +6-15% in 3Q26**, with the big foundry/EMIB-T growth still a 2029/30 story.
- **General-purpose server outlook raised (KeyBanc via @firstadopter, 2026-07-14):** KeyBanc lifted its **general-purpose server unit-growth outlook to +30% (from +18.5%)** as AMD/INTC squeeze out extra wafer supply and ARM demand rises for agentic-AI workloads; **both AMD and INTC now hope to grow server-CPU units >50% in 2027**, and NVDA has raised Vera CPU capacity 1.2-1.5M → 2.5-3.0M units. Supportive DCAI/server read into the print.
- **€5B Leixlip (Ireland) expansion — Fab 34 (Intel Foundry / FT / @QQ_Timmy, 2026-07-13):** Intel will invest **€5B (~$5.7B)** to upgrade its existing Leixlip Fab 34 for **Xeon 6 and next-gen Xeon on the Intel 3 process** — an equipment/facilitization upgrade (not a new building), started this year and done by end of next year, bringing total Fab 34 investment to **>$24B** and creating hundreds of permanent + thousands of construction jobs (Intel Foundry CTO/COO Naga Chandrasekaran); TrendForce frames Fab 34 as positioning Intel as **Europe's advanced-node leader**. Caveat (@IntelProMUltra, 2026-07-14): read this as **primarily captive/own-product capacity** (Granite Rapids, Clearwater Forest, prepping Fab 34 for Diamond Rapids) rather than external-foundry capacity — despite LBT's "incremental capex tied to external demand" line — a step toward reversing the server-share-loss narrative and reinforcing the IDM capacity-control advantage.
- **Nova Lake pulled mostly in-house on 18A yield confidence (@QQ_Timmy / TrendForce, 2026-07-15):** Intel now reportedly plans to build **80-90% of Nova Lake-S compute tiles on its own 18A** — up from an original plan to outsource **60-70% to TSMC** — as 18A yields have improved enough to avoid product delays (Fab 52 Arizona + Hillsboro Oregon 18A capacity ~30k wpm, sufficient for internal needs). Margin-accretive and a concrete "bring wafers home" execution tell that **cuts against the earlier MS bus-tour read that Nova Lake desktop would likely stay at TSMC** (MS/Joe Moore, 2026-06-16).
- **18A the US 2nm first-mover, corroborated (@lithos_graphein, 2026-07-13; @PatrickMoorhead, 2026-07-15):** Samsung's Texas fab completed tape-out of Tesla's A15 — "the second 2nm chip made in the US, just behind Intel's 18A" — a read-through confirming 18A as the US 2nm leader; separately Patrick Moorhead notes Intel Foundry is **first with High-NA EUV for a volume part**, a "pipe cleaner" step that corroborates Bernstein's High-NA-first call (2026-07-06).
- **Government-overhang flare-up — INTC ~-6% on Trump "heavy hand" (@scottlincicome via @carlquintanilla, 2026-07-14):** "Intel Shares Tumble 6% as Trump's Heavy Hand in Chipmaker's Revival Alarms Investors" — a fresh, market-moving instance of the standing government-equity/DOC overhang (see Risks), with investors uneasy about the degree of federal involvement in the turnaround. A rare tape-negative amid an otherwise bullish CPU-preview week.
- **Apple foundry commitment — WSJ (2026-07-11):** [[AAPL]] reportedly secured an **exemption from semiconductor tariffs in exchange for committing to use Intel's fabs** — and will manufacture **not only Mac chips but also iPhone chips at Intel** (WSJ, 2026-07-11; @jukan05, 2026-07-11). Escalates the mid-June "Apple chip deal" (which drove INTC **+8-9% on 06-18**) and the "M7 on 18A-P" thread into a materially bigger external-foundry commitment spanning Apple's highest-volume product (iPhone) — potentially the single largest validation of the Intel Foundry thesis if it holds, though **node/wafer-volume/timing are unspecified** and prior Apple-Intel foundry reports drew skepticism (Fubon read Apple as leveraging TSMC on capacity, not a genuine switch — see Catalysts/AAPL page).
- **High-NA EUV validation + CPU-driven capacity — ASML roadshow (2026-07-17):** ASML frames Intel as **"a bit of a validation of where we stand with High-NA"** — Intel is the **first customer running High-NA EUV in production**, now using it on its most advanced node **18A** and shipping product with benefit; ASML expects other logic + DRAM customers (which took tool delivery soon after Intel) to reach the same conclusion. Separately, **current CPU demand has driven Intel's need for more capacity to fulfill its own demand**, which ASML says it is starting to notice in its order intake from Intel — a concrete demand/execution tell that dovetails with the in-sourcing (Nova Lake/18A) and server-CPU-shortage threads on this page (ASML mgmt "2Q26 London Roadshow", 2026-07-17). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_17_asml_2q26_london_roadshow_virtual_group_meeting.html)
- **Advanced packaging — TSMC "welcomes" others (positive for Intel), tempered by an IBP learning-curve nuance (Jefferies · Sherman Chang, 2026-07-17):** TSMC publicly welcomed other suppliers to step in and help alleviate advanced-packaging (CoWoS) capacity tightness; Jefferies interprets this as a **positive development for Intel and MediaTek in advanced packaging** (corroborating the 07-16 Fubon read that TSMC framed EMIB-T as complementary capacity). Nuance/negative: Taiwan supply-chain rumors of Intel's **slow learning-curve improvement in its advanced packaging (IBP)**; Jefferies flags Intel as **"very important in the supply chain"** (in the Google-TPU context) and says it will keep monitoring (Jefferies · Sherman Chang "Update with Fubon post-TSMC 2Q earnings", 2026-07-17). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_17_jefferies_update_with_fubon_post_tsmc_2q_earnings.html)

## Debate / thesis
**🗄️ ARCHIVE BACKFILL — DAMNANG (Substack), 2026-03-15 → 2026-05-04 (six pieces).** ⚠️ **INDEPENDENT KOREAN SEMIS SUBSTACK (damnang2.substack.com; also publishes as "The Optical Edge"), NOT A BROKER — no rating, no price target, no formal estimate.** Launched 2026-03-15, ~9k subscribers, paid. **These are 4-5 months old and are filed as historical context ONLY. Nothing here supersedes any current number, rating, guide or thesis on this page — in particular the Q1-2026 and Q2-2026 prints, the 18A/14A status and the capex guide have all moved since.** ⚠️ **The ingest router falsely detected "Morgan Stanley" as the broker on the 2026-03-18 EMIB piece; the byline on page 1 is DAMNANG. Corrected here.**
- **🗄️ WHY THE PACKAGING LEG IS THE ONE THAT MONETISES FIRST — and this page has been carrying the conclusion without the mechanism** (**DAMNANG (Substack)** — "EMIB: Intel Foundry's Best Hope", **2026-03-18**; ⚠️ independent author, NOT a broker). **The utilisation argument is the load-bearing one: *"Intel puts EMIB bridge wafer utilization at around 90%, versus roughly 60% for large interposers"* — because EMIB places small silicon bridges only where dies meet rather than a full interposer under the package.** **EMIB-T specs as printed: *"supports a 120mm x 180mm package with 38 or more bridges"*, with bump pitch *"tightened from 55μm in the first generation to 45μm and now 35μm, with 25μm in development"*, and glass-substrate support.** **Scale-up path, sourced to ETNews/TrendForce (2026-03-18): Intel scaling EMIB packages *"to 120×120mm (up from the 100×100mm standard used in Blackwell), targeting 12 HBM stacks per package"*, and by 2028 *"120×180mm packages with up to 24 HBM stacks."*** **Track record and adjacent assets: EMIB *"in production since 2017, starting with the Stratix 10 FPGA and running through server CPUs including Sapphire Rapids and Granite Rapids"*; Foveros in production since 2019 (Lakefield, then Meteor Lake) with Foveros Direct enabling copper-to-copper hybrid bonding; Clearwater Forest as the 3.5D proof point — *"18A process logic chiplets stacked on a base die via Foveros, with EMIB connecting those modules horizontally"*; and *"over 8 million PICs"* shipped in silicon photonics, with an Intel/Ayar Labs EMIB demo pairing an FPGA with a TeraPHY optical I/O chiplet.** **Amkor is named as the *"sole external assembly partner"* for EMIB (Icheon K5 operational; Portugal and Arizona expanding, Arizona 2028).** ➜ **His thesis — that EMIB is not a CoWoS replacement but a complementary second source, and that because Intel is a "systems foundry" (wafer + package + test) packaging can be the ON-RAMP that pulls wafer orders behind it, the way Samsung uses memory to pull foundry orders — is the clearest articulation on this page of WHY the EMIB-T datapoints matter beyond their own revenue.** ⚠️ **His own limiting condition is carried too: it only works if 18A/14A wafer processes are themselves competitive, and TSMC's Arizona packaging build-out (~2028-29) erodes Intel's current US-packaging edge.**
- **🗄️ THE STCO FRAME, WHICH IS THE MOST ORIGINAL ARCHIVE ITEM AND HAS NO EQUIVALENT ANYWHERE ELSE ON THIS WIKI** (**DAMNANG (Substack)** — "Intel's Real Moat That Nobody Talks About: STCO", **2026-05-04**; ⚠️ independent author, NOT a broker). His argument for the April re-rating — **"On April 24, Intel's stock surged 24% in a single day. It was the largest one-day gain since 1987 … For the month of April, shares rose 114%," touching $100 intraday** — is that the market began pricing **System Technology Co-Optimization**: co-optimising transistors, packaging, heterogeneous devices, chiplet standards and system architecture as one loop, which he frames as an IDM advantage a pure-play foundry structurally cannot run. **Technical marks as printed: PowerVia at *"cell utilization exceeding 90% … a 5% to 10% improvement over baseline"* with *"performance gain at iso-power … up to 4%"*, and full 18A at *"15% better performance per watt and 30% higher density compared to Intel 3"*; 14A's RibbonFET + PowerDirect combination *"could deliver 15% to 20% improvement in performance per watt"*; Foveros pitch going 55μm (Lakefield) → 36μm (Meteor Lake) → a 3μm Foveros Direct target, *"a 144x increase in connections per unit area"*; glass substrates targeted for *"mass production in the latter half of the decade"* toward *"one trillion transistors in a single package."*** **Ecosystem evidence: UCIe is Intel-led with *"AMD, ARM, Qualcomm, Samsung, TSMC, Microsoft, Meta, Google, and NVIDIA"* participating; Xeon 6 is the host CPU in [[NVDA]]'s DGX Rubin NVL8; Google signed a co-development deal for Xeon 6 and custom IPUs.** ⚠️ **He is explicit that this is a TIME GAP rather than a moat — RibbonFET/PowerVia's backside-power lead is *"closer to a time gap before TSMC catches up"* with A16 and Super Power Rail — and he stages the re-rating in three parts: product recovery (confirmed in Q1), packaging option value (partially priced), foundry re-rating via 14A external customers (still unproven). ⚠️ ALL Q1-2026 figures in that piece ($13.6B revenue, non-GAAP EPS $0.29 vs $0.01 consensus, DCAI $5.1B +22%, GAAP EPS −$0.73, 41.0% non-GAAP GM) are APRIL prints and have been superseded by the Q2'26 print at the top of this page — they are retained only to date the re-rating, not as current financials.**
- **🗄️ THE FOUNDRY STARTING POINT, AND IT IS WORTH KEEPING BECAUSE IT DATES THE BASE THE CURRENT NUMBERS GREW FROM** (**DAMNANG (Substack)** — "Intel Foundry: A Last Chance", **2026-03-15**; ⚠️ independent author, NOT a broker). **"In Q4 2025, Intel Foundry posted revenue of $4.5 billion alongside an operating loss of $2.5 billion,"** with CEO Lip-Bu Tan quoted admitting **"the company invested too much, too fast, without sufficient demand."** **Density gap as printed: *"TSMC N2 achieves 313 million transistors per square millimeter versus Intel 18A's 238 million"*; 18A PDK 1.0 released July 2024 with *"more than 100 tapeouts"* reported; 18A yield *"floated … in the low-to-mid 60% range"* by some sources (⚠️ never officially disclosed — treat as rumour).** **Government support: *"$7.86 billion in direct CHIPS Act grants plus a separate government equity stake of approximately 9.9%"* (restated in his 04-08 piece as *"$8.9 billion U.S. government common equity … backed by $5.7 billion in outstanding CHIPS funds and $3.2 billion from the Secure Enclave program … total government investment stands at $11.1 billion"*).** **14A framed as *"the first commercial application of High-NA EUV lithography"*, expected *"around 2027"*, with Intel *"will not invest without customer commitments"* — the same conditionality this page still carries.** ⚠️ **Also note a dated 18A-yield line he attributes to Intel's own CFO in the 03-17 piece: reaching *"profitable yield levels"* won't happen *"until late 2026."***
- **🗄️ TERAFAB, TWICE, AND HIS READ IS THE OPPOSITE OF THE HEADLINE BOTH TIMES** (**DAMNANG (Substack)** — "Terafab is Bullshit", **2026-03-17**, and "The Real Game Behind Terafab", **2026-04-08**; ⚠️ independent author, NOT a broker). The 03-17 piece is a Tesla-feasibility argument in which Intel is a minor character — the genuinely Intel-relevant lines are that 18A *"volume production started in October 2025"* against a CFO admission on profitable yields, that **Intel *"has already locked up the first ten units of High-NA EUV production for its 14A node"*** so a hypothetical new fab customer ordering today *"wouldn't [see tools] arrive until 2028 at the earliest"*, and that DigiTimes reported **Dojo 3 chip packaging may be handled at Intel's Arizona facility.** The 04-08 piece follows Intel's own X post — *"Intel is proud to join the Terafab project with [[SPCX]], xAI, and Tesla to help refactor silicon fab technology"* — and notes there was **"No press release. No SEC filing."** 🔴 **His conclusion is the useful one and it is a caution this page should hold: Intel's Terafab participation is unlikely to be near-term wafer revenue, because *"AI6 wafer production is already pointed at Samsung's Taylor facility, and AI5 is reportedly going to TSMC"* — what Intel gets is CREDIBILITY as a qualified external partner and a PACKAGING entry point that monetises faster than wafer share, while Tesla gets a negotiating lever against Samsung and Washington gets a policy narrative. *"Reading it as a simple 'Intel win' oversimplifies a multi-party capital and policy game."*** **Contemporaneous foundry marks from 04-08, useful as a dated baseline: *"Intel Foundry posted $17.8 billion in revenue for 2025 but recorded $10.3 billion in operating losses, and external customer revenue was just $307 million"* — 1.7% of total foundry revenue. And Intel *"repurchased a 49% stake in its Ireland Fab 34 from Apollo for $14.2 billion"* on April 1, regaining full control (Apollo had invested $11.2bn in 2024).**
- **🗄️🔴 THE APPLE AND NVIDIA RELAYS — CARRIED WITH A HEAVY CAVEAT, BECAUSE THEY ARE THIRD-HAND** (**DAMNANG (Substack)** — "The Age of the TSMC Bottleneck", **2026-04-06**; ⚠️ independent author, NOT a broker). ⚠️⚠️ **PROVENANCE FIRST: these are an independent Substack author RELAYING two named sell-side analysts in April. They are NOT primary reporting, NOT company disclosure, and NOT confirmed by Intel or Apple. The same caveat is applied on the [[AAPL]] page, where the Apple side of this file is filed.** **① Ming-Chi Kuo: *"Apple has signed an NDA with Intel, received the 18A P performance variant PDK, and is running internal simulations. Results are reportedly approaching expectations, and Apple is waiting on the formal PDK release before moving forward."* If that holds, *"Intel could be manufacturing lower-end M-series processors for MacBook Air and iPad beginning in 2027"*, at an annual volume tier of *"15 to 20 million units by analyst estimates."* **② Jeff Pu (GF Securities) goes further: *"There are indications that Apple is evaluating Intel's 14A process for non-Pro iPhone A-series chips starting in 2028"*, with Pro-model chips *"expected to remain at TSMC."*** ⚠️ **NOTE THE INTERNAL DISCREPANCY ACROSS THE ARCHIVE AND DO NOT RESOLVE IT: the 03-15 piece relays KeyBanc's John Vinh on Apple discussing *"entry-level iPhone A-series processors on Intel's 14A process around 2029"*, while this 04-06 piece relays Jeff Pu at **2028**. Two named analysts, three weeks apart, a year apart on timing. Both are logged; neither is preferred.** **③ NVIDIA Feynman: DigiTimes and others report the Feynman I/O die (2028) would run on Intel 14A or 18A and be packaged with EMIB, with *"roughly 25% of total Feynman packaging"* possibly going to Intel, while the GPU compute die stays on TSMC A16. ⚠️ Jensen Huang drew the line himself — *"This announcement is focused on custom CPU."* And Reuters (Dec 2025) reported NVIDIA had sampled 18A and stopped; a SemiWiki insider is quoted as saying *"NVIDIA and AMD are so tightly bound to TSMC on big chips, CPU and GPU class designs, that it may simply never happen at Intel."*** 🔴 **THE CONSTRAINT HE PUTS ON HIS OWN BULL CASE, WHICH IS THE MOST USEFUL LINE IN THE FILE FOR THIS PAGE: *"without significant 14A capacity investment, Intel cannot handle Apple iPhone volumes in 2028. The timing is tight."* He also relays CFO Zinsner directly — *"14A is tied to foundry customers. There's no point making a major capacity investment before customers are secured"* — with two potential customers running 14A test chips and Intel hoping for firm commitments in 2H-2026, and by SemiWiki counts *"four confirmed external customers on Intel 18A … HVM targets are 2028 to 2030"* against an expectation that *"IFS will be burning cash through at least 2027."*** **Capex scale, for the same reason: *"TSMC's annual capex for 2026 is estimated at $52 to $56 billion. Intel's is $16 billion. TSMC is building more than three Intels every year."* ⚠️ That $16bn is an APRIL figure and is superseded by the >$20bn FY26 guide at the top of this page — retained only to date the gap.**
- **Bull:**
  - **18A/14A is finally executing.** 18A yields ahead of internal plan; 14A "maturity, yield and performance outpacing 18A" at the equivalent point, with external customers engaged in the *definition* phase (vs only development for 18A) and management landing more internal tiles on 14A — a confidence tell (StoneX 2026-05-12; Q1 FY26 call). ASML 18A/14A commentary on customer wins / capacity-ramp needs is one of the pillars of the bullish semicap trade (BofA/Evans via briefing, 2026-06-15). Process-architecture edge: Intel is the **first-mover on backside-power (BSPDN)**, shipping at end-2025 vs TSMC late-2026/early-2027 and Samsung 2027 — though it chose the simpler **Power Via** implementation while TSMC/Samsung took Direct Backside Contact, which Bernstein argues should give TSMC a performance advantage despite later adoption (Bernstein Semis deck, May-2025).
    - **The SMIC “tighter than 18A” headline is not evidence against Intel's node.** SemiAnalysis/STEEL measured **32.5nm M0** on SMIC N+3 versus **36nm M0 in shipping Panther Lake**, but notes that Intel 18A supports **32nm M0** and Panther Lake deliberately uses looser high-performance cells. PowerVia removes front-side power routing, improving routing freedom; SemiAnalysis says overall 18A transistor density still clearly exceeds N+3 even with the relaxed M0. Read: the teardown does not change the 18A execution thesis; it clarifies that cell/library choice, full interconnect stack, PPA and yield matter more than a single local-metal pitch (SemiAnalysis / STEEL Team, 2026-06-14).
    - **…and here is the number the headline omitted, plus the roadmap distance (SemiAnalysis / STEEL Team, 2026-06-14).** The page has carried the qualitative rebuttal; the report actually quantifies it. On a normalised **Bohr-density basis SMIC N+3 is ~114 MTr/mm², 38% BELOW Intel 18A's HD library** — SemiAnalysis's own words on the title question are that the tighter pitch is a **"true but incomplete cherry picked metric."** The forward distance is the more useful part for the 18A/14A debate: a *theoretical* **SMIC N+4** (198nm cell height, 54nm CGP) only reaches **137.8 MTr/mm² ≈ TSMC N5 / Samsung SF4**, and SMIC would need a *theoretical* **N+5 with backside contacts** (170nm cell height, 53nm CGP) just to hit **163.6 MTr/mm², "on par with Intel 18A's HP library"** — and even then, "this would not make N+5 cost-competitive with the leading edge. It would reach a similar density through a much more expensive route," requiring new backside-alignment, wafer-thinning, contact-reveal and backside-metallisation flows. Three supporting details that read as quiet endorsements of Intel's process history: Intel's own **22nm/14nm/10nm FinFET cross-sections are used as the industry reference for fin-profile quality** (taller, narrower, more vertical fins across generations); **Intel reached a comparable CGP on Intel 10nm / Intel 7 without EUV**, and Intel used the **single-diffusion-grid / reduced-poly-end-to-end scaling booster on Intel 4** (TSMC did the equivalent p-to-n spacing reduction on N3) — i.e. the DTCO levers SMIC is now pulling are ones Intel already worked through; and, on the ceiling, **"even with EUV, 48nm has been the practical CGP limit for yield and process control."** Net for the page: an independent, technically-grounded rebuttal of the "SMIC has passed Intel" narrative from a source with no Intel axe to grind — worth two-plus generations of theoretical SMIC roadmap. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_14_ENC_Is_SMIC_N3s_Metal_Pitch_Smaller_than_Intel_18As_email.html)
    - **Update (2026-07-14/15) — the 18A execution signals keep improving.** KeyBanc puts **18A yields at >85% (from 65% last quarter)** and sees Intel "significantly expanding" 18A capacity on yields + design wins (KeyBanc via @firstadopter, 2026-07-14); and Intel is now reportedly pulling **80-90% of Nova Lake-S compute tiles in-house onto 18A** (from an original ~60-70%-to-TSMC plan), a direct confidence tell on internal-node reliability (@QQ_Timmy / TrendForce, 2026-07-15). Both cut against the bear read that Intel's most process-sensitive products still lean on TSMC.
  - **Packaging as the trojan horse.** EMIB/EMIB-T reticle ~30% larger than CoWoS; per-engagement value re-rated from "hundreds of millions" to **multiple billions/yr**; backlog growing, ramp visible 2027 (StoneX 2026-05-12). Redburn (bus tour, briefing 2026-06-17): Intel "put on a brave face" but advanced packaging is the real play — "using packaging as a trojan horse into customer wins." Asia checks sharpen the call to **EMIB-T, not plain EMIB**: **Google is evaluating EMIB-T for TPU v9 and Meta for next-gen MTIA**, citing CoWoS capacity constraints and EMIB-T's power-delivery advantages in the HBM4 era; Intel CVP John Pitzer guided EMIB-T to a **"meaningful contribution in 2H26"** with the narrative shifting from CoWoS overflow to technology-driven adoption (Jefferies Techknowledge, 2025-12-17). Optical optionality sits on top: Intel's CPO roadmap pairs an **OCI chiplet (4 Tbit/s bi-directional, 64 lanes @ 32 Gbit/s, ~5 pJ/bit) co-packaged with a Xeon CPU**, a 2025 detachable glass optical-bridge connector, and a 2027 target for 3D-integrated (vertical) photonics (Semianalysis CPO primer, 2026-02).
    - **Update — EMIB-T now load-bearing for an external customer's roadmap, not just an Intel bet.** Morgan Stanley sales (Amir Amerian, "glass/panels" note) telegraphs performance/cost advantages of EMIB-T over TSMC's CoWoS specifically for chips **above 9.7 reticle-size**, driven by (1) higher sample yields and (2) a warpage/cost edge at large chip sizes (Morgan Stanley · Amir Amerian, 2026-06-30).
    - **ECTC 2026 — the technical case for EMIB-T, from Intel's own disclosures.** Intel was the **largest corporate presenter at ECTC** (12 papers vs TSMC's 3, Samsung's 11), and SemiAnalysis calls EMIB-T (next-gen EMIB **with TSVs**) the **"most credible alternative to TSMC's CoWoS platform for large-package AI accelerators,"** with the disclosures explaining **why it is expected to be used in Google's TPU v9**. Specifics: validated at **36/35 µm bump pitch on a 2× reticle package** (down from 45 µm on Granite Rapids = 65% higher bump density), with 36/35 µm now expanding to **4.5× reticle silicon (cert targeted end-2026)** and a **25 µm pitch** test vehicle underway; a **240×240 mm quarter-panel** test vehicle (~67 reticles) shown (though the booth sample had "severe warpage"). The "T" is for the **TSVs** that deliver power vertically through the bridge — Intel claims **DC voltage drop cut 68-80%**; on-bridge MIM caps (500 nF/mm², ~Intel-18A-class) improve PDN AC impedance **>82%**; simulated **HBM4E at 12 Gb/s** held **>60% UI eye width** to 16 Gb/s. The forward roadmap adds active bridges, embedded voltage regulators, and >2500 nF/mm² eMIM-T caps. SemiAnalysis's caveat (see Bear): EMIB-T is **"still behind TSMC's CoWoS on several vectors"** (TSMC already deploys DTC/eDTC, is further along on IVR and active LSI) — "Intel is still catching up to an ecosystem that has been executing in volume for years." Separately, Intel showed an **industry-first 510×515 mm, 24-layer glass-core panel** (copper-filled TGVs, 2 embedded EMIB bridges, co-formed optical waveguides, no SeWaRe cracking after thermal shock) — a leading data point in the glass-substrate race vs TSMC (SemiAnalysis "EMIB-T Roadmap / ECTC 2026", 2026-07-02). The sharpest confirmation: JPMorgan's MediaTek note ties **upside in MediaTek's TPU v9 ("Humufish") unit** partly to the **progress of Intel's EMIB-T packaging yield** — i.e. EMIB-T yield is now a modeled input to a major external customer's accelerator roadmap, not just an internal Intel packaging bet (JPMorgan · Gokul Hariharan, MediaTek note, 2026-07-01). UBS corroborates the traction but flags a capacity/prioritization catch: EMIB-T is **resource/capacity constrained in 2028**, and Intel will likely prioritize internal products plus the Google/MediaTek TPU v9 engagements first — i.e. real demand, but a rationing problem, not a green light for everyone (UBS · Sunny Lin, 2026-07-01).
    - **Bear-side nuance on the same engagement — yield is the gate (Fubon, 2026-07-08).** Fubon's latest checks confirm **Google initially planned to use Intel EMIB-T for its TPU V9 production in late-2027/2028** — a positive advanced-packaging read-through that Intel Foundry's packaging trojan-horse is landing on a marquee external roadmap. But Google **asked Intel to lift EMIB-T yield to 95%+ before production**, and Fubon sees some yield improvement yet judges hitting that target by mid-2027 "very challenging," concluding Google will "at last rely on TSMC's [2028 CoWoS] expansion plan." Net: the design intent validates EMIB-T's competitiveness, but the yield bar (echoing Fubon's earlier 60-70% EMIB-vs-95%+-CoWoS framing, 2026-06-05) keeps the 2028 volume outcome contested (Fubon "TSMC — Expecting GM upside going forward", 2026-07-08).
    - **🆕 ⚠️ SUPERSEDED CONCLUSION — Fubon reverses itself three weeks later: "Intel's supply is a must by 2028" (Fubon Research · Sherman Shang / Daniel Yang, "Our advanced packaging, CoWoS forecast adjustments; other updates", 2026-07-28).** The bullet above is Fubon's 07-08 view and is **retained for the record**; its *conclusion* is now the superseded one. Verbatim: ***"Based on our checks, Google plans to have 12-15mn TPUs in 2028. Entering 2028, Google's TPUs will enter the V9 generation with four compute dies, suggesting that their capacity consumption will more than double in 2028 vs. 2027. Although we do not have the detailed allocation yet, we think it is difficult to reach Google's target with TSMC alone, and Intel's supply is a must by 2028."*** **The reversal is on capacity arithmetic, not on yield — which is why it is durable: Fubon has not withdrawn the 60-70%/95%+ framing, it has stopped treating the yield gate as decisive because there is no TSMC-only path to 12-15mn TPUs with a four-compute-die V9 doubling per-unit capacity consumption. On Intel itself: "although there is still a lot of noise on Intel's EMIB-T progress, especially its learning curve improvement, we see Intel becoming more positive in capacity expansion, which is positive to the Google supply chain."** **NET-NEW capacity path, the first monthly EMIB mark on this page: "Intel's EMIB capacity will reach 10-12k by the end of 2026, and the plan is to double its capacity to 24-25k per month by the end of 2027."** **NET-NEW on where the capex goes: "the CFO highlighted that a great portion of the new capex is earmarked for expanding advanced packaging capabilities such as EMIB-T"** — third-party corroboration that the >$20B FY26 / "significantly above" CY27 step-up is materially a packaging build, i.e. the leg MS calls "reasonably low risk" rather than the wafer-foundry "show-me story." **NET-NEW watch item, corroborating UBS from a second house: "Intel's major supplier Ibiden's upcoming earnings call and potential capex increase in the next two years will be another highlight."** ⚠️ **Two limits kept on the record: Fubon supplies no new EMIB-T yield number here; and "Intel's supply is a must" is a statement about Google's arithmetic, not a disclosed order — it does not close this page's "still no named anchor customer" bear.** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Our_advanced_packaging_CoWoS_forecast_adjustments_other_up.html)
