    - **🆕 The EMIB-T book, finally sized — and the yield spread on this page finally reconciled (UBS · Sunny Lin / Randy Abrams / Nicolas Gaudois / Timothy Arcuri / Shingo Hirata / Jerry Su / Diana Chang, "Can Intel challenge TSMC's advanced packaging leadership? — Intel's EMIB-T vs TSMC's CoPoS", full note, 2026-07-27).** The 07-26 entry captured the TAM and the stress test; the full PDF supplies the two things this page most needed. **First, a revenue model. UBS's base case has Intel's EMIB-T external sales at US$5,438m (2028E) / US$9,570m (2029E) / US$15,791m (2030E)** — **9% / 11% / 13% of industry advanced packaging** — built bottom-up as **Google TPU US$5,003 / 8,374 / 13,159m on 2.3 / 3.5 / 5.0mn packages** plus **Others US$435 / 1,196 / 2,632m on 0.2 / 0.5 / 1.0mn packages**, at **EMIB-T content of US$2,175 / 2,393 / 2,632 per package** (the packages carry ~4 XPU dies each, hence "~24mn accelerator dies or ~6mn chipsets"). UBS's **upside case runs US$5,438 / 11,963 / 21,054m = 9% / 14% / 18% share**, on Google TPU units of 2.3 / 4.5 / 6.5mn. Two things to hold about this: it is the **first per-unit, per-dollar EMIB-T model on this page** (against MS's unquantified "billions next year" and Arete's $1.5bn/$3.7bn '27/'28), and note that **UBS books zero external EMIB-T revenue before 2028** — i.e. Pitzer's "meaningful contribution in 2H26" and UBS's model are not describing the same thing. **Second, the yield reconciliation.** This page has carried marks spanning 50% to 99% and called them incompatible; the full note shows most of the spread is definitional. UBS's four figures, kept distinct: **standard EMIB *packaging* yield is "high (ie >90%)"; standard EMIB *substrate* yield "should've reached… close to 60% level"; EMIB-T *substrate* yield is "around 50% for the leading Japan suppliers"; EMIB-T *packaging* yield is "about 90%, based on trial production runs of MediaTek's TPU v9 prototype."** So the 60-70% (Fubon) and ~50% (UBS) marks are substrate, the >90%/98-99% marks (KeyBanc, Kaizad) are packaging, and the two sets were never in conflict. **What remains genuinely unresolved is the bar, not the number: "for HPC products… packaging yields ideally should improve to the high 90% range, considering the substantial value of the compute chips and HBMs being integrated"** — which is the same place Google's 95%+ gate sits. **Third, the substrate constraint has a named, dated workaround: Ibiden announced a ¥220.0bn investment for its Gama plant in February 2026, with EMIB-T mass production scheduled to begin in late 2027 and a more meaningful ramp in 2028** — and UBS's checks say **"demand from Google TPU programs alone could largely absorb the planned capacity, increasing the likelihood of additional capex announcements as demand visibility improves."** Read plainly: Intel's 2029-30 EMIB-T volume is gated on Ibiden's *next* capex decision, not this one. Unimicron "previously partnered with Intel on prior generations of EMIB, though at relatively low volumes," is developing EMIB-T "although yields remain below optimal levels," and UBS positions it as **second source if volumes exceed expectations** — Ibiden stays primary. **Fourth, three manufacturing/execution specifics not previously on the page:** EMIB-T substrates are **"5x or even larger" than current EMIB packages**, potentially **>9-10x reticle** for AI accelerators (vs 2-3x today) at **20+ layer count**, with a **greater number of silicon bridges** — the cavity-formation step Intel has the most experience with is also the step that gets hardest as area scales; **TC NCF (thermal compression with non-conductive film) will be required for bridge attachment on panel-level substrate cavities rather than standard die attach**, forcing equipment suppliers to optimise TC NCF bonders for **510×515mm² panel-format** substrate; and because EMIB-T **"only involves chip-on-substrate assembly and does not require a separate chip-on-panel stage," the equipment set is simpler than CoPoS's — so "a key leading indicator of EMIB-T capacity expansion is likely to be thermo-compression bonding (TCB) tool demand for die bonders"** (Intel's primary TCB suppliers today: **K&S and ASMPT**; UBS also notes **Intel may rely more on US-based equipment suppliers** where TSMC's chain is Asia-localised). **Fifth, the assembly footprint:** UBS sees Intel **vertically integrating EMIB-T packaging for external customers at its Penang, Malaysia plant (currently expanding)**, while the **2025 Intel-Amkor strategic partnership makes Amkor's Korea, Portugal and US lines available for EMIB assembly** — UBS expects Intel to keep the marquee external programs (Google TPUs, 2028-29) in-house for delivery certainty and outsource the **more mature in-house CPU products' on-substrate packaging to Amkor**, with **C4 wafer bumping** a candidate for further outsourcing given industry tightness. UBS rates **Amkor Neutral, PT US$80** (px US$65). **Finally, UBS's own head-to-head scorecard, because it is more balanced than either side's marketing:** EMIB-T wins on **architecture simplicity, package complexity, scalability beyond 12x reticle, cost potential (assuming reasonable yield) and time-to-market (H2'27 vs 2028)**; CoPoS wins on **performance, power integrity, yield confidence and ecosystem strength**; **manufacturing risk is a tie.** Intel can also **leverage its SMT expertise for board-level integration** to help customers manage warpage — a genuine, rarely-cited Intel advantage. **Two flags to carry rather than smooth over.** ⚠️ **The note is internally inconsistent on TPU v9 mass-production timing:** the manufacturing-challenges section says EMIB-T is **"slated for mass production in late 2027, with Google's TPU v9 expected to be its first major external product,"** while the stock-implications section says Intel is **"prioritizing internal products and Google TPU v9… set for mass production in 2028."** Both are UBS's own words in the same document; the page carries both and reconciles neither — the gap matters because it is the difference between EMIB-T revenue landing in Intel's FY27 or FY28. ⚠️ **And UBS is disagreeing with itself across desks on the same day:** UBS Asia (Sunny Lin) is constructive here and calls the EMIB-T execution risks "manageable," while **UBS US (Timothy Arcuri, "SemiBytes," same 2026-07-27 date) describes EMIB-T as "a process we think still suffers from very low yields and immaturity"** — using it as grounds to be *less* negative on [[AVGO]] versus [[MEDIATEK]] (see [[GOOG]], and the 07-27 entry in this page's Changelog). Same house, same day, opposite characterisations of the same process. Note Arcuri is also the analyst of record on Intel in the Figure-1 table below, which makes the divergence a within-coverage one rather than a regional-desk artefact. UBS's rating stays **Neutral, PT US$121** (px US$100.23, priced 23 Jul 2026; mkt cap US$521,617m; 2026/27E P/E 70.4x/51.1x, P/BV 4.3x/3.6x, ROE 6.2%/7.6%) — **explicitly on valuation, not execution**: "while a successful EMIB-T ramp could lift Intel's foundry sales and profitability, we remain Neutral due to valuation considerations." [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/UBS_on_adv_packaging.html)
  - **🆕 The EDA-ecosystem tell — Intel Foundry's viability, measured in someone else's earnings calls (SemiAnalysis "EDA Market Primer", 2026-05-21).** A genuinely different lens on the foundry thesis, and one this page had no coverage of: **EDA vendors are the foundry kingmakers**, because a foundry that generates no EDA-vendor discussion through PDK development, IP validation or customer design activity "is not a viable manufacturing option." Foundries **co-develop PDKs with EDA vendors ~24 months before production** and then *mandate* specific signoff software for their whole ecosystem — Intel, like TSMC and Samsung, specifies **"Calibre-clean" DRC/LVS as a tape-out criterion**, and **Synopsys's Intel 18A certification (February 2024) was a prerequisite for Intel Foundry existing as a third-party manufacturing option at all**. The measurable signal: **Intel Foundry ecosystem mentions in EDA earnings calls surged from 0-1 per quarter (an 18-year average) to 6 per quarter by 2025Q1** — which SemiAnalysis names one of **four 2026 catalysts** for the whole EDA thesis, framed as *"whether a third foundry meaningfully expands EDA TAM or remains a perpetual aspiration."* That framing is a clean, external, non-Intel-sourced scoreboard for the external-customer question this page is gated on. Two adjacent reads: (a) Intel is also **sized as an EDA buyer and it is large** — Intel was **Synopsys's single largest customer for over two decades, peaking at 17.9% of Synopsys revenue in FY2017, 12.6% ($772M) in FY2024, and below 10% in FY2025** (a milestone SemiAnalysis attributes mostly to Ansys diluting Synopsys's denominator rather than Intel cutting spend) — so Intel's design-tool spend is roughly a $700-800M/yr line at one vendor alone, real negotiating leverage as it courts external customers onto a shared flow; (b) the **1990s-2000s internal-EDA lesson** is Intel's own — Intel built internal P&R tools and "gradually abandoned them for advanced nodes (10nm+) as transistor complexity outpaced internal software teams," as did Samsung and (in a different form) TSMC, which is why foundry vertical integration into EDA has not happened and will not. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_05_21_ENC_EDA_Market_Primer_email.html)
  - **CPU-per-GPU re-rating + supply scarcity.** Tan frames the ratio moving from ~1:8 (training) → 1:4 (inference) → parity (agentic); NVIDIA host-node win (custom Xeon in NVLink for Rubin NVL8) levels the field vs Arm. Server is supply-, not speeds-and-feeds-, constrained today — an IDM with internal wafers has more levers, and tightness likely extends into 2027 (StoneX 2026-05-12). BofA frames the same TAM tailwind: **server CPU TAM ~$27B (CY25) → ~$60B (CY30E), +17% CAGR**, with AI servers ~70% of that and CPUs gaining inference relevance — Intel's *dollar* sales likely still grow even as share falls (BofA CPU deep-dive, 2026-02-23).
  - **Recapitalized balance sheet.** Cash/STI ~$37.4B exiting FY25 after **NVIDIA $5B** (215M shares @ $23.28), **SoftBank $2B** (87M @ $23.00), **US government** (CHIPS Secure Enclave; up to 433M shares to DOC @ $20.47 + 241M warrants @ $20), Altera 51% ($4.3B) and Mobileye monetizations (10-K FY25; Q3/Q4 FY25 calls).
  - **The capex turn / "Intel Should Raise Capital" thesis.** SemiAnalysis (Patel/Kundojjala) frames Q1-26 as the inflection: Intel raised FY26 capex guide to flat y/y (from flat-to-down) — the **first capex guide-up in the saga** — and bought back **Apollo's 49% of Fab 34 for $14.2B** (closed Apr-8-2026, ~$7.7B cash + $6.5B bridge), management calling the SCIP unwind accretive, i.e. conceding the off-balance-sheet structures were expensive money. With ~$51.5B of debt post-bridge and the alternatives (SCIPs, more debt, asset sales) exhausted, SemiAnalysis argues equity is now Intel's cheapest capital and it should raise **~$40B** (~4-5% dilution off a ~$498B mkt cap) to fund the **Terafab** ramp (SpaceX/Tesla anchor; multi-phase up to **$119B**, 100k→1M WSPM) and the **N3-overflow opportunity** — if Agentic-CPU TAM is ~$200bn (~100k WSPM) and most N3 shifts to accelerators, **~50k+ WSPM could flow to Intel** as TSMC stays capex-conservative; it pegs a **$20-35B financing hole**. The order book is filling: NVIDIA DGX Rubin NVL8 lists dual Xeon 6 host CPUs, Google signed a multiyear Xeon + custom-IPU deal, SambaNova on inference (SemiAnalysis "Intel Should Raise Capital", 2026-06-11; building on its Dec-2024 "Intel on the Brink of Death" board-failure thesis). Funding/dilution risk is the obvious flip side — see Bear.
  - **Apple as a foundry customer — potentially the marquee external win.** WSJ (2026-07-11) reports [[AAPL]] secured a **semiconductor-tariff exemption in exchange for committing to Intel's fabs**, extending beyond Mac chips to **iPhone chips at Intel** — if it materializes at scale, exactly the "first major external customer" the whole foundry thesis is gated on, and on Apple's highest-volume product. Follows the mid-June Apple-chip-deal sentiment pop (INTC +8-9%, 06-18) and the M7-on-18A-P reports. Caveat: node/volume/timeline unspecified, and skeptics read prior Apple-Intel foundry noise as capacity leverage over TSMC rather than a real switch (Fubon, 2026-06-05). (WSJ, 2026-07-11; @jukan05, 2026-07-11)
- **Bear:**
  - **Foundry still loses ~$2.4B/qtr and the external book is $174M.** Break-even is an *exit-2027* aspiration that management has repeatedly caveated will slip the moment a big customer is won (you layer cost ahead of revenue) (StoneX 2026-05-12). Long-run target is only ~40% GM / ~30% OM for external wafers & packaging — structurally below Intel's historical product margins, and ASICs/packaging/external wafers all dilute corporate GM as they scale (StoneX 2026-05-12).
  - **Structural server-share erosion to AMD/ARM.** BofA models INTC server value share falling from **63% (CY24) toward ~40% (CY30E)** while AMD goes 33%→~40% and **ARM 5%→20%+** (toward 25%+ if more workloads shift to custom Arm silicon) — the loss is mostly at Intel's expense; ARM already claims ~50% of *new* hyperscale instances and NVIDIA's standalone **Vera Arm CPU (2H26)** is a fresh merchant entrant into Intel's enterprise turf, with the main offset being x86's still-dominant ~80-90% installed-base ecosystem in CY26E (BofA CPU deep-dive, 2026-02-23; Jefferies Techknowledge 2025-12-17). EMIB itself is contested: Kinsus calls plain EMIB **too expensive with very low yield and hard to mass-produce**, and sees IC designers preferring to wait for TSMC to expand CoWoS (Jefferies Techknowledge, 2025-12-17). Fubon's checks reinforce this — **EMIB production yield is only ~60-70% vs TSMC CoWoS 95%+**, and EMIB-T "is not yet ready to enter mass production," so MediaTek/Google's priority stays TSMC CoWoS-L despite the EMIB-T interest (Fubon, 2026-06-05). On servers, UBS independently models Intel's **server-CPU unit share falling 60% (2025) → 29% (2030)** as ARM rises 16%→42% and AMD 24%→29% (UBS server-CPU TAM, 2026-05-21) — directionally the same erosion BofA flagged on a value-share basis.
    - **⚠ SUPERSEDED (2026-08-12) — BofA's CY30E landing zone was cut roughly in half: value share now 61% (CY24) → 40% (CY25) → 34% (CY26E) → 22% (CY30E), not "~40% by CY30E"; AMD lands ~31% (not ~40%) and ARM ~47% total — 38% merchant + 9% custom — not "20-25%+" (BofA · Vivek Arya, 2026-08-12; old values in the Changelog).** ⚠️ **The bear reading is sharper than the old one because it is now decomposed: Intel loses share in VALUE while KEEPING it in UNITS (~36% unit share CY30, still the largest vendor), which is the arithmetic of a vendor whose ASP ($1,579 CY30E) stays ~40% below AMD's ($2,601) and ~56% below ARM merchant's ($4,515). The share loss is a price problem before it is a volume problem.** ✅ **The offsetting fact, and it is why BofA is Buy and not Underperform on the same table: Intel's server-CPU DOLLARS still compound +22.2% CAGR CY26-30 to $46.3bn, and its AI-CPU line (compute node + agentic node) goes $3.9bn CY25 → $27.2bn CY30E (+32.6% CAGR) — so "structural share erosion" and "Intel's best server-CPU dollar growth in a decade" are both true in the same model.** ⚠️ **Cross-check on the ARM leg from the same note's third-party data: Mercury Research's own long-run forecast has Arm server unit share at only ~15-16%, versus BofA's ~34% — the share path is the least-agreed number in this entire debate** (BofA · Vivek Arya, 2026-08-12).
  - **Roadmap still behind on speeds-and-feeds.** Management concedes Diamond Rapids' lack of SMT was "a mistake" (8-channel non-hyperscale SKU pulled); real competitiveness vs AMD waits for **Coral Rapids**, with no firm date. Near-term share stabilization is a who-gets-more-wafers story, not a competitiveness story (StoneX 2026-05-12).
  - **The SMT gap, quantified — the single most important competitive datapoint on Diamond Rapids.** Intel began removing simultaneous multi-threading (hyper-threading) starting with Lion Cove (2024 client PC) — a reasonable trade for PCs given integrated E-cores, but "maximum throughput matters for datacenter CPUs, severely handicapping Diamond Rapids." SemiAnalysis's math: versus the current **128-core/256-thread Granite Rapids**, the new **192-core/192-thread Diamond Rapids will be only around 40% faster**, "exposing Intel for another generation with lower performance than AMD." Because AMD already gets significantly higher performance per core (96-core Turin matches 128-core Granite Rapids), "the performance gap between AMD Venice and Intel Diamond Rapids will widen even more in the 2026 to 2028 generation." Cross-CBB latency is a further overhang: SemiAnalysis "expect[s] cross-CBB latencies to be appreciably worse off" now that die-to-die links run over long substrate traces rather than EMIB (SemiAnalysis "CPUs are Back," 2026-02-09, p.32-33,36).
  - **🆕 The restructuring has opened a supplier-side re-evaluation window — and Intel lost the first round of 18A IP (SemiAnalysis "EDA Market Primer", 2026-05-21).** The flip side of the ecosystem-mentions bull above. **Cadence is gaining traction at Intel, historically a Synopsys stronghold where Cadence had minimal presence**, and SemiAnalysis is explicit about the mechanism: *"Intel Foundry's restructuring and leadership turnover have created evaluation opportunities across the EDA stack that did not previously exist,"* and *"every foundry transition opens a window for competitive re-evaluation, and Intel's current transformation is the largest such window in a decade."* Read as a risk rather than a curiosity: the churn that lets a supplier win share also means **Intel's design-flow ecosystem is being re-qualified from scratch during the exact window it needs external customers to commit** — and that costs time, because switching or re-certifying a flow requires re-characterising IP and PDKs, which is the same friction that makes external customers slow to move to Intel in the first place. Concretely, the 18A ramp already produced a supplier-side casualty with customer consequences: **Synopsys "lacked foundry-ready IP for Intel 18A during the critical ramp window, missing early design wins that went to Cadence"** — i.e. at the moment 18A most needed a full, silicon-proven IP catalogue to attract external tape-outs, the largest IP vendor did not have one ready. A related governance footnote worth knowing: **Lip-Bu Tan explored returning to Cadence as CEO in 2024; the board confirmed Devgan, and Tan went to Intel instead** — the same Cadence that is now taking EDA share at Intel. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_05_21_ENC_EDA_Market_Primer_email.html)
  - **Show-me on the external customer.** A former Marvell AVP was "skeptical about customer shift to Intel for leading-edge nodes — yield/delay risks too high" (AlphaSense expert via briefing, 2026-06-17). 14A funding is explicitly gated on a binding external commitment that has not yet been disclosed (Q2 FY25 call, 2025-07-24).
  - **Dilution + GAAP losses.** Share count jumped to ~5,083M (from 4,343M y/y) on the SoftBank/NVIDIA/DOC issuances; GAAP EPS $(0.73) on restructuring; 10-K flags a potential *pause/discontinuation of 14A and next-gen nodes* if no significant external customer materializes.
  - **"The foundry upside is already in the market cap" (BofA's Vivek Arya).** On a CPU-roadmap call, Arya argued INTC's market cap rose **~$110B since the prior earnings call**, conceptually already anticipating near-perfect foundry execution: at his model's **$3B (2027) / $6B (2028) external foundry revenue**, applying TSMC's ~10x price-to-sales values the external book at **~$60B** (~$100B if external revenue is $10B) — so the move already discounts that. He pegs **best-case earnings power at only ~$2–2.50**, and notes that adding back stock-comp expense would **cut reported EPS by ~30–50%** on an apples-to-apples basis vs NVDA/AVGO/AMD (BofA "CPU roadmap" call, 2026-04-22).
  - **The model doesn't work below mid-40s GM.** Arya frames the gating economics bluntly: to be a viable manufacturer Intel's capital intensity must run **~30–35%** (vs TSMC's 40–50%), and **without sustainable mid-40s gross margin** it cannot translate sales into meaningful EPS or cash — "the toughest thing for the company to do." Near-term GM upside is real but quality-flagged: the **server-CPU growth is mostly ASP, not units** (Q1 units roughly **flat y/y**), and the offset is that holding PC share forces **more wafers from TSMC at higher cost** plus the 18A yield drag (BofA "CPU roadmap" call, 2026-04-22).
  - **Intel's server ASP base is structurally low — the "growth" is partly catch-up.** Exiting Q4-25, **Intel blended server ASP ~$800 vs AMD ~$1,400** (Intel sells more single-socket telco/workstation; cloud ASPs are closer) — so Intel's ASP-led growth is off a very low base, while AMD's growth is unit-led (~2/3 units, 1/3 ASP) (BofA "AMD callback" call, 2026-05-06).
  - **Management would rather be a foundry than a product company.** Asked directly, Intel said it might be **"happier making ARM CPUs"** — the entity gets a higher valuation framed as a manufacturer than as a product company, since keeping up with AMD on product "has not proven easy"; Arya expects Intel to **lose product share but possibly gain on manufacturing** (e.g. an ARM CPU or Apple M-series tile fabbed at Intel; SoftBank already an Intel investor) (BofA "AMD callback" call, 2026-05-06).
  - **TSMC ex-exec: Intel is ~1.5–2 years behind on yield/process.** A 26-year TSMC sales/marketing veteran put Intel **~1.5–2 years behind TSMC** (Samsung ~1 year), with TSMC at **~70% foundry share vs Intel <10%**; the core gap is **yield, not density** — Intel actually packs *more* transistors/mm² at 2nm (tighter metal pitch) but lacks the volume "pipe-cleaner" that drives TSMC's yield learning. Switching a leading-edge product to Intel takes **~1+ year of co-development / custom IP libraries**, so wins require advance engagement (TSMC former-exec call, 2026-04-14).
  - **Former Samsung EVP: Intel foundry has "no chance" to catch TSMC within 2-3 years.** A Capstone expert call with a former Samsung EVP (now a photonics-startup CEO) put it starkly: Intel is seen as having **no chance to catch up to TSMC's leading edge within the next 2-3 years**, and — a concrete, present-tense tell rather than a forward risk — **internal Intel products are increasingly shifting production to TSMC** (Capstone expert call, former Samsung EVP/photonics CEO, 2026-06-30). This is a harder-line version of the TSMC former-exec's ~1.5-2yr gap call above, from a rival-IDM vantage point rather than a TSMC one.
  - **Terafab poaches an 18-year Intel veteran and former Intel Arizona fab manager.** Elon Musk's **Terafab** ($55bn chip-manufacturing JV between Tesla/SpaceX) hired **Gary Jiang**, an 18-year Intel veteran and former **Intel Arizona factory manager**, as its first major hire in a foundry-manager role — a concrete talent departure from Intel into a new, well-funded competing fab project, and a data point that cuts against the "Intel benefits from supplying/servicing Terafab" framing elsewhere on this page (The Information · Grace Kay, 2026-07-01).
  - **AMD data-center crossover comparator.** In its AMD/Lisa Su profile, Barron's notes "AMD pulled even with Intel in [data centers] in 2024, and Wall Street analysts expect AMD's data-center sales to surpass Intel's in 2026" — a comparator on the same server-share erosion theme flagged above (Barron's, 2026-06-22). Separately, in its Nvidia cover story Barron's frames Nvidia's stand-alone CPU business (Vera/Grace) as already "roughly the size of Intel's own data-center business" — a scale comparator for how far merchant-Arm CPU competition has come (Barron's, 2026-06-01).
- **Where the sell-side/buy-side stands:** Improving but still skeptical — the move is sentiment, not yet earnings. **Bernstein (David Dai / Stacy Rasgon) Outperform, PT $100** (~8x EV on avg FY27/FY28 sales ~$66.1B), and expects INTC to be the **FIRST to adopt High-NA EUV, in 2028** (at A14) ahead of Samsung logic (~2029) and TSMC (~2030) (Bernstein "ASML: High-NA EUV cost deep dive", 2026-07-06) — an upgrade from the prior Market-Perform $100 carried on the page (briefing 2026-06-17; prior MP now in Changelog). **Goldman Sachs (James Schneider) Neutral, 12-mo PT $150** (30x normalized EPS $5.00); expects modest 2Q upside with 3Q guidance ~in line (server-CPU strength offset by tepid PC), with investor focus on external foundry customers (2H26), advanced packaging, and 2H gross-margin puts/takes on the 18A/14A ramp — views the structural-turnaround thesis as "largely priced in" (Goldman Sachs "2Q Preview...", 2026-07-05). **BofA Buy, PT $160** (Vivek Arya, "Core Wars Q2 Preview", 2026-07-13; rating per the note's ratings table — C-1-9 = Buy) — values INTC as a fully-integrated IDM with **~$6.24 CY30E EPS power** at **31x PE (up from 25x prior on the peer re-rating), discounted back two years**; models **INTC server-CPU sales ~$40bn+ by CY30E (~25% of a $170bn TAM)**, Products OpM up **~600bps in Q1 vs the 2025 avg**, 18A-P/14A engagements building — implying **10.7x total-company 2028E EV/S** (above the 1.7x-4x historical range). **This supersedes BofA's prior Underperform / PO $96** ("AI 2030", 2026-05-13; up from $56 on 04-24, $40 on 02-23; SOTP internal IDM $74 + external foundry $21-22) — a two-notch move to Buy on the server-CPU + external-foundry opportunity _(prior $96/$56/$40 POs in Changelog)_. **⚠ UPDATED 2026-08-12: BofA CUT the PO to $145 (Buy maintained) — 29x CY30E EPS power of "$6+" discounted back two years, implying 8.9x 2028E EV/S (from 31x/$6.24/10.7x). The $160 mark is superseded and sits in the Changelog. The nuance worth carrying: this is a PO cut delivered inside a TAM RAISE (CY30E server-CPU TAM ~$170bn → $210.6bn), with Intel's own dollars revised UP (~$40bn+ → $46.3bn CY30E) and its value share revised DOWN (~24% → 22%) — so the cut is a multiple/valuation-frame decision, not an estimate cut** (BofA · Vivek Arya, "Rise of the agents: raising CPU TAM (again) to $210bn", 2026-08-12). **Mizuho (Vijay Rakesh) Neutral, PT $135** (~10x F27E P/S; "DC Capex Ramping to >$1.4T in 2028E", 2026-07-11; bull $170 @ $69.8B rev / bear $100 @ $63.3B) — first Mizuho mark on the page; a technology-enabler of the AI-DC-capex ramp but "on the sidelines" (weak foundry roadmap, AI-accelerator lag vs AMD/NVDA, low wafer/memory supply capping 2026 top-line/margin), FPGA spin-off a value driver. **Arete (Janco Venter) Neutral, PT $99** (price $95.04) — constructive on the EMIB-T advanced-packaging ramp ($1.5bn/$3.7bn AP revenue '27/'28; Google 3M-unit TPU order for '28) but the rating stays Neutral; see the 07-21 Intra-quarter row. **MS (Joseph Moore) Equal-weight, PT $75** (raised from $73 on 2026-07-20 into the print, "Weekly: Earnings Week 1") — MS expects a good Q2 print with a likely modest upside surprise to the Sept-Q EPS guide, but explicitly sees **"better risk-reward elsewhere"** in semis (first MS mark carried on the page; prior MS references were bus-tour sales commentary, not a rated mark). The more cautious houses sit lower: **UBS Neutral, PT $122** (Timothy Arcuri, dated 06 Jul 2026) — PT via a SOTP of the five contributing segments (Intel Products, Intel Foundry, Altera, Mobileye, All Other), each valued on a tailored P/S multiple applied to F2026E revenues (UBS "SpaceX's Terafab", 2026-07-07). _(This $122 supersedes UBS's prior Neutral $40 SOTP from the Dec-2025 2026 overview — old framework moved to Changelog.)_ **The Terafab angle is now the incremental UBS INTC read:** UBS thinks of **INTC as a potential "knowledge owner" in SpaceX's Terafab** — a technology-licensing arrangement analogous to the historical IBM-AMD technology-transfer framework that ultimately seeded GlobalFoundries: INTC would provide access to **process flows, manufacturing IP, PDKs, design rules, tool recipes, and operational know-how** while **retaining ownership of the underlying technology and collecting license/royalty payments**. Alternative structures UBS floats: on a successful pilot, INTC could **dedicate an entire site to Terafab in a JV** — potentially its **"Ohio One" site (large enough for two leading-edge fabs)** as one of Terafab's campuses; or **SpaceX invests in INTC further down the road** (likelihood *increasing* if Terafab ultimately proves unsuccessful). INTC **has already engaged with SpaceX** on Terafab, and UBS's recent supply-chain checks suggest **INTC may be helping Terafab secure tool slots at toolmakers** (UBS "SpaceX's Terafab", 2026-07-07). (Prior UBS view — Neutral $40 SOTP: Intel Products ~$31/sh at ~$51.5B rev / 20% normalized EBIT / 15x EV/EBIT, net Intel Foundry ~$9/sh, Altera ~$1/sh — in Changelog.) INTC **+9% on 2026-06-18 on an Apple chip deal** (GS TMT, briefing 2026-06-18) and **+2.6% pre-mkt 2026-06-17** on the 18A-P update. MS bus tour: bullish, citing CEO commitment to foundry and positive microprocessor view (briefing 2026-06-15); INTC included in MS West Coast Semis bus tour bullish names (briefing 2026-06-16). Intel is the most-mentioned name across the equity-call/briefing repo — the read-throughs run through ASML/ASMI (18A/14A capacity), TSMC (foundry competition) and the packaging complex. **🆕 2026-08-19 — MS's EW / $84 mark is re-confirmed, and the scenario band is the new information: in Exhibit 12 of MS · Salvatore / Blake / Byrd et al, "AI Sovereignty: Policy, Market, and Economic Implications" (ratings-screen data as of 12-Aug-2026; MS analyst Joseph Moore), INTC.O sits at Equal-weight, PT $84.0 vs $100.95 last — base −17%, bull +36%, bear −50%, materiality "Significant", mcap US$515.2bn. That is the WIDEST bull-bear spread of any name in the note's semis cohort and the only one where the bear case is a halving. ⚠️ The combination is the point: MS argues at length that AI sovereignty drives domestic fab demand (US$860bn of US AI capex this year alone, plus a call for further federal support for the build-out) and still will not underwrite the equity — thematic centrality ≠ rated upside. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/USELECTION_20260819_1049.html)**
  - **Post-Q2'26-print marks (2026-07-24) — everyone raised numbers, almost nobody raised the rating.** The striking feature of the day-after flow is that **estimates went up 20-40% across the board while ratings barely moved** — the disagreement has shifted entirely onto capex/FCF and the multiple. **MS (Joseph Moore) Equal-weight, PT $84 — raised from $75** (CY27 EPS $1.77 → **$2.00**, same 42x target multiple = high end of peer group on operating-leverage potential; CY26 estimates to **$62.72bn rev / 40.8% GM / $1.57 EPS** from $58.76bn / 40.9% / $1.19) — "strong upside… stay EW"; the sidelining rationale is explicit: *"Running free cash negative during the biggest server inflection in history puts more pressure than we would like on foundry prospects."* **JPM (Harlan Sur) Underweight — new Dec-27 PT $85, from a Dec-26 PT of $45** (24x, group 20-25x, × **$3.60 CY28 EPS**) — a near-doubling of the target that *still* sits ~15% below spot, hence UW maintained; JPM materially raised forward estimates. **UBS (Timothy Arcuri) Neutral, PT $121** (from $122; C2027/2028 EPS raised to **$2.17/$2.82**) — likes the manufacturing narrative, the **product roadmap remains the objection**; blue-sky **~$6-7 EPS in 2030** on a $150bn revenue scenario (~$60bn server, ~$40bn client, ~$40bn advanced packaging + foundry, ~$10bn ASIC), "not enough to get us overly excited at this price given the financing risk/potential dilution." **Barclays (Tom O'Malley) Equal Weight, PT $100 unchanged** (first Barclays mark on the page) — "consecutive sizable top-line and GM beats is encouraging," but the Q2 GM beat was **largely ASPs** and the Q3 guide is closer to **flat q/q adjusted for the inventory write-down**; "**No IFS customer yet but another step in the right direction**." **BofA (Vivek Arya) Buy, PO $160 reiterated** — raising CY26/27/28E EPS **20-40%**, reads the capex raise + 14A commit + the SK-Hynix packaging hire as three signals of coming foundry success, expects Intel capex growth **at least in line with ASML's ~30%+ annual WFE growth**; flags FCF near-zero-or-negative through CY26-27 and foundry break-even pushed out. Bernstein's desk framing is the neatest summary of the split: **"INTC solid print though capex and FCF story feels eerily similar"** (Bernstein · Tyler Seidman / David Patafio, 2026-07-24). Also on the tape: **Guotai (via @sssjeffpu) raised its TP to $136** (5x 2027E BVPS / 34x 2028E EPS), noting **18A yields beating internal targets** and Panther Lake in line with its ~80% assumption (@sssjeffpu, 2026-07-24). Buy-side read from Daniel Niles: INTC "reported ~2x the EPS & beat revs by 12%… **they raised capex (18%) much like their customers did yesterday but unlike their customers their Q3 EPS is going up by ~40%** — I like infrastructure beneficiaries w/ expanding margins of all that spend, not the spenders" (@DanielTNiles, 2026-07-24).

## Catalysts / what to watch
- **🆕🔴 THE 14A "0.9 PDK" GATE IS NOW MANAGEMENT-CONFIRMED AND DATED TO THE LATE-OCTOBER EARNINGS CALL** — *"management expects to confirm the 0.9 PDK on the late-October earnings call, ahead of risk production in 2027 and volume in 2028"* (CFO David Zinsner, $15bn follow-on roadshow call, 2026-08-10). Same call: **14A described as "a straightforward shrink with the hardest engineering (GAA, backside power) already done"** and its **defect-density trajectory "the best Intel has seen since 22nm"**; **18A "meeting or beating its ~7% monthly defect-density improvement target."**
- **🆕 EQUIPMENT PURCHASE ORDERS "WITHIN THE NEXT FEW WEEKS", ACROSS MULTIPLE VENDORS** — management said Intel *"won't place purchase orders without identified funding"*, which is why the raise came now (2026-08-10). ⚠️ **A datable WFE order catalyst**: cross-ref [AMAT.md](AMAT.md), [LRCX.md](LRCX.md), [KLAC.md](KLAC.md), [ASML.md](ASML.md), [TOKYOELEC.md](TOKYOELEC.md), [themes/semicap-wfe.md](themes/semicap-wfe.md).
- **🆕⚠️ FOUNDRY BREAK-EVEN — WATCH THE RE-FRAMING, NOT JUST THE DATE.** Still **exit-2027**, but management now says it **"could slip into 2028"** and **pre-framed that slip as a positive, demand-driven signal rather than an execution miss** (2026-08-10). Log the outcome against the framing when it lands.
- **✅ Q2 FY26 print — RESOLVED 2026-07-23 (bull won): big beat & raise, stock +8-9%.** Rev $16.1B / GM 41.8% / EPS $0.42 all well above Street; Q3 guide $15.8-16.8B / GM ~42% / EPS $0.38 above cons; 2026 capex raised to $18-20B; multi-year CPU/XPU LTAs signed; 14A HVM pulled in to 2028. See Current state + the fresh Q2'26 Intra-quarter section. Next watch: **Q3 FY26 call (Oct-2026)** — GM durability, external-foundry revenue scaling, server-CPU share, first 14A external-customer disclosure. _(Pre-print preview retained below for the record.)_ **Citi preview (07-17):** models **2Q26 sales/EPS $14.4B (+6% Q/Q)/$0.24 vs cons $14.36B/$0.21** and **3Q26 $15.1B (+5% Q/Q)/$0.29, above cons** — on DC upside from better-than-expected CPU demand + custom-ASIC sales and opex discipline; sees **foundry sales scaling in 2027 driven by EMIB packaging + Apple/NVDA/MediaTek/Terafab**. UBS Tech Musings (07-18) carries INTC among its earnings previews. Context: **IBM −26% negative pre-announcement** contributed to the week's momentum reset (IT-spend read). (Citi · Atif Malik, 2026-07-17; UBS · Ruple, 2026-07-18)
- **🆕 CY27 capex number (formal, January 2027) + the capital-raise decision — now the #1 swing item.** On the Q2'26 call mgmt guided CY27 capex "significantly above" the >$20B FY26 level but **deferred the formal number to January**, conceded that **positive 2027 FCF is "more challenged,"** and said it **"may need to tap the capital markets."** Street marks for CY27: **MS $30bn / JPM $26bn (flagged as possibly light) / UBS ~$30bn**. Watch (a) the January number, (b) any equity/convert issuance — **share count is already ~5.1B vs ~4.4B a year ago**, so dilution would land exactly as EPS momentum builds — and (c) the alternatives management/BofA point to instead: **CHIPS grants, non-core asset disposals (~$10B monetizable), and customer prepayments**. Liquidity today is not the constraint (~$30B cash + $10B revolver). This is the item on which MS ($84 EW), JPM ($85 UW) and UBS ($121 Neutral) all explicitly hang their refusal to upgrade despite raising EPS 20-40%. (MS · Moore; JPM · Sur; UBS · Arcuri; BofA · Arya, all 2026-07-24)
- **🆕 14A: 0.9 PDK in October 2026 → risk production 2H27 → HVM 2028.** Now a fully committed roadmap with dated gates. The **October 0.9 PDK is the near-term watch item** — JPM notes customer decisions become possible "relatively quickly" after it, since yield extrapolation turns roughly linear from that point (UBS). UBS believes the commitment itself implies a firm customer-group commitment, **most likely Apple (compute processor), a gaming console, and possibly Google (though mostly EMIB-related)**. Note this timeline **supersedes the older MS bus-tour read of 14A risk production Q4-2028 / volume 2029** (see Changelog). (JPM · Sur; UBS · Arcuri, 2026-07-24)
- **First major external 14A / foundry customer announcement** — the single biggest swing; 14A design-win window "opens 2H-2026 into 1H-2027"; risk production 2H-2027 internal / 2H-2029 external (StoneX 2026-05-12). Watch the **Apple chip deal** disclosed mid-June for substance.
- **18A ramp milestones** — Panther Lake (notebook) through 2026, **Nova Lake** (notebook + desktop) late-2026/early-2027, then server onto 18A; 18A-P process update (post-close, mid-June 2026). Watch the **in-sourcing mix**: Intel now reportedly plans **80-90% of Nova Lake-S tiles on 18A** (from ~60-70%-to-TSMC), and KeyBanc pegs **18A yield >85%** (@QQ_Timmy/TrendForce 2026-07-15; KeyBanc via @firstadopter 2026-07-14) — confirm on the Q2 call.
- **🔴 EMIB PRODUCTION YIELD — the six-month learning curve, now the swing factor for the whole external-packaging thesis (Fubon · Sherman Shang / Arthur (hosted by Jefferies), "industry highlights & checks update", 2026-08-03).** Fubon's watch, verbatim: *"we still need to closely monitor Intel's expansion and production learning curve improvement in the next six months."* ⚠️ **The mark to track against, and it is the sharpest on this page because it separates lab from production for the first time: "some reports are talking about the production rate has already improved to 90%, but I don't think that is a real number. I heard the 90% rate is just from the LAB… The production rate is lower than that — I heard it is only around 75%."** That reframes UBS's ~90% (measured on MediaTek TPU v9 **trial** runs) as a trial figure and contradicts the same-morning DIGITIMES "yields hit target" headline. **Bar to clear: UBS's stated HPC requirement of "high 90%" and the 95%+ gate Google reportedly set.** See [[MEDIATEK]], [themes/cowos-packaging](themes/cowos-packaging.md).
- **🔴 Ibiden earnings call — 2026-08-04, "another important indicator for Intel's EMIB" (Fubon, 2026-08-03).** Fubon hears **Ibiden** (ASR: "Epident") *"is also very aggressive in expanding their substrate capacity to support Intel's production in the next two years,"* following Unimicron's positive capex commentary — and explicitly flags the call as the near-dated read. **This is now the third house making Ibiden's capex the gate on EMIB-T volume beyond the first tranche** (after UBS 07-27 — "demand from Google TPU programs alone could largely absorb the planned capacity" — and Fubon's own 07-28 note). ⚠️ **Watch it as a proxy for whether the "double capacity in both 2027 and 2028" plan is actually funded down the chain.**
- **Advanced packaging (EMIB/EMIB-T)** order conversion — first ramp year 2027, billions/engagement by year two; capacity adds and tool lead-times into 2027; watch **EMIB-T "meaningful contribution in 2H26"** (Pitzer) and any Google TPU v9 / Meta MTIA confirmation (Jefferies Techknowledge, 2025-12-17).
- **Server roadmap:** Granite Rapids (Intel 3) workhorse 2026; Diamond Rapids; **Coral Rapids** pull-in (no firm date) — the true AMD-competitiveness gate. **ARM 'Arm Everywhere' (Mar-24-2026)** merchant-CPU unveil and NVDA Vera (2H26) standalone deployments are the new x86-displacement watch items (BofA CPU deep-dive, 2026-02-23). MS bus-tour cross-read adds dates and a caution: Lip-Bu Tan reiterated Diamond Rapids' dropped SMT was a "flawed architecture" but **Coral Rapids on 18A-P appears pulled up to mid-2027** (from 2028) as the gap-narrowing part; meanwhile MS heard in Taiwan that **the highest-end server family beyond Coral Rapids, plus the Nova Lake desktop, likely stay at TSMC** — i.e. Intel's most process-sensitive products still lean external, a tell on internal-node confidence (MS bus tour / Joe Moore, 2026-06-16).
  - **SemiAnalysis's independent Coral Rapids read is later than MS's mid-2027 pull-in and worth flagging as a rival data point.** SemiAnalysis (2026-02-09, p.65-66) has Lip-Bu Tan trying to accelerate Coral Rapids following the Diamond Rapids-SP cancellation (see Risks), but believes it was originally designed for Intel's **14A process (P1280, risk production 2027)**, implying a launch "in late 2028 at the earliest, if not early 2029"; to pull it in, Intel "may have to port the design to a refined 18A-Ultra node." The critical fix: Coral Rapids **returns SMT to the P-core architecture** — Intel "realized their mistake." Watch for which of the two timing reads (MS mid-2027 vs SemiAnalysis late-2028/early-2029) proves out; a 18A-Ultra port would reconcile them by pulling SemiAnalysis's date forward.
  - **NVLink-C2C custom Xeon — an x86 alternative to Nvidia rackscale GPU designs.** Before Coral Rapids, SemiAnalysis expects Intel to release a custom Xeon CPU with an **NVLink-C2C chiplet**, positioning x86 as an alternative to Nvidia's own rackscale GPU/CPU pairing rather than only a host-CPU supplier to it (SemiAnalysis "CPUs are Back," 2026-02-09, p.65).
- **Foundry milestones (MS bus tour):** Lip-Bu Tan "all-in" and bullish on foundry with "significant potential capex"; cited **14A defect density 0.5 → 0.1–0.2 by 1Q27** (the viability threshold), but reiterated **14A risk production Q4-2028, volume 2029**, with **first foundry revenue ~end-2028**. Packaging is "ahead of wafers" as the near-term foundry opportunity; the **MediaTek TPU packaging opportunity "seems real"** but not yet locked (MS bus tour / Joe Moore, 2026-06-16).
- **Capex/Ohio:** equipment spend +25% y/y FY26; Ohio facilitization "after 2030" unless external wins pull it forward; FY27 capex number not yet given.
- **SK Hynix–Ohio-fab JV/sale chatter (unconfirmed):** Korean media (JoongAng Ilbo / @jukan05, 07-21) floated **SK Hynix acquiring Intel's Ohio fab for US memory production** under US-government local-investment pressure; both sides denied a sale/acquisition (07-21) but **neither denied a possible JV**, and SK Hynix's 07-22 statement said it "has neither pursued nor decided" on the plant. Watch for any JV structure — a potential Ohio-site monetization / US-local-memory catalyst. (@jukan05 / Korean media, 2026-07-21; SK Hynix, 2026-07-22)
- **2026 capex guide-up watch (FundaAI, 2026-07-08):** FundaAI sees Intel **possibly raising its 2026 capex guidance from $18bn to $20bn** at the Q2 earnings call — would be a second consecutive guide-up (after the flat-y/y raise at Q1) and a positive read for WFE suppliers. (mixed)
- **Leading-edge WFE broadening as a 2027 tailwind (MS KLAC note, Shane Brett / Joseph Moore, 2026-07-09):** MS frames a **broadening of leading-edge WFE spend across Intel, Rapidus and TeraFab** as a key 2027 tailwind for process-control/WFE demand — a read-through of rising Intel capex confidence. (mixed)
- **🆕 EDA-vendor commentary as the external-foundry scoreboard (SemiAnalysis "EDA Market Primer", 2026-05-21).** Track **Intel Foundry ecosystem mentions on Synopsys/Cadence/Siemens earnings calls** — the run from an 18-year average of 0-1 per quarter to **6 per quarter (2025Q1)** is SemiAnalysis's named 2026 catalyst for the question *"whether a third foundry meaningfully expands EDA TAM or remains a perpetual aspiration."* It is a free, third-party, quarterly read on external design activity that leads reported external-foundry revenue by design cycles, and it does not depend on Intel disclosing customer names. Watch alongside it whether **Synopsys closes its 14A foundry-ready-IP gap in time** (it missed the 18A ramp window, with wins going to Cadence) — a full, silicon-proven IP catalogue from *both* vendors at 14A PDK 0.9 (October 2026) is a precondition for external tape-outs, not a nice-to-have. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_05_21_ENC_EDA_Market_Primer_email.html)
- **Photomask capacity build — Intel Mask Operations (IMO), Santa Clara.** Intel has broken ground on a new photomask manufacturing facility at the Bowers Campus, expanding IMO — an under-the-radar leading-edge supply-chain investment (masks feed directly into 18A/14A litho) worth tracking for timeline/capacity disclosures (Semiecosystem/Substack · Mark LaPedus, 2026-07-01).

## Risks
- **🔴 THE EMIB FAILURE CASE IS NOW EXPLICIT, AND IT IS THE BULL CASE'S OWN AUTHOR WHO STATES IT (Fubon · Sherman Shang, 2026-08-03).** The same analyst who says *"Intel's is a must have in 2028"* also says, asked what could break the [[MEDIATEK]] ASIC numbers: **"I think Intel's production is the biggest risk… if Intel is unable to enter production, that means MediaTek, they need to go back to TSMC. And I don't think TSMC has the capacity to support Google to do 12 million to 15 million. So when that happens, we will probably see some potential downside for the numbers."** ⚠️ **Those are not contradictory statements — they are the same one: Intel's EMIB has become the single swing factor for the whole [[GOOG]]/[[MEDIATEK]] TPU ramp, which is simultaneously the largest identified external-packaging prize on this page and the largest concentration of execution risk. The yield input is the bear side: reported ~90% is a LAB figure per Fubon, with production "only around 75%," against UBS's high-90s HPC bar. Note also the fragility of the demand-side endorsement — Fubon says MediaTek's rising confidence is a response to Intel's CAPACITY aggression ("they are seeing Intel's more aggressive capacity expansion"), NOT to its yield, and MediaTek keeps "two advanced packaging technologies in their pocket" despite officially saying it "will only use Intel" for V9.** Compounding it: **[[TSM]] is working on an in-house EMIB alternative** (same call; "very, very early stage, no production timeline yet"), which caps how long the window stays open. See [[MEDIATEK]], [[TSM]], [themes/cowos-packaging](themes/cowos-packaging.md).
- **No external 14A customer → potential pause/discontinuation of 14A and next-gen leading-edge nodes** (explicit 10-K risk factor).
- **Foundry losses persist**; break-even slips when a big customer is layered on; ~40/30 endpoint GM/OM structurally below legacy product margins; ASIC/packaging/external-wafer mix dilutive to corporate GM.
- **Competitive position vs AMD/Arm** in servers still gated on Coral Rapids; Diamond Rapids SMT misstep; hyperscaler BYO (internal Arm) silicon a structural share headwind in DCAI; BofA sees server value share toward ~40% by CY30E (BofA CPU deep-dive, 2026-02-23).
- **Sierra Forest — direct hyperscaler-rejection evidence.** SemiAnalysis reports hyperscaler adoption of the 144-core E-core Sierra Forest Xeon "has been limited" because hyperscalers are "already adopting AMD and designing their own ARM-based CPUs," while traditional enterprise customers "were not interested" either. The dual-die 288-core Sierra Forest-AP (Xeon 6900E) never reached general availability and survives only as a low-volume, off-roadmap part — concrete, already-realized share loss rather than a forward risk (SemiAnalysis "CPUs are Back," 2026-02-09, p.25).
- **Clearwater Forest's successor line is confirmed dead.** SemiAnalysis titles a section "Clearwater Forest Failure" (p.25); its E-core successor (codenamed Rouge River Forest / DMR-HD) has been cancelled under Lip-Bu Tan's roadmap streamlining — "the series will end with just 2 generations" (SemiAnalysis "CPUs are Back," 2026-02-09, p.65-66).
- **Diamond Rapids-SP (mainstream 8-channel) cancelled outright — a multi-year product-line gap.** In "a late move," Intel cancelled the mainstream Diamond Rapids-SP platform entirely, "leaving their highest volume core market without a new generation into at least 2028." SemiAnalysis calls this "the wrong move," arguing AI tool-use/agentic context-storage workloads favor mainstream memory connectivity over max performance-per-socket flagship parts — i.e. Intel is skipping a generation in the segment where volume (and share defense) actually lives (SemiAnalysis "CPUs are Back," 2026-02-09, p.33).
- **Diamond Rapids yield read-through.** The full CBB die has 256 cores but mainline SKUs enable only up to 192 — a level of core disablement that reads as a yield/binning constraint rather than a clean architectural choice (SemiAnalysis "CPUs are Back," 2026-02-09, p.32).
- **Cross-CBB latency.** Dropping EMIB for long substrate traces between each Core Building Block die and the two I/O/Memory-Hub dies removes an advanced-packaging cost step but SemiAnalysis explicitly flags "cross-CBB latencies to be appreciably worse off" — a potential real-workload performance tax not captured in the headline core-count/TDP specs (SemiAnalysis "CPUs are Back," 2026-02-09, p.32-33).
- **🆕 Negative FCF through CY26-27 + equity-dilution risk — now the consensus bear anchor (2026-07-24).** With FY26 capex >$20B (~$8B above depreciation) and CY27 "significantly above" that, MS models Intel **modestly FCF-negative in both 2026 and 2027**, and management itself conceded 2027 positive FCF is "more challenged" and that it "**may need to tap the capital markets**." Brookfield proceeds also begin being **repaid next year**. The dilution math is the sharp edge: **share count is already ~5.1B vs ~4.4B a year ago** (JPM), so a raise compounds an existing ~17%-a-year issuance pattern from the NVIDIA/SoftBank/DOC transactions. BofA (Buy) concedes the same risk — "FCF remains near-zero or negative through CY26-27, foundry breakeven likely pushed out, w/ high capex for a few years" — but points to CHIPS grants, ~$10B of monetizable non-core assets and customer prepayments as non-dilutive alternatives. MS's framing: *"Running free cash negative during the biggest server inflection in history puts more pressure than we would like on foundry prospects."* Note this is the **inverse of the SemiAnalysis "Intel Should Raise Capital" bull thesis** in Debate/Bull — same facts, opposite conclusion on whether dilution is a cost or an enabler. (MS · Moore; JPM · Sur; UBS · Arcuri; BofA · Arya, all 2026-07-24)
- **🆕 The server earnings stream is partly a shortage rent (MS, 2026-07-24).** MS's caution is worth isolating because it is not a share argument: "earnings prospects from server alone are **at least partially dependent on maintaining a shortage**, as CPUs being fully in supply would likely result in **some share loss and pricing degradation**." Given the Q2 upside was disproportionately **ASP/mix rather than units** in client and price-and-supply-driven in server (China server-CPU prices +40% YTD; Xeon 6980P +~12%), a normalisation of supply is a margin risk even if unit demand holds. "Stocks that trade on the basis of shortages are numerous, and much less expensive than Intel at least on near-term earnings prospects."
- **🆕 Q4 is where the PC headwind lands (2026-07-24).** Client was guided flattish in Q3 partly on **channel CPU builds ahead of the softness**, with mgmt flagging **PC consumption sub-seasonal in 2H and the PC market down low-double-digit % in CY26 on memory inflation** — i.e. the client weakness hits Intel's P&L in **Q4, not Q3**. JPM explicitly lists "memory-driven erosion of the client TAM beginning in 2H26" as one of the three reasons it stays UW. Watch the interaction with the memory-cost thread on [[MU]] / [[SAMSUNG]]: Intel is simultaneously a **victim** of memory inflation (client TAM) and a **beneficiary** of the resulting mix shift up. (JPM · Sur; UBS · Arcuri, 2026-07-24)
- **Execution / yield** — 18A still below management's bar; "7-8% yield improvement/month" but not yet at industry standard.
- **PC TAM down low-double-digits FY26** on memory-cost-driven inflation hitting sell-through; supply-constrained on older nodes (Intel 10/7).
- **Government/partner overhang** — DOC equity (up to 433M shares) + warrants (241M @ $20) contingent on Intel keeping ≥51% of the foundry; NVIDIA/SoftBank/DOC issuance diluted share count ~+17% y/y; geopolitical/trade and China exposure. **Realized once (2026-07-14): INTC fell ~6% as investors reacted to "Trump's heavy hand in the chipmaker's revival"** — the government-involvement risk is not just theoretical, it now moves the tape (@scottlincicome via @carlquintanilla, 2026-07-14).
- **Valuation** — at ~72x NTM PE the stock already discounts meaningful foundry success; downside if the external-customer / 18A-yield narrative stalls (UBS 2026 overview, 2025-12-22).
- **Note on financials:** Intel's reported EPS is **near-breakeven** on a normalized basis; GAAP is swung by large restructuring/impairment charges and equity-investment gains/losses — do not read the GAAP loss as run-rate.

Both sides of the trade now agree that in a shortage Intel's units go to the competitor even where it wins on price.** Other first-party marks logged: **18A "continue to beat," 14A "tracking close to 22nm node performance," 14A ramping in '28 with more revenue in '29; "the TIGHTEST cyclical environment they have ever seen"; EMIB "great validation with strong customer interest" + the SK Hynix hire, "preparing for a healthy ramp"; and no-named-customers restated as POLICY ("won't announce customer names"), not absence.** ⚠️ **Two items that cut against the bull case and are logged as such: (a) Foundry break-even is UNCHANGED at a "run-rating $8B loss," and Pitzer says winning external customers PUSHES break-even OUT because they bring incremental capex/opex — i.e. the anchor-customer win this page waits for has a cost; (b) on a capital raise, "LipBu is financially disciplined and WOULD NOT TAKE ANY STRATEGY OFF THE TABLE," which is not a denial, read against management's Q2 concession that 2027 FCF is "more challenged." Also: Intel 3 and 18A are beating internal targets but remain BELOW internal GM targets, a stated headwind; new "Rule of 45" framework (growth + OPM = 45%); Analyst Day committed but NO DATE.** Second row covers the packaging chain: **Fubon's checks show Intel "MORE AGGRESSIVE IN CAPACITY EXPANSION (doubling in both 2027/28)," with Ibiden expected to follow Unimicron with 2027/28 capex** — and two net-new framing items, **(i) [[TSM]] "also working on their IN-HOUSE EMIB solution… still very early stage, no production timeline yet"** (the first sign the incumbent is copying rather than ignoring the technology — caps the window's duration), and **(ii) the causal explanation for the win itself: "Google turned to Intel for EMIB packaging because NVIDIA PRE-BOOKED OVER 50% OF TSMC'S COWOS FOR THE NEXT 3 YEARS" (@dnystedt, 2026-08-02)** — capacity displacement, not technology preference. **DIGITIMES headline "Intel EMIB yields hit target" logged WITH the caveat that it discloses no number and sits against the 50-60% substrate-yield bracket already on the page and Pitzer's own guarded "doing the work to ramp to high quality/volume."** Also noted for context: **INTC is one of the ten names in BofA's new "US 10 — Top Pitches from the US Sales Team" (BofA · Brian Fenske, 2026-08-03)** — a sales-desk pitch list, not a research rating. Only net-new content was added.** **Two of the additions are materially useful. (1) The EMIB-T book is now SIZED for the first time on this page: base case US$5,438m (2028E) / US$9,570m (2029E) / US$15,791m (2030E) — 9%/11%/13% of industry advanced packaging — decomposed as Google TPU US$5,003/8,374/13,159m on 2.3/3.5/5.0mn packages plus Others US$435/1,196/2,632m on 0.2/0.5/1.0mn, at EMIB-T content of US$2,175/2,393/2,632 per package (~4 XPU dies each). Upside case: US$5,438/11,963/21,054m = 9%/14%/18% share on Google TPU units of 2.3/4.5/6.5mn. Flagged for the reader: UBS books ZERO external EMIB-T revenue before 2028, which is not the same picture as Intel CVP John Pitzer's "meaningful contribution in 2H26" carried elsewhere on this page — a genuine timing divergence, left unreconciled.** **(2) The yield spread this page complained about on 07-26 ("60% to 99%… three incompatible readings") turns out to be mostly definitional, and the full note separates the four figures cleanly: standard-EMIB PACKAGING ">90%"; standard-EMIB SUBSTRATE "close to 60%"; EMIB-T SUBSTRATE "around 50% for the leading Japan suppliers"; EMIB-T PACKAGING "about 90%, based on trial production runs of MediaTek's TPU v9 prototype."** So Fubon's 60-70% and UBS's ~50% are substrate marks while KeyBanc's 98% and ex-Intel-CVP Kaizad's 98-99% are packaging marks — they were never measuring the same thing. **The 07-26 framing that this page carries "three incompatible readings" is therefore partly retired; what genuinely remains open is the BAR, not the numbers — UBS: "for HPC products… packaging yields ideally should improve to the high 90% range," which is where Google's 95%+ TPU-v9 gate sits.** Other net-new: **Ibiden's ¥220.0bn Gama-plant investment (Feb-2026) puts EMIB-T MP at late 2027 with a meaningful 2028 ramp, but "demand from Google TPU programs alone could largely absorb the planned capacity"** — so 2029-30 volume is gated on Ibiden's *next* capex decision, with **Unimicron** (yields "below optimal") as second source only if volumes surprise; **EMIB-T substrates are "5x or even larger" than today's, potentially >9-10x reticle at 20+ layers**, requiring **TC NCF rather than standard die attach** into panel-level (510×515mm²) cavities; but the **equipment set is SIMPLER than CoPoS's** (no chip-on-panel stage), making **TCB tool demand (K&S, ASMPT) the named leading indicator of EMIB-T capacity expansion**, with Intel likely leaning on **US-based** equipment suppliers; the **assembly footprint is Penang (expanding, vertically integrated for external customers) plus Amkor's Korea/Portugal/US lines under the 2025 partnership**, with Intel expected to keep Google TPUs in-house and outsource mature CPU on-substrate work (**Amkor Neutral, PT US$80**); and UBS's **head-to-head scorecard — EMIB-T wins architecture simplicity, package complexity, scalability >12x reticle, cost (assuming reasonable yield) and time-to-market; CoPoS wins performance, power integrity, yield confidence and ecosystem; manufacturing risk a TIE** — plus a rarely-cited Intel edge, its **SMT expertise for board-level warpage management**. Rating rationale restated because it matters for how the page reads UBS: **Neutral is a valuation call ("we remain Neutral due to valuation considerations"), not a verdict on the EMIB-T ramp.** Cross-filed [[TSM]], [[MEDIATEK]], [[AVGO]], [[TOKYOELEC]].

- 2026-07-26 — **/wiki-ingest (Outlook, weekend batch). 4 intra-quarter rows. No rating, PT or estimate superseded** (UBS Neutral $121, MS EW $84, JPM UW $85, Barclays EW $100, BofA Buy $160, Arete Neutral $99 all stand). **The material item is UBS's first full-length EMIB-T-vs-CoPoS deep dive, and its contribution is to make the yield picture on this page WORSE-defined, not better — which is the honest outcome and is recorded as such.** UBS says Intel "must lift **EMIB-T packaging from ~90-92% validation yields** toward high-volume manufacturing levels," and that **EMIB-T substrate fabrication yields "may currently be at ~50%, vs >80% for HPC ABF substrates."** Set that against the marks already on the page — **Fubon 60-70% (06-05), KeyBanc "reached 98%" (07-24), ex-Intel CVP Kaizad "mature yield 98-99%" (07-16)** — and the **packaging-yield estimates now span 60% to 99%, while substrate estimates span ~50% (UBS) to 60-70% (KeyBanc)**. Two things make this tractable rather than noise: UBS's figure is the **only one explicitly labelled *validation* yield** (which may reconcile part of the spread against production-yield marks), and UBS's own base case **routes around the substrate problem rather than solving it — "capacity expansion at Ibiden could offset lower yields and support selected products like Google's TPU v9."** So the constraint identified on 07-25 (substrates, not bridges) survives, with a lower number attached and a named workaround. **Not resolved. The 95%+ Google TPU-V9 gate remains the single item on which the Foundry thesis turns, and this page now carries three incompatible readings of it.** Net-new sizing: **advanced-packaging TAM US$19bn (2026E) → US$122bn (2030E), a 59% CAGR**, with UBS's *bullish* Intel stress test at **13% industry share / US$16bn of sales by 2030 (~24mn accelerator dies)** — a case in which [[TSM]] still compounds advanced-packaging sales at 50% and holds 65-70% share. **EMIB-T's structural advantages, per UBS: interposer elimination (structural cost), and scalability to ultra-large packages by building directly on large substrates — with MP targeted H2'27-28, slightly AHEAD of CoPoS in 2028.** And the two Intel-specific risks UBS names that were not on this page: **"integration between the foundry (TSMC) and back-end (Intel) processes, as well as yield responsibility"** — a contracting problem (who eats a yield loss when TSMC owns the die and Intel owns the package), not a technical one, and arguably the harder of the two to fix. Rating stays Neutral **explicitly on valuation**, not on execution. **Second net-new thread — glass core substrates now have a dated Intel ramp: Lens Tech (300433.SZ) is partnering with Intel on glass core with TGVs, pilot line ready by end-2026 and "more meaningful volume shipments might start from 2H27"** (MS/Derrick Yang), possibly with Korean players too. That is the structural answer to the substrate-yield constraint (lower signal loss, CTE matching against warpage, mechanical strength at panel scale), and on MS's checks Intel is **~12-18 months ahead of the TSMC/Ibiden glass path (Innolux MP 2H28-2029; BOE capacity 2027 for 2028 MP)** — the same ordering UBS gives for EMIB-T vs CoPoS. MS's caveat kept: helps sentiment, "more meaningful revenue contribution should take time," hurdles are "process control instead of pure equipment capability." **Third: 14A described as ahead of where 18A was — "defect density and transistor performance are outpacing 18A development"** (Jefferies), with Intel "aggressively locking in tool purchase orders and accelerating cleanroom buildouts," 14A "likely to be a material user of high-NA," and [[ASML]] revenue from Intel "subdued this year but likely to accelerate in 2027." The gate is unchanged and worth repeating: **the WFE step-up requires a named 14A customer, and official C27 guidance is ~6 months out.** **Fourth, the tape, recorded because it is the actual outcome: INTC closed -8% Friday having been +5% pre-market on results JPM's Harlan Sur said "cleared expectations by wide margins" — the SOX is -19% from its mid-June high, CPUs have derated -21% since June 22, and INTC is -34% since the Tech-Momentum ATH.** JPM's positioning work explains part of it — aggregate equity positioning light (US TPM 19th %ile) but **global semis crowded at the 98th %ile on both net exposure and L/S ratios**. Cross-filed [[TSM]], [[ASML]], [[MEDIATEK]], [[GOOG]], [themes/cowos-packaging](themes/cowos-packaging.md), [themes/semicap-wfe](themes/semicap-wfe.md).

  - **SemiAnalysis "EDA Market Primer" (Kundojjala/Patel/Wong, 2026-05-21)** — **entirely net-new to this page** (no prior EDA-ecosystem content existed). Added as one Bull sub-bullet, one Bear sub-bullet and one Catalyst. **Bull side:** EDA vendors as **"foundry kingmakers"** — a foundry generating no EDA-vendor discussion via PDK/IP/design activity "is not a viable manufacturing option"; foundries **co-develop PDKs with EDA vendors ~24 months before production**; Intel (like TSMC/Samsung) **mandates "Calibre-clean" DRC/LVS for tape-out**; **Synopsys's Intel 18A certification (Feb-2024) was a prerequisite for Intel Foundry existing as a third-party option**; and the measurable scoreboard — **Intel Foundry ecosystem mentions rose from an 18-year average of 0-1/quarter to 6/quarter (2025Q1)**, named one of four 2026 EDA catalysts framed as *"whether a third foundry meaningfully expands EDA TAM or remains a perpetual aspiration."* Plus Intel sized as an EDA buyer: **Synopsys's largest customer for 20+ years — 17.9% of Synopsys revenue at the FY2017 peak, 12.6% ($772M) FY2024, below 10% FY2025** (the last mostly Ansys diluting the denominator, not Intel cutting spend); and the **1990s-2000s internal-EDA lesson** Intel itself learned (built internal P&R, abandoned it at 10nm+ as complexity outpaced internal software teams — same for Samsung; TSMC never tried). **Bear side:** **Cadence is gaining traction at Intel, historically a Synopsys stronghold**, because *"Intel Foundry's restructuring and leadership turnover have created evaluation opportunities across the EDA stack that did not previously exist"* and *"every foundry transition opens a window for competitive re-evaluation, and Intel's current transformation is the largest such window in a decade"* — framed here as a risk (the flow ecosystem is being re-qualified precisely while Intel needs external commitments), with the concrete casualty that **Synopsys "lacked foundry-ready IP for Intel 18A during the critical ramp window, missing early design wins that went to Cadence"**; plus the governance footnote that **Tan explored returning to Cadence in 2024, the board confirmed Devgan, and Tan went to Intel**. **New Catalyst:** track EDA-vendor Intel-Foundry mention counts as a third-party external-design-activity scoreboard, and watch whether **Synopsys closes its foundry-ready-IP gap by the 14A 0.9 PDK (October 2026)** — a full silicon-proven IP catalogue from *both* vendors is a precondition for external 14A tape-outs.
  - **No intra-quarter row added for either source.** The live window is the fresh **Q2'26 window opened at the 07-23 print**, and the Q1 FY26 window (Apr 23 → Jul 22) is archived/closed — so neither the 05-21 nor the 06-14 date is covered by an open window; both are folded as dated structural context in Debate/Catalysts per the run's backfill convention. High-NA "done right" can be a real **differentiator/leapfrog** for Intel (and Samsung) vs TSMC: ~75% fewer process steps, relaxed design rules, lower parasitic capacitance/better RC. The feared half-field **stitching** problem proved "not as serious as we thought" and designers are open to it. Sam's team validated Intel's High-NA line as "healthy and ready to go" before his Sep-2025 retirement (BofA "litho expert", 2026-05-21).
