MS's framing is the cleanest bear case: packaging is low-risk, wafer foundry needs a cultural shift "we still have not seen") **· 07-28 UPGRADE TO THE PACKAGING LEG: Fubon — this page's anchor EMIB-T bear since 06-05 — reverses its conclusion to "Intel's supply is a MUST by 2028," on the arithmetic that Google's 12-15mn 2028 TPU target with a four-compute-die V9 (capacity consumption more than doubling vs 2027) cannot be met by TSMC alone. Not a yield capitulation: the 60-70% framing is retained and the "learning curve improvement" noise is explicitly still flagged; the gate is simply no longer decisive. It also supplies the first monthly EMIB capacity path on this page — 10-12k/month by end-2026, planned to double to 24-25k/month by end-2027 — and third-party confirmation that "a great portion of the new capex is earmarked for expanding advanced packaging capabilities such as EMIB-T" (CFO, via Fubon). Reconciliation item left open: a 24-25k/month 2027 exit implies ~290-300k packages/yr of run-rate capacity entering 2028, against UBS's 2.3mn-package 2028E — either a further large step is assumed or the two are on different bases. And the bear anchor is untouched: "must" is Google's arithmetic, not a disclosed Intel order, so there is still no named anchor customer.** **· 08-03 — THE YIELD MARK GETS A LAB-VS-PRODUCTION SPLIT, FROM THE SAME HOUSE, AND IT IS THE SHARPEST DATAPOINT ON THIS PAGE: Fubon's primary transcript says the widely-reported ~90% "is just from the LAB… the production rate is LOWER than that. I heard it is only around 75%." ⚠️ That contradicts the DIGITIMES "EMIB yields hit target" headline of the same morning and reframes UBS's ~90% (measured on MediaTek TPU v9 TRIAL production runs) as a trial rather than a production number. Yield picture to carry, with basis attached and NOT averaged: SUBSTRATE 50-60% (Unimicron ~50% initial target under Intel commercial protection; JPM ~60%) · PACKAGING ~90% lab/trial vs ~75% production (Fubon) · required bar "high 90%" for HPC (UBS) and the 95%+ gate Google reportedly set. The offsetting bull leg is CAPACITY, not yield: Intel "more aggressive… planning to DOUBLE the capacity in both 27 and 28," corroborated via Unimicron's capex and Ibiden's expected follow (Ibiden call 08-04 flagged as "another important indicator"). ⚠️ And Fubon states the causal chain explicitly — MediaTek's rising confidence is a response to Intel's CAPACITY aggression, not to its yield: "MediaTek becomes more confident is… they are seeing Intel's more aggressive capacity expansion." A capacity-based endorsement is a weaker foundation than a yield endorsement. The mirror of "Intel's supply is a must" is stated in the same call: "INTEL'S PRODUCTION IS THE BIGGEST RISK" to the TPU numbers, with a six-month learning-curve watch.** |

**🆕 Update 2026-08-17 — the packaging leg of the thesis got its best external datapoint and its worst measurement problem in the same sentence.** Mizuho's primary AI-server note says **"EMIB-T is seeing good yields >97% and real traction," with [[MEDIATEK]]'s TPU the FIRST HIGH-VOLUME EMIB-T CUSTOMER IN 2028E and TPUv9 potentially on EMIB-T** — which, taken at face value, would clear the **95%+ bar [[GOOG]] set** and that Fubon (07-08) judged "very challenging." 🔴 **But the note never says which level the >97% is measured at, and that is decisive: four independent sources bracket EMIB-T SUBSTRATE yield at ~50–60% while putting PACKAGE/chip-on-substrate yield at ~90–92%. So >97% is either an incremental improvement on the package step (interesting, not thesis-changing, and silent on the binding constraint) or a refutation of four sources (thesis-changing). Both readings stay on the page; nothing is netted, and the Google gate is NOT declared cleared.** Net-new and useful regardless of the yield question: **CoPoS/EMIB-T in 2028E unlocks 8–12x reticle "or a 5–10x increase in ASIC outs" vs CoWoS-L's 5.5x today** — the first output-denominated version of the reticle ladder on this page, and the denominator that actually converts packaging into Foundry revenue. The **$109 PT is a reiteration**, which retroactively firms up the un-rationalised 08-09 cut from $135.

**Síntese do quarter:** one day into the new window the operating debate is already settled — nobody is contesting the beat, and estimates went up **20-40% at every house that published** (MS CY26 EPS $1.19→$1.57; BofA CY26/27/28 +20-40%; UBS C27/28 to $2.17/$2.82; JPM "materially higher") — yet **only MS moved its PT and no one changed a rating**. The fault line has migrated cleanly from *can Intel execute* to **who funds the capex**: FY26 >$20B (tooling +40% y/y) with CY27 "significantly above" (Street $26-30bn), management conceding 2027 FCF is "more challenged" and that it "may need to tap the capital markets," against a share count already ~5.1B vs ~4.4B a year ago. Bernstein's "**eerily similar**" and MS's "**running free cash negative during the biggest server inflection in history**" are the two lines that define the bear case now. The bull offset is that the 14A HVM commitment is itself circumstantial evidence of an anchor customer (UBS: "implies… a firm commitment from a customer interest group including AAPL or Google"), and DCAI is now the largest profit pool (+59% y/y, OpInc $2.5B > Client for the first time) with ASICs at a ~$2B run-rate heading to $4B. Watch item into January: **the formal CY27 capex number**, which is when the funding question stops being rhetorical. **⚠️ UPDATE (08-03) — a SECOND fault line has opened alongside the funding one, and it is the packaging leg the bull case leans on most. Intel IR's own framing on 08-03 is that the next 18 months of share is "more about GETTING SUPPLY, WAFERS AND SUBSTRATES vs roadmap competitiveness" — i.e. the constraint is supply, which the [[AMD]] read-across (+~20% q/q server volume vs Intel's +9% with +10% pricing) shows converting into share transfer. On EMIB-T the picture has become genuinely two-sided within a single source: Fubon says Intel is "more aggressive… planning to DOUBLE the capacity in both 27 and 28" (corroborated through Unimicron's capex and Ibiden's expected follow, with the Ibiden call on 08-04 as the near-dated indicator) while simultaneously calling the reported ~90% yield a LAB figure against a production rate "only around 75%." Both statements come from the analyst who is most bullish on EMIB-T. The synthesis this page should carry: Intel's EMIB is now the SWING FACTOR for the entire [[GOOG]]/[[MEDIATEK]] TPU ramp — "Intel's supply is a must by 2028" and "Intel's production is the biggest risk" are the same sentence — and the customer's stated confidence rests on Intel's CAPACITY aggression rather than on its yield. Watch order for the next six months: Ibiden capex (08-04) → the production-yield learning curve → the formal January CY27 capex number.** **⚠️ UPDATE (08-12, primary transcripts of the 07-24 BofA client call and the 07-28 Intel-IR call now on file) — three refinements, none of which move the two fault lines but all of which change how they should be modelled. ① THE FUNDING GAP IS WIDER THAN THE HEADLINE: the $20bn 2026 capex is GROSS of a ~$5-6bn Brookfield contribution that "GOES AWAY" in 2027, so Intel's own cash outlay steps up by that delta before the number itself grows — and IR's rebuttal is not a funding plan but a duration argument (Intel 7/10 now generating 5-6x its book carrying value; "argue against using that capex-to-rev ratio"). ② THE FOUNDRY BREAK-EVEN GATE IS NOVA LAKE, NOT THE ANCHOR CUSTOMER: 18A currently carries only Panther Lake, a notebook-only part, and IR is explicit that the internal glide path to break-even comes from broadening 18A across the product line — while the external win PUSHES break-even out. That inverts the way this page has been reading the "no named customer" wait: the customer announcement is a revenue event and a break-even DELAY, simultaneously. ③ THE MARGIN BEAT DOES NOT REPEAT MECHANICALLY: Q2's beat was volume + like-for-like pricing + better yields, and the client inventory write-downs were taken to free capacity for server and are GONE ("we wrote them down to that") — so the "write-downs reverse into Q3" argument is closed off, with Intel 3/18A still dilutive and growing as a share of mix into Q3. Net: the bull case gets a better ASP map (Intel ~$10/core vs [[AMD]] ~$20/core; client ASP +27% y/y on units −8%, i.e. price/mix not channel-stuffing) and a stated asymmetry from its most bullish house ("$10 down and $50 to $100 up" at $100), while the bear case gets a bigger cash hole and a later break-even.** **⚠️ UPDATE (08-12) — the "no one changed a rating, only MS moved its PT" line above needs amending: BofA has now moved its PT, and DOWNWARD — Buy maintained, PO $160 → $145 — inside a note that RAISES the industry CY30E server-CPU TAM 24% to $210.6bn and raises Intel's own CY30E server-CPU dollars to $46.3bn (from ~$40bn+). The cut is entirely in the valuation frame (31x → 29x CY30E EPS power, "$6.24" → "$6+", 10.7x → 8.9x 2028E EV/S), not in the operating model. That is a third, quieter fault line for this page: with the stock at ~$101 against a $145 PO, the most bullish major house has begun taking multiple out of a name whose fundamental trajectory it just upgraded — and the Q2'26 evidence it cites (server value share +200bp QoQ to 36.3% on ASP +21.6% QoQ, unit share −140bp) is itself a mix/price story that its own model does not extend (ASP CAGR +11.3% CY26-30). Watch whether the January CY27 capex number lands against a Street that is now marking multiple rather than estimates.**

**🆕 Síntese — addendum 2026-08-20 (MS "AI Sovereignty", 08-19): the cleanest statement yet of the gap between Intel's thematic position and Intel's rated upside, and it comes from the house with the only EW on the page.** MS writes a 73-page structural case that AI sovereignty drives domestic fab demand, discusses Intel across the US-restrictions, China-localization and domestic-fab sections rather than only in the screen — and then rates it **Equal-weight with a $84 target against a $100.95 price, a −17% base case**. It is also the **widest-dispersion name in the note's entire semis cohort: bull +36%, bear −50%**, an 86-point spread and the only one of the nine names on this wiki where the bear case is a halving. ➤ **The right way to carry this: thematic centrality is not the same as underwriting the equity. MS agrees with the demand argument this page has been building (US$860bn of US AI capex this year, a call for further federal support for the build-out, and "a larger and longer-lasting global AI capex cycle" even under tighter controls) and still thinks the distribution around Intel is binary rather than skewed. That is a sharper articulation of the page's own "the move is sentiment, not yet earnings" line than the sell-side has managed since the print — and it is consistent with the fault line the synthesis above already identifies, because a −50% bear case is a funding-and-execution bear case, not a demand one.** ✅ **Nothing was superseded: EW / $84 is the same mark MS set on 07-24, confirmed three and a half weeks later.** ⚠️ **Also recorded from the same note, because it is the mechanism most directly aimed at Intel's China exposure: China's "safe and reliable" domestic-technology procurement standards are ALREADY being used to phase foreign processors, OS and database products out of government computers and servers, alongside cybersecurity review (precedent: the 2023 Micron review) and MOFCOM's Unreliable Entity List — with a state-procurement rule that evaluates a qualifying "local" product as if its price were 20% lower.** **🆕 Síntese — addendum 2026-08-25.** Two things changed this week and they pull in the same direction on the FRANCHISE while leaving the EQUITY debate exactly where it was. **First, the capex story acquired an independent number from the people who sell the tools rather than the people who rate the stock: MS's semicap team raised its 2026/27/28 WFE forecast for the fifth time this year and named "Intel spending of $20bn+" as one of three reasons its bull case became its base case, modelling Intel at $20.5bn of 2027 leading-logic WFE against TSMC's $43.3bn — Intel as the unambiguous No.2 leading-logic spender, and included by name in both the 2nm and 1.4nm capacity adds.** **Second, the packaging leg got its most durable phrasing yet: MS Asia writes that TPU v10 "CONTINUES to use Intel EMIB-T", modelling EMIB-T forward into a 1.4nm Google generation on the strength of a reticle expanding past 12 from ~9.** ⚠️ **Neither is an equity mark, and both must be read with their limits: the $20.5bn is a WFE subset, not total capex, and is a forecast of Intel executing on 14A — the very thing `## Risks` still treats as uncommitted; the EMIB-T line is an Asia supply-chain check with no revenue, wafer or unit number attached, and MS Asia simultaneously models Intel's Habana turnkey revenue at Alchip to zero from 2026. **The honest synthesis is unchanged from 08-20: Intel's THEMATIC position keeps strengthening on evidence from third parties with no axe on the stock, while the rated upside does not — and the gap between those two is now this page's central tension rather than a footnote.**

That is a more precise version of the "improving but still skeptical / the move is sentiment, not yet earnings" framing already in `## Current state`.** **The macro frame behind it, and it is the constructive half: MS's house AI-capex figure is **US$860bn "in the US this year alone"**, cited twice, paired with a call for **further federal support to facilitate the $860bn AI infrastructure build-out** and a continuation of US-China *"de-risking"* via tighter controls. ⚠️ **SCOPE FLAG, recorded so this does not get chained into a series: the $860bn is a US figure. MS's earlier "Power Struggle" (2026-07-14) put AI capex at $877bn in 2026 on a GLOBAL basis. These are different scopes, not a revision — do not net them or read a $17bn "cut."** MS's structural case: technology restrictions create winners and losers across the stack, but *"the broader outcome is a larger and longer-lasting global AI capex cycle"*; moving to a multipolar AI ecosystem *"drives a larger global TAM, in particular in the US and China"*; and sovereign capacity requires *"additional domestic and regional data centers, greater redundancy, more power generation and transmission, localized cloud and networking infrastructure, and in some cases, parallel technology stacks."* Base case on controls is *"a middle path that protects frontier US IP and limits designated Chinese technologies while preserving enough openness to support innovation and model adoption."* **The China-localization sections are the other side of the same coin and are unusually well quantified: Politburo "Six Networks" (六张网) directive (Apr-2026) elevating the compute network to national strategic infrastructure, *"same tier as water, power, logistics, communications, urban underground"*; State Council operationalisation (May-2026) with an NDRC briefing at **>RMB 35 trillion of Six Networks capex in the 15th Five-Year Plan (2026-2030), of which >RMB 1 trillion is compute**; "Implementation Opinions on Intelligent Agents" (CAC + NDRC + MIIT, May-2026) — the world's first dedicated AI-agent framework, with 3 decision-authority tiers, 19 priority scenarios and **open-source & domestic-chip compatibility requirements**; the Aug-2025 "AI Plus" initiative (Guofa [2025] No.11) targeting **>70% penetration by 2027 and >90% by 2030**; a National AI Fund of RMB 60bn (~US$8.2bn) plus RMB 138bn of local funds and Hangzhou at up to RMB 300bn/yr for the "Six Little Dragons" (DeepSeek, Zhipu, MiniMax, Moonshot); and **government procurement that evaluates a qualifying "local" product as if its price were 20% lower** than a non-domestic alternative. China's toolkit against foreign providers: cybersecurity review (precedent cited: the **2023 review of Micron**), "safe and reliable" domestic-technology procurement standards **already used to phase foreign processors, OS and database products out of government computers and servers** — the most directly Intel-relevant line in the China section — and MOFCOM's **Unreliable Entity List**. MS's read is **regulatory localisation and effective exclusion of unapproved foreign models rather than an explicit ban**, and its strategic conclusion is that *"China's AI infrastructure cycle is no longer only a technology theme, but also a state-backed macro, earnings, and capital-market allocation theme,"* with policy support *"most capital-intensive and security-focused at the hardware and infrastructure layers, where China seeks greater self-reliance in chips, computing systems, and enabling software."*** ⚠️ **ROUTING CAVEAT, RECORDED ONCE FOR THE WHOLE NOTE AND FILED HERE BECAUSE INTEL IS ITS MOST BODY-PRESENT NAME: the ingest router proposed 33 tickers for this note, of which 13 — DELL, PANW, WMB, CRM, LITE, CRWD, COHR, NOW, SHOP, ANET, CSCO, HPE and TEL — appear ONLY in the p62-64 "Other companies mentioned" boilerplate list and were DROPPED. The nine names folded across this wiki ([[INTC]], [[TXN]], [[SAMSUNG]], [[SMIC]], [[AMAT]], [[LRCX]], [[KLAC]], [[TER]], [[TOKYOELEC]]) are the ones with genuine body or ratings-screen presence. Note also that "TEL" in the US wiki is TE Connectivity, not Tokyo Electron — a second reason that particular router hit was wrong.** (Morgan Stanley, 2026-08-19) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/USELECTION_20260819_1049.html) |

| **08-12** | **⚠️ BofA · Vivek Arya (w/ Duksan Jang, Michael Mani, Liam Pharr) — "Rise of the agents: raising CPU TAM (again) to $210bn" · BUY MAINTAINED, ▼PO $160 → $145 (Price Objective Change; price $100.95)** | **valuation / demanda** | **mixed** | **A PO CUT INSIDE A TAM RAISE — the combination is the story, and it is the first PT move on this page since MS's.** ★ **The TAM: CY30E server-CPU TAM to $210bn+ from ~$170bn, "nearly 5x growth off the ~$35bn CY25 level, and still 2.5x growth off a strong CY26E base," +36% CAGR vs +30% prior — because "agentic inference is pushing the CPU to GPU ratio from ~1:4 in the training era to, ~1:2 for AI inference, and ultimately toward ~1:1 for agentic AI, as CPUs become the orchestration control plane." CPUs become ~10% of the ~$2.2Tn CY30 DC systems TAM vs ~7% in the CY24-25 training era; AI CPUs are ~$180bn of the $210bn, split ~50/50 between compute/head nodes (~$90bn) and standalone agentic CPU racks (~$90bn).** ⚠️ **BofA answers the substitution-vs-expansion debate in favour of the incumbents: CPUs are "additive to overall system TAM… alongside (not instead of) XPUs," which "lifts both x86 incumbents and ARM challengers" — evidence cited is near-record GPU rental and memory spot prices, not model output.** **THE INTC LINE: revenue $14.1bn CY25 → $20.8bn CY26E → $27.8bn CY27E → $33.6bn CY28E → $46.3bn CY30E (+22.2% CAGR CY26-30); units 18.5mn → 20.2mn → 29.3mn (+9.7% CAGR); ASP $761 → $1,028 → $1,579 (+11.3% CAGR); value share 40.4% CY25 → 33.9% CY26E → 22.0% CY30E, while UNIT share stays the largest at ~36% CY30.** Workload split of the CY30E $46.3bn: **traditional/IaaS $19.1bn (+12.4% CAGR), AI compute/head node $13.6bn, agentic AI node $13.6bn** (the agentic line is $0 before CY26 and $1.3bn in CY26E — the fastest-growing thing Intel sells, at +79% CAGR off nothing). **Verbatim on the trade-off: "we see share declining to just ~22% of total server CPU value by CY30 from ~34% in CY26, though we expect INTC's continued relative strength in traditional enterprise workloads. However, we see continued $ revenue growth of +20% CAGR between CY26-30… as INTC navigates through the industry-wide supply shortages, benefits from ASP expansion. By CY28, 18A-P-based Coral Rapids should ramp and could begin to narrow the performance gap vs competition."** **Why still Buy: "We also like INTC given its foundry optionality, incremental Q3 supply ramping into Q4, and gen-on-gen ASP uplift."** **Q2'26 Mercury evidence, and it is Intel's best pricing quarter of the cycle: server sales $5.2bn +29% QoQ (vs AMD +21%, ARM est. +17%) on "strong ASP gains from newer Granite Rapids mix shift" — server ASP $1,042, +21.6% QoQ / +50.3% YoY; server value share 36.3% (+200bp QoQ, −400bp YoY) on unit share 53.9% (−140bp QoQ). Notebooks: units +15% QoQ (AMD +18%, ARM +18%) as "INTC mostly caught up to AMD's growth as it improved availability of its 18A supplies," ASP +10% QoQ to $163.1 (+34.2% YoY) — "INTC ASPs back above AMD's" — value share 61.6% (+120bp QoQ, −550bp YoY). Desktops: units −10.7% QoQ but value share +110bp QoQ to 59.4%.** ⚠️ **Product spec, unflattering where it matters for agentic racks: Granite Rapids 128C/256T (Intel 3, 3Q24, 3.9GHz) and Diamond Rapids 192C (Intel 18A, 3Q26) vs AMD Venice 256C/512T — BofA's modeled rack-level geomean has Xeon 6980P at 1.46x [[NVDA]] Vera vs AMD Turin 2.37x and Venice 3.30x.** 🔴 **THE PO: $145 = 29x CY30E EPS power of "$6+", discounted back two years, implying 8.9x total-company 2028E EV/S — "well above historical 1.7x-4x range but we view as appropriate given increasing server CPU and external foundry opportunity, offset by near-/medium-term manufacturing ramp-up uncertainties." Down from $160 on 31x/$6.24/10.7x. Upside risks: external foundry packaging/wafer deals, better 18A/14A yields, a stronger PC market (Win10 refresh/AI uplift), geopolitics favouring domestic manufacturing. Downside risks: lower yield/ramp at Intel Foundry on 18A/14A, no material external wafer customer, weak mature PC market, accelerated CPU share loss.** (BofA · Vivek Arya, 2026-08-12) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Vivek_on_CPU.html) |
| 08-10 | **Bernstein · Mark Li / Edward Hou / Yipin Cai — "TSMC: Higher revenue & capex on CPU" (PRIMARY, 19pp; [[TSM]] note, read-through only)** | competicao | **mixed** | **No INTC rating or PT — Intel appears as a TSMC CUSTOMER and as a packaging COMPETITOR, and the two readings point opposite ways.** 🔴 **① THE OUTSOURCING DATAPOINT, and it is the sharpest sizing of Intel-at-TSMC on the wiki: Intel was 10-15% of TSMC's CPU revenue in 2023 and is now "a close No. 2" at MID-30s% of it in 2025, second only to [[AMD]] at 35-40% — on a TSMC CPU book that grew from US$6-7bn (2023) to US$15-16bn (2025). That implies roughly US$5-6bn of Intel silicon at TSMC in 2025, up from ~US$1bn in 2023.** ⚠️ **Bernstein's framing of the sequence is unflattering and is carried as stated: the second wave of TSMC's CPU business began "in 2023 [when] Intel's outsourcing to TSMC kicked in… as COMPETITIVE PRESSURE FORCED INTEL to take advantage of TSMC's capacity too," following a first wave in which TSMC's technology and cost "help[ed] these customers GAIN SHARE FROM INTEL in the CPU market." And it does not stop here: "we believe SOME OF INTEL['S] UPCOMING CPU NOVA LAKE WILL BE ON TSMC N2 TOO" — the leading-edge outsourcing extends into the N2 generation, alongside [[AAPL]]'s A20 and Qualcomm's Snapdragon 8 Elite Gen 6.** ➤ **Hold this against the 14A/18A internal-node case on this page: it is the clearest measure of how much leading-edge product Intel is currently choosing NOT to run internally, and it is growing.** **② THE OTHER SIDE — EMIB-T IS NOW IN A MAJOR SELL-SIDE CAPACITY MODEL AS REAL SUPPLY, NOT A THREAT: Bernstein extends its CoWoS model "to also model Intel's EMIB-T capacity by converting the panel-based capacity of EMIB-T to the equivalent CoWoS capacity measured in wafers," putting Intel at 0 / 0 / 25k / 100k wpm CoWoS-equivalent for CY25 / 26E / 27E / 28E (chart read) — against TSMC 60 → 130 → 160 → 170k and ASE+Amkor 5 → 30 → 60 → 100k. By 2028E Intel is ~27% of the CoWoS-equivalent pool on these numbers, level with the two established OSATs combined.** **The trigger is dated: "Intel packaging [[GOOG]]'s TPU v9 with EMIB-T will be another example STARTING LATE 2027 and MORE MEANINGFULLY IN 2028."** 🔴 **And the strategic reading is the most favourable Intel has had on this page: Bernstein says TSMC is NOT contesting it — "TSMC is still the best CoWoS supplier, but Amkor offering similar services earlier proves this doesn't have a long-term defendable moat. TSMC thus won't build capacity to defend share." On that view Intel's packaging entry is not a share fight against a defending incumbent; it is an opening the incumbent is deliberately leaving. ⚠️ The corollary is priced in too: "the margins of CoWoS will be structurally lower." Intel would be entering a business whose economics are being reset downward by its own entry plus ASE's, Amkor's and [[SAMSUNG]]'s.** (Bernstein · Mark Li / Edward Hou / Yipin Cai, "TSMC: Higher revenue & capex on CPU; Outperform with 40% upside", 2026-08-10) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/BERN_259272.html) |

| 08-03 | **UBS IR Call Series — John Pitzer (VP/IR), hosted by Tim Arcuri (sales notes via Robert Ruple)** | guidance | **mixed** | 🔴 **FIRST-PARTY IR COLOUR ON EVERY OPEN ITEM ON THIS PAGE, AND ONE LINE RESETS THE COMPETITIVE FRAME. On share: "NEXT 1.5YR MARKET SHARE [IS] MORE ABOUT GETTING SUPPLY, WAFERS AND SUBSTRATES VS ROADMAP COMPETITIVENESS."** ⚠️ **That is Intel IR conceding, in its own words, that the constraint is supply — which is exactly the mechanism behind Jefferies' 08-02 read-across (Intel printed +9% q/q server volume with pricing +10% while [[AMD]] is expected at ~+20% "after capacity constrained INTC"). Two independent sides of the same trade now agree: in a shortage, Intel's units go to the competitor even when Intel wins on price. Do not net this against the roadmap optimism below — Pitzer is saying the roadmap is not the binding constraint, which cuts both ways.** **On the nodes, and it is more confident than the page has carried: "18A CONTINUE TO BEAT and 14A progressing well — pleased by performance and yield; TRACKING CLOSE TO 22NM NODE PERFORMANCE." 14A ramp "start ramping in '28 and more of the revenue in '29." Demand framing: "THIS IS THE TIGHTEST CYCLICAL ENVIRONMENT THEY HAVE EVER SEEN."** **On the no-named-customer bear anchor — reaffirmed as policy, not absence: "Their philosophy won't talk about wafer customers… focused on execution with customers and feel confident not enough capacity and need for customer choice for Foundry. Focused on 5-10 yr roadmap and building trust SO WON'T ANNOUNCE CUSTOMER NAMES."** **On EMIB: "Like the technology and getting GREAT VALIDATION WITH STRONG CUSTOMER INTEREST… an important new addition from SK Hynix to run the division… PREPARING FOR A HEALTHY RAMP."** **On Foundry break-even — NO CHANGE, and the loss is quantified: "Run-rating $8B LOSS"; B/E gets PUSHED OUT when they win external customers because those bring incremental capex and opex ("will do what they think is right").** ⚠️ **That is the honest version of the bull case's cost: winning the anchor customer this page keeps waiting for MOVES BREAK-EVEN FURTHER OUT, not closer.** **On gross margin: "up very modestly sequentially, but still mix driven. Intel 3 and 18A are doing BETTER THAN INTERNAL TARGET, BUT STILL BELOW INTERNAL GM TARGETS — creates a headwind to overcome"; the three new businesses (ASIC, Packaging, Wafers) carry different GM profiles; guidance philosophy "starting with 40s and then move up." New framework disclosed: "RULE OF 45 — growth rate + OPM needs to be 45%."** **On the funding question that is this page's live bear anchor: capex is "SKEWED TOWARDS EQUIPMENT vs brick/mortar this year — getting a place in line for tools and accelerated clean room capacity," normalising later; the gross-vs-net capex delta is the INVESTMENT TAX CREDIT (backable out of the filings); Brookfield inflows this year "become outflows"; and on a raise — "LipBu is financially disciplined and WOULD NOT TAKE ANY STRATEGY OFF THE TABLE."** ⚠️ **That is not a denial. Read against management's own Q2 concession that 2027 FCF is "more challenged" and it "may need to tap the capital markets."** **Analyst Day: "committed to have one, BUT NO DATE YET" — wants more hiring done and to see the leadership team for a quarter first.** Products: Nova Lake feels good on performance/cost, vPro activation +1,500% in enterprise since Q3; **server roadmap "more work to do," Granite a fast ramp, "DIAMOND MULTI-THREADING [was] A MISTAKE" though single-thread premium makes it more competitive for agentic, and Coral "gives them the opportunity to close the gap."** ASIC framed as **"a TRUE DESIGN SERVICES business… probably not appropriate to sit with Intel Products."** (UBS Sector Sales · Robert Ruple, IR call hosted by Timothy Arcuri, 2026-08-03) |

Unimicron already announced it is increasing substrate capacity for this so we should hear some positive things from Ibiden too."** **(2) NET-NEW AND IT IS THE COMPETITIVE-MOAT POINT: "TSM also working on their IN-HOUSE EMIB SOLUTION for different chip designs — but still VERY EARLY STAGE, NO PRODUCTION TIMELINE YET."** ⚠️ **[[TSM]] is building a response to EMIB-T. It is far enough out to be irrelevant to the 2028 TPU decision, but it caps the duration of the window, and it is the first evidence on this page that the incumbent considers the technology worth copying rather than ignoring.** **(3) The causal explanation for why Google went to Intel at all, from the trade press: "Google turned to Intel for EMIB packaging BECAUSE NVIDIA PRE-BOOKED OVER 50% OF TSMC'S COWOS PACKAGING FOR THE NEXT 3 YEARS" (@dnystedt relaying media, 2026-08-02, 506 likes).** That reframes the EMIB-T win as a **capacity-displacement** outcome rather than a technology-preference outcome — which is more durable than a yield race but less durable than a technical moat. **(4) DIGITIMES headline the same morning: "INTEL EMIB YIELDS HIT TARGET, MediaTek eyes 2028 ASIC ramp," with [[MEDIATEK]] confirming on its call that the second-gen DC ASIC "remains on schedule for mass production in 2028" (DIGITIMES Daily Picks, 2026-08-03).** ⚠️ **"Yields hit target" is a headline, not a disclosed number — hold it against the 50-60% substrate yield bracket this page carries from Unimicron and JPM, and against Pitzer's own more guarded "doing the work to ramp to high quality/volume."** **(5) @dnystedt also relays that EMIB-T "wins ASIC project and the technology is making good progress for 2028 mass production," quoting UBS's Sunny Lin pressing Intel on the call about "the SECOND CLOUD ASIC PROJECT" and whether early 2028 is the right frame (2026-08-03).** Cross-file [[MEDIATEK]], [[GOOG]], [[TSM]], [[NVDA]]. |
| 08-03 | **Fubon · Sherman Shang / Arthur (hosted by Jefferies) — "industry highlights & checks update" (PRIMARY TRANSCRIPT of the call relayed in the row above)** | supply | **mixed** | 🔴 **THE PRIMARY SUPPLIES THE YIELD NUMBER THE DESK RELAY DID NOT, AND IT IS WORSE THAN THE BULL HEADLINE — verbatim: "although there is still a lot of noise on Intel's production rate, I think some reports are talking about the production rate has already improved to 90%, but I DON'T THINK THAT IS A REAL NUMBER. I heard the 90% rate is IT'S JUST FROM THE LAB, it's just from the lab. The production rate is LOWER than that. I heard it is ONLY AROUND 75%. So they are still trying to improve the production rate."** ⚠️ **This is the sharpest yield datapoint in the call and it does three things to this page. (a) It DIRECTLY CONTRADICTS the DIGITIMES "Intel EMIB yields hit target" headline logged in the row above, from an analyst who was on the phone the same morning. (b) It reframes the ~90% figure this page has carried since UBS's 07-27 note ("~90% EMIB-T PACKAGING yield, based on trial production runs of MediaTek's TPU v9 prototype") as a LAB/trial mark rather than a production one — which is consistent with UBS's own framing (trial runs) but is a materially different thing from a production yield, and nobody had made the lab-vs-production distinction explicitly until now. (c) It leaves the yield picture as: EMIB-T SUBSTRATE 50-60% (Unimicron ~50% initial target with Intel commercial protection; JPM ~60%), EMIB-T PACKAGING ~90% in lab/trial but ~75% in production per Fubon, against UBS's stated HPC bar of "high 90%" and the 95%+ gate Google reportedly set. Log all three levels with their basis; do not average them.** ⚠️ **ASR caveat, flagged once for this page: machine transcript — `Fubon` (ASR: "Foobon") · `Ibiden` (ASR: "Epident") · `CoWoS` (ASR: "CoOS"/"COAS") · `SerDes` (ASR: "services") · `ASE` (ASR: "ASC"/"ASP") · `Broadcom` (ASR: "Borcom") · `SoIC` (ASR: "SOIC").** **THE OFFSETTING BULL LEG, and it is why MediaTek's confidence rose: "Intel is becoming MORE AGGRESSIVE on capacity expansion, planning to DOUBLE the capacity in BOTH 27 AND 28… they are very, very aggressive in expanding." Fubon corroborates through the substrate chain rather than through Intel: "Unimicron earlier during the earnings call, they are very aggressive about their CAPEX in the following year. And from our supply chain check, we also heard Ibiden is also very aggressive in expanding their substrate capacity to support Intel's production in the next two years." And the near-dated check: "Ibiden will host the earnings call TOMORROW, and we believe it is ANOTHER IMPORTANT INDICATOR FOR INTEL'S EMIB."** ⚠️ **Note the causal claim Fubon makes explicitly, which is the cleanest statement on this page of why the customer's confidence moved: "I think MediaTek becomes more confident is I think they are SEEING INTEL'S MORE AGGRESSIVE CAPACITY EXPANSION in the next two years" — i.e. [[MEDIATEK]]'s 07-31 "we are more committed to EMIB-T" is a response to CAPACITY, not to yield. That is a weaker foundation than a yield endorsement, and it should be read that way.** **THE STRATEGIC FRAME, restated from the primary: "TSMC, they don't have enough capacity to do that [Google's 12-15mn 2028 TPUs]… that's why I say INTEL'S IS A MUST HAVE IN 2028 to support Google."** **AND THE INVERSE, WHICH IS THE MOST IMPORTANT SENTENCE ON THIS PAGE TODAY: asked what could send MediaTek's business back to Broadcom given sell-side 2028 ASIC-revenue estimates spanning US$35-52bn — "I think INTEL'S PRODUCTION IS THE BIGGEST RISK. Because if Intel is unable to enter production, that means MediaTek, they need to go back to TSMC. And I don't think TSMC has the capacity to support Google to do 12 million to 15 million. So when that happens, we will probably see some potential downside for the numbers. But right now we still need to closely monitor INTEL'S EXPANSION AND PRODUCTION LEARNING CURVE IMPROVEMENT IN THE NEXT SIX MONTHS."** ⚠️ **So the same analyst holds both that Intel is a MUST and that Intel is the BIGGEST RISK — those are not in tension, they are the same statement: Intel's EMIB has become the single swing factor for the entire [[GOOG]]/[[MEDIATEK]] TPU ramp, which is a large external-revenue prize and a large systemic dependency at once.** **ONE MORE FIRST-PARTY-ADJACENT ITEM, net-new to this page and it cuts against the exclusivity read: MediaTek "officially say they will ONLY USE INTEL" for TPU V9 packaging — a named external commitment to Intel Foundry's advanced packaging, and the most concrete one on this page — BUT Fubon's own check says "that doesn't mean MediaTek is giving up TSMC's at all. They still have two advanced packaging technologies in their pocket. I mean both Intel's and TSMC's."** ⚠️ **Carry the official line and the check as two distinct claims. Read against [[TSM]] working on its own in-house EMIB (same call, "very, very early stage, no production timeline yet") the durability of Intel's position looks like a capacity window rather than a lock-in.** Cross-file [[MEDIATEK]], [[GOOG]], [[TSM]], [themes/cowos-packaging](themes/cowos-packaging.md). (Fubon · Sherman Shang / Arthur (hosted by Jefferies), 2026-08-03) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_08_03_sherman_update_03_08_26.html) |
| 08-02 | **@IntelProMUltra relaying UNIMICRON's Q2'26 earnings call — the EMIB-T substrate economics, disclosed** | supply | **mixed** | **★ THE FIRST LOOK AT THE MANUFACTURING ECONOMICS UNDER THE EMIB-T ECOSYSTEM, FROM A SUPPLIER'S OWN CALL RATHER THAN FROM INTEL. Four disclosures: "(1) The company expects EMIB-T SUBSTRATE MASS PRODUCTION TO BEGIN IN 2027. (2) The EMIB-T substrates will initially be supported by UNIMICRON AND 2 JAPANESE VENDORS (IBIDEN AND SHINKO ELECTRIC). All 3 vendors have worked with Intel as long-standing advanced-substrate partners for a very long time. (3) The INITIAL YIELD TARGET IS AROUND 50%. (4) More importantly, Unimicron indicated that INTEL WILL PROVIDE COMMERCIAL PROTECTION DURING THE EARLY RAMP, ALLOWING IT TO MAINTAIN REASONABLE PROFITABILITY IN THE LOW YIELDS."** ⚠️ **Point (4) is the one to sit with. Intel is underwriting its substrate suppliers' margins to get the ecosystem stood up — the relay's own read is that "this does not look like a conventional supplier-customer relationship; Intel appears to be COORDINATING the technology, supplier qualification [and] manufactur[ing]." Two ways to take it: (a) BULLISH — Intel is behaving like a platform owner building an ecosystem the way it did for ABF substrates, and it has the three incumbent advanced-substrate vendors committed; (b) BEARISH ON THE P&L — a ~50% initial substrate yield with vendor margin guarantees means Intel absorbs the cost of the learning curve, and that cost sits inside Foundry gross margin in exactly the 2027 window where the turnaround case needs GM expansion. The page should carry both; neither is contradicted by the disclosure.** ⚠️ **THE YIELD NUMBER IS THE CROSS-CHECK THAT MATTERS. J.P. Morgan's supply-chain checks the same weekend put EMIB-T at "~60% currently at SUBSTRATE level and ~90% at CHIP-ON-SUBSTRATE level" (JPM · Hariharan on [[MEDIATEK]], 2026-08-02). So two independent sources now bracket EMIB-T SUBSTRATE yield at 50-60% today — and only one of them mentions that the economics are Intel-subsidised. JPM treats the yield question as the cleared obstacle; Unimicron's own disclosure suggests it is cleared because Intel is paying for it to be. Anyone reading the "EMIB-T yield is fine" headline should carry the subsidy alongside it.** ⚠️ **TIMING TENSION, unresolved: Unimicron says substrate MASS PRODUCTION IN 2027, while MediaTek's management (via Morgan Stanley, 2026-08-01) dates EMIB-T mass production to 2028 with TSMC CoWoS/CoPoS as the named back-up. Those are reconcilable if the substrate ramps a year ahead of the packaged part, which is the normal sequence — but this page has also carried Unimicron saying on 07-31 that EMIB-T is "UNLIKELY TO REACH FULL MASS PRODUCTION BEFORE 2027," so the vendor is now the source of both the earlier and the later date. Treat 2027 as substrate MP and 2028 as customer HVM until a single source states both.** **The demand-side positive stands: MediaTek's "we are more committed to EMIB-T" (07-31) plus three qualified substrate vendors plus a 2027 substrate ramp is the most concrete external validation of an Intel packaging technology in the period this page covers, and Unimicron expects customers "to stay on EMIB-T for THREE PRODUCT GENERATIONS."** See [[MEDIATEK]], [[TSM]], [themes/cowos-packaging](themes/cowos-packaging.md). (@IntelProMUltra, 2026-08-01, 49 likes — FinTwit relay of a public earnings call; the underlying call is the primary source and is NOT independently archived on this wiki.) |
| 08-02 | Jefferies · Blayne Curtis (via the [[AMD]] preview) | competicao | **bear** | **A read-across that arrives as somebody else's bull case: Jefferies expects AMD server volume +~20% q/q in June "AFTER CAPACITY CONSTRAINED INTC PRINTED 9% IN 2Q26 WITH PRICING UP 10%+ ON MIX DYNAMICS"** (2026-08-02). ⚠️ **Two facts inside one sentence and they point opposite ways: Intel got 10%+ pricing on mix — genuinely good, and consistent with a server market where supply is short — but it converted only 9% q/q of volume growth while a competitor is expected to do ~20% because Intel could not supply more. In a shortage, "capacity constrained" is a market-share transfer, not a neutral fact. This is the cleanest current evidence that the 2026 server CPU up-cycle is being monetised more by [[AMD]] than by Intel on units, even where Intel wins on price.** |

And there's been **some success in terms of their ability to bring technology online**… this was kind of inevitable that you would see a broadening."* He ties it directly to the setup for 2027. **Why this is worth carrying on the Intel page.** The customer-broadening thesis has mostly been argued *about* Intel by Intel bulls; here the leading process-control vendor is attributing a **raised multi-year outlook** in part to leading-edge logic broadening beyond TSMC, and confirming *"some success"* in bringing that technology online — a supplier's revenue-backed judgement rather than a narrative. It also corroborates MS's standing framing on the KLAC page that **KLA is "the way to play Intel" in US semicap** because this year's Intel spend is concentrated in process control (MS, 2026-06-22). Same call, adjacent: KLA cites *"one of the large foundry customers last week"* raising capex partly on **equipment price increases** — a reminder that a 14A pull-in is being funded into a rising equipment-cost environment. (KLA Corp Q4 FY26 earnings call — Bloomberg FINAL TRANSCRIPT, 2026-07-28) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/20260728_KLA_Corp-_Earnings_Call_2026-7-28_RT000000003114277744pdf.html) |
| 08-01 | **UBS · Timothy Arcuri (ENTG preview) + David Vogt (PC pricing) — two independent Intel read-throughs from the supplier side** | produto | **bull** | **★ ① THE MATERIALS-SUPPLIER VIEW OF INTEL FOUNDRY, and it is a stronger endorsement than anything the sell-side has said about the customer book: in Arcuri's Entegris preview, "ENTG also highlighted INTEL FOUNDRY AS AN INCREMENTAL OPPORTUNITY, noting INTC's TAM AND ECOSYSTEM ARE COMPARABLE IN SCALE TO THOSE OF [[TSM]]."** ⚠️ **Read who is saying it and why that matters. Entegris sells consumables per wafer start — its revenue is a function of actual production volume, not of announced design wins — so a materials supplier treating Intel Foundry as a TSMC-scale ecosystem opportunity is a claim about expected WAFER STARTS, which is the metric the external-customer debate on this page has lacked. It is also a supplier positioning statement rather than an audited forecast, and Entegris has an obvious interest in a second leading-edge customer base existing.** Context: ENTG management now refers to a potential "DOUBLING" of the business, which is informing its manufacturing-footprint consolidation; UBS Buy, PT $205, sentiment 7.5/10 into the 08-04 BMO print. **② PC ASPs CONFIRM THE PRICING PASS-THROUGH: "Intel called out ASPs UP ~27% YoY in the qtr, IN-LINE WITH OEM PRICING ACTIONS"** (UBS · David Vogt, "Intel PC unit and pricing comments are consistent with our expectations", 2026-08-01). ⚠️ **A +27% y/y client ASP is a very large number and it is the mirror image of the memory-cost squeeze showing up on [[AAPL]]'s gross margin and in [[AMD]]'s below-Street Client revenue — Intel is capturing price where AMD is being modelled flat-to-down on Desktop ASP (−5% q/q in UBS's own Sept model). Whether that is share-neutral pricing discipline across the x86 duopoly or Intel pricing ahead of a share loss is the open question; Vogt frames it as simply consistent with OEM actions, i.e. cost pass-through rather than mix.** **③ Supply-chain corroboration from the same week: Randy Abrams' APAC Tech Views — "Intel supply chain implications — UPSIDE FROM CPU AND PACKAGING… strong demand upside amid TIGHT SUPPLY," consistent with the supply-driven server-share framing John Pitzer gave on the 07-28 BofA-hosted IR call already on this page. Also logged from the Susquehanna weekly link flow: "Intel Cracks The Encapsulation Wall Blocking Hyper-Large Chips, Pushing Advanced Packaging To 24X RETICLE SIZE AND BEYOND" (press report, not company-confirmed — logged for follow-up alongside the EMIB-T mass-production-in-2028 date [[MEDIATEK]] gave on its call).** **UBS positioning tracker has INTC in BOTH the Semis long-biased AND debated buckets** — the same split it carried the prior week. (UBS · Timothy Arcuri / David Vogt / Randy Abrams, via Robert Ruple, 2026-08-01) |
| 07-30 | Bernstein · David Dai / Carmine Milano / Juho Hwang ("ASML: Best Idea 3Q26") | produto | **bull** | **Read-through only — an [[ASML]] Best-Idea note with no INTC rating or PT. But it contains the cleanest third-party statement on this page of what 18A's High-NA deployment means for the industry, and it is unambiguously favourable to Intel's process-leadership claim.** Verbatim: **"Intel's adoption of HNA in 18A this year (Intel Foundry is using HNA on the Intel 18A) confirms that the technology is ready for high-volume manufacturing and removes a key uncertainty around adoption."** Intel is the *only* company Bernstein credits with having validated High-NA in production — the note's adoption roadmap (Exhibit 10) has **logic HNA validated by Intel at 18A now, broader logic adoption at Intel A14 and Samsung SF1.4, and [[TSM]] not inserting broadly until ~A10 at the end of the decade**, having publicly called High-NA too expensive. That is a genuine, dated, first-mover process credential against the foundry incumbent, and it is being asserted by a house with no Intel axe (the note is a long-ASML piece). ⚠️ **Two honest limits.** First, the claim Bernstein is making is about *tool readiness*, not about Intel Foundry's yield, cost or customer traction — it says the technology works in HVM, not that 18A is winning business. Second, the same note flags **equipment price inflation as an industry-wide cost headwind Intel itself has acknowledged**: *"Recent commentary from both TSMC and Intel also appears to acknowledge ongoing equipment price inflation"* — with ASML modelling **EUV ASPs +~10% in 2027 and +7% in 2028** before any like-for-like hikes, and Bernstein arguing the like-for-like lever is live and under-modelled. Intel is therefore simultaneously the validation story and a payer of the resulting bill, which matters for a company whose capex discipline is the live investment debate. Offsetting mechanics worth carrying: HNA's economics work through **process simplification** — single-exposure HNA replaces LNA multi-patterning (SALELE), eliminating masks, litho-etch loops and cut steps, with ASML estimating **litho capex/opex ~1.2x against a decline in non-litho cost** — so the tool is expensive but the flow is cheaper. Bernstein also notes the strongest HNA economics are in **DRAM, not logic**, because smaller DRAM dies avoid stitching → higher throughput and lower cost per exposure; Intel's logic dies do require it. (Bernstein · David Dai, 2026-07-30) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/BERN_258544.html) |

| 07-28 | **Intel IR · John Pitzer (VP, Investor Relations) — PRIMARY TRANSCRIPT of the BofA-hosted post-2Q call (host: BofA · Vivek Arya). ⚠️ PARTIAL DUPLICATE of the BofA note in the row below — that row is the relay, this is the call itself. No figure in it is revised; the items below are the ones the summary could not carry** | guidance | **mixed** | ⚠️ **Speaker is INTEL MANAGEMENT (IR), not an analyst — read as company commentary, not as an estimate.** 🔴 **① THE NODE DISTINCTION THE SUMMARIES COLLAPSE, in IR's own words: "18A THE NODE WILL BE INTERNAL ONLY. 18A THE FAMILY, WHICH INCLUDES 18AP, WILL ABSOLUTELY BE AN EXTERNAL FOUNDRY NODE" — 1.0 PDK is out, in production, "you'll start to see volume ramps next year," and the change is customer-pulled: "CUSTOMERS KEPT PULLING US TO ENGAGE WITH THEM ON INTEL 18AP" versus Lip-Bu's early-tenure position that the whole 18A family would be internal. On 14A the same reversal is stated bluntly at the top of the call: "just changed 180 DEGREES and WE'VE LANDED A LOT MORE TILES THAN WE THOUGHT ON INTEL 14A."** ⚠️ **And the no-names policy is restated as permanent, not tactical: "I love our philosophy just to KEEP OUR HEADS DOWN… and NOT REALLY WORRY ABOUT MARKETING THEIR NAMES."** 🔴 **② THE FOUNDRY LOSS, DECOMPOSED — the answer this page has been missing. Asked what drives the ~$2bn/qtr: "the BIGGEST CONTRIBUTOR is really that FIVE NODE AND FOUR YEAR JOURNEY — when you're LAYERING STARTUP COST ON TOP OF STARTUP COST ON TOP OF STARTUP COST, it's hard to recoup the investment," plus "just NOT BEING AT SCALE." The specific scale gate is named and it is a PRODUCT gate, not a yield gate: "on Intel 18A… CURRENTLY WE'RE BUILDING PANTHER LAKE ON 18A. THAT IS ONLY A NOTEBOOK PART. IT DOESN'T COVER OUR DESKTOP NEEDS. As you move to NOVA LAKE at the end of this year and into next year, you SIGNIFICANTLY BROADEN OUT THE COVERAGE of 18A against our own product portfolio."** ➤ **So the break-even glide path runs through Nova Lake volume, not through the external customer — "you have a glide path to get to op profit break even on JUST THE INTERNAL DYNAMICS of our fabs supporting Intel products," and the external win is what PUSHES IT OUT ("we're not going to be penny wise and pound foolish… we're not going to hit some arbitrary target of break even").** 🔴 **③ ADVANCED-PACKAGING CAPITAL INTENSITY, QUANTIFIED FOR THE FIRST TIME ON THIS PAGE: "for every DOLLAR YOU SPEND ON ADVANCED PACKAGING, YOU'RE PROBABLY SPENDING $5 ON WAFERS — that's manager math, don't hold me to it, but it's probably the right ratio." Capacity is in New Mexico plus the Malaysia expansion flagged in Q1, and Intel says YES it can ramp MULTIPLE EMIB customers next year.** ⚠️ **The binding constraint is external and is stated twice: "the BIGGEST CONSTRAINT on growing that business is NOT INTERNAL, IT'S THE SUBSTRATES EXTERNALLY"; "this is an environment where PREPAYS ARE NECESSARY. AND WE'RE GETTING PREPAYS FROM OUR CUSTOMERS, SOME OF THOSE PREPAYS ARE BEING USED TO PREPAY OUR SUPPLIERS" (with letters of credit as the non-cash alternative). ➤ Read against the Unimicron/Ibiden substrate thread on this page and against [[MEDIATEK]]: Intel's packaging ramp is gated by the same substrate queue its customers are in, and it is funding its place in that queue with customer money.** **④ THE ASIC LINE — the $1bn → $2bn → $4bn walk, sourced: "as Dave talked about a couple quarters ago… this was on a BILLION DOLLAR annualized run rate. WE'RE NOW SAYING TWO, and Dave alluded last week that IT GETS TO FOUR PRETTY QUICKLY." Run by "Shrini from [[CDNS]]." The model is explicitly the merchant one — "the CLASSIC [[AVGO]] AND [[MRVL]] MODEL, where we provide the design services and the wafers [are] built at TSMC. We're very happy to do that and WE MAKE VERY GOOD PROFITABILITY off that business" — customer's choice on x86 or Arm core, one-stop-shop or design-only.** ⚠️ **So a material part of the ASIC line is TSMC-fabbed by design and does NOT fill Intel fabs.** **⑤ GROSS MARGIN — what actually drove the Q2 beat and why it does NOT repeat: "the beat was really VOLUME AND PRICING. PRICING WAS SOMEWHAT LIKE FOR LIKE," plus better yields and cycle times on 18A/Intel 3 driving unit costs below plan. On the inventory write-downs: they were taken to FREE UP CLIENT CAPACITY FOR SERVER ("we had intent to build those client parts… we were just happy to have capacity we could drive to Intel 3"), and asked directly whether they can come back as a Q3/Q4 benefit — "WE WROTE THEM DOWN TO THAT," i.e. GONE.** ➤ **That closes off the "Q2 write-downs reverse into Q3 margin" bull argument some of the flow has leaned on. Intel 3 / 18A "are progressing AHEAD of internal targets [but] they're STILL DILUTIVE and they're going to GROW SEQUENTIALLY AS A PERCENT OF MIX Q2→Q3."** **⑥ CAPEX FRAMEWORK — IR argues AGAINST the intensity lens the sell-side is using: asked whether TSMC's mid-30s capex/revenue is the right anchor, "I would ARGUE AGAINST USING THAT CAPEX-TO-REV RATIO when we're so early in the build out of this external foundry business… we're going to have to PUT A LOT OF CAPITAL ON THE GROUND BEFORE we start to get revenue and profitability out of those fabs." The counter-example offered is the best long-duration-asset argument Intel has made: "if you look at the cash flow that INTEL 7/10 is generating today, it's tremendous… as a MULTIPLE OF THE BOOK VALUE CARRYING, IT'S LIKE FIVE OR SIX X the book value today" — on a node he concedes "was one of the WORST NODES THAT WE EVER DEVELOPED OR RAMPED."** **⑦ THE RE-RATING ARGUMENT, in management's own framing: "if you look at our 30-YEAR REVENUE CAGR, IT'S ABOUT 3%. And highly cyclical. And historically, THE MARKET WAS WILLING TO PAY A 10 MULTIPLE ON THAT." Against three markets with "almost no share" — ASIC, advanced packaging, external foundry — "you can make an argument that our ADDRESSABLE MARKET IS GOING UP 8 TO 10X." Rule of 45 restated with its historical benchmark: "as dominant as we were, we were only driving a rule of SOMEWHERE BETWEEN 30 AND 35."** **⑧ SUPPLY AGREEMENTS — the terms, which were not previously on this page: "year to date we've signed TEN LONG TERM SUPPLY AGREEMENTS"; they cover **volume AND pricing** (explicitly NOT share), and "if you look at the TENOR on these, they range… 18 MONTHS ALL THE WAY OUT TO FIVE YEARS."** **⑨ CLIENT/EDGE: Q2 units −8% y/y against a full-year market "down call it 10 to 12%… with the BACK HALF BEING WORSE THAN THE FRONT HALF"; Q3 CCG up modestly q/q but "ALL OF THAT IS BEING DRIVEN BY EDGE"; PCs down sequentially in Q4 is in the forecast; **130 design wins for Core series 3 at the edge**, "a bunch of those are brain and control for ROBOTICS," and "over time the EDGE MARKET COULD RIVAL THE OVERALL CLIENT MARKET IN SIZE."** ⚠️ **IR also decouples the capex raise from client explicitly: "the CAPEX comments that Dave made… that's much more about servicing very strong and sustainable demand in CPUs FOR SERVERS. IT'S NOT NECESSARILY A ROBUST CALL ON THE CLIENT MARKET in the near term."** **⑩ EXTERNAL WAFERS / [[TSM]]: "our third party foundries are really important partners… TSMC, SAMSUNG AND QUITE FRANKLY UMC… we NEED WAFERS FROM THEM to support our business on the PC front"; Panther Lake still has external tiles; **Nova Lake keeps external-foundry support, with ~70% of tiles internal on the VOLUME SKUs — "that's still the right ballpark."** **⑪ SERVER ASP — IR pushes back on the unit-ASP comparison the Street is running: "the VAST MAJORITY of [the y/y ASP uplift] was actually MIX RELATED… you really have to compare it ON A CORE BASIS because we are generating units with Granite that has significantly more cores. Now WE ALSO DID BETTER ON ASP PER CORE." On China: "I'm not sure I would characterize [China as the source of strength] — in this sort of a market EVERYTHING'S STRONG… WE ARE ABSOLUTELY SUPPLY CONSTRAINED. IF WE HAD MORE SERVER WAFERS TO SELL, WE'D SELL MORE SERVERS." Net strength = US hyperscalers. On [[NVDA]]: the relationship is "EXTREMELY HEALTHY," with **full NVLink integration** the point — "if we're on a level playing field on that KPI, we have a lot of confidence in just the overall performance of our CPUs being better than the ARM camp."** 🔴 **⑫ THE DIAMOND CORRECTION — this contradicts the compressed version of the same message in the 08-03 UBS relay above, and the primary should be carried: Pitzer does NOT call multi-threading "a mistake." He says the opposite conclusion follows from it — "we've been pretty transparent that NOT HAVING MULTI-THREADING IN DIAMOND… perhaps SINGLE THREADED WORKLOADS HAVE BECOME MORE IMPORTANT, WHICH GIVES US SOME OPTIMISM THAT DIAMOND IS ACTUALLY A MORE COMPETITIVE PRODUCT THAN WE WOULD'VE THOUGHT SIX OR NINE MONTHS AGO." Both versions retained, primary preferred — see `## Changelog`.** **On the roadmap he is candid on the remaining gap: with Coral "we start to bring a product to market that's much more competitive — MORE ON A PERFORMANCE BASIS THAN A COST BASIS… I THINK WE'LL CLOSE THE GAP ON PERFORMANCE BEFORE WE CLOSE THE GAP ON COST, and that's an area that we need to work on," and on x86-vs-Arm the differentiator he leans on is SECURITY (vPro +1,500% enterprise activations since Q3'25, confidential computing on server).** **⑬ ANALYST DAY: "definitely on the table, THE TIMING IS STILL NOT… WE'RE NOT COMMITTING TO," gated on Lip-Bu completing the leadership team — and pointedly, "the investor day WAS NEVER MEANT TO BE AN OPPORTUNITY OF BEING ABLE TO NAME EXTERNAL CUSTOMERS."** **⑭ WAFER PRICING POLICY, net-new: "a guiding principle of MARKET-BASED PRICING FOR ALL OF OUR WAFERS"; for externals "could we USE PRICING TO GET OUR FOOT IN THE DOOR? SURE"; internal gets scale treatment. GM ladder: four handle (done) → mid-four → "something that starts with a five."** ⚠️ Machine transcript; ASR renders "ASIC" as "A six", Intel 3 as "Intel 43/Intel 710", EMIB as "EIP", Lip-Bu as "Lipu", and Seok-Hee Lee as "Suki to cut Lee." (Intel IR · John Pitzer, hosted by BofA · Vivek Arya, 2026-07-28) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_28_intc_ir_post_2q_bofa.html) |
| 07-28 | BofA · Vivek Arya (hosted the INTC IR call with John Pitzer) | guidance | **bull** | **Maintain Buy, PO $160** (price $91.67). BofA hosted INTC IR head **John Pitzer** for a well-attended investor call the week after the Q2 print — the most granular forward detail on this page. **Eight takeaways.** **(1) Server share is SUPPLY-driven through 2026-28, not design**, with **Coral Rapids** also closing the performance gap in 2027. **(2) Q2 server ASP strength of +48% y/y was driven by Granite Rapids adoption** (higher core count), with the ramp continuing through 2H — and **ASP/core is also rising**, so it is not purely mix. **(3) Foundry is inflecting: 18A yields nearing industry-standard exiting 2026, and a path to operating-margin breakeven exiting 2027 EX-external customers.** **(4) 18A-P and 14A are the external nodes — 18A-P volume committed for 2027, 14A HVM for 2028.** **(5) EMIB-T backlog is building, ramping 2027-28, with each engagement worth multiple $bn/yr.** (6) **PC TAM on track for a 10-12% y/y DECLINE in 2026**, though INTC sees limited inventory risk given visibility into sell-in vs sell-through. **(7) Long-term GM goals: mid-40%+ then 50%+.** (8) Multiple levers — **prepayments, non-core asset sales, equity** — to support rising capex intensity. (BofA · Vivek Arya, 2026-07-28) |
| 07-28 | @jukan05 (X, relaying Asia sell-side) | competicao | **bull** | On [[GOOG]]'s TPU capacity: **“Although we do not have the detailed allocation yet, we think it is difficult to reach Google's target with TSMC alone, and Intel's supply is a must by 2028.”** An external-foundry datapoint that lines up with the **14A-HVM-in-2028** timeline Pitzer gave BofA the same day — logged as corroboration of the external-node thesis from outside the INTC-sponsored channel. ⚠️ Relayed quote without a named originating broker — attributed to the handle. (@jukan05, 2026-07-28) **✅ ORIGINATOR IDENTIFIED 2026-07-28 — the quote is verbatim from Fubon Research (Sherman Shang / Daniel Yang), "Our advanced packaging, CoWoS forecast adjustments; other updates," 2026-07-28, distributed by Jefferies. See the primary row below; the attribution gap is closed and the full context (which materially changes what the line means) is logged there.** |
| 07-28 | **Fubon Research · Sherman Shang / Daniel Yang — "Our advanced packaging, CoWoS forecast adjustments; other updates"** (dist. Jefferies; sector Overweight) | competition | **bull** | 🔴 **The primary source behind the @jukan05 relay above — and read in full it is a bigger item than the relay implied, because it is FUBON REVERSING ITS OWN POSITION. Fubon has been this page's anchor bear on EMIB-T** (06-05: EMIB yield 60-70% vs CoWoS 95%+, EMIB-T "not yet ready to enter mass production," so MediaTek/Google prioritise TSMC CoWoS-L; **07-08**: Google asked Intel for **95%+** EMIB-T yield before production, "very challenging to achieve by mid-2027," so Google would **"at last rely on TSMC's [2028 CoWoS] expansion plan"**). **It now says the opposite conclusion, verbatim: *"Based on our checks, Google plans to have 12-15mn TPUs in 2028. Entering 2028, Google's TPUs will enter the V9 generation with four compute dies, suggesting that their capacity consumption will more than double in 2028 vs. 2027. Although we do not have the detailed allocation yet, we think it is difficult to reach Google's target with TSMC alone, and Intel's supply is a must by 2028."*** **The logic that changed is worth stating precisely, because it is NOT a yield capitulation: 07-08 argued the TSMC fallback would absorb Google's demand; 07-28 argues the demand is too large for TSMC to absorb, so Intel is required regardless of where the yield debate lands. The learning-curve caveat is explicitly retained, reclassified from "reason the ramp doesn't happen" to "risk to the ramp."** Fubon's own words on Intel: ***"although there is still a lot of noise on Intel's EMIB-T progress, especially its learning curve improvement, we see Intel becoming more positive in capacity expansion, which is positive to the Google supply chain."*** **NET-NEW AND THE MOST TRACKABLE CAPACITY MARK ON THIS PAGE: "Intel's EMIB capacity will reach 10-12k by the end of 2026, and the plan is to double its capacity to 24-25k per month by the end of 2027."** That is the first monthly EMIB capacity path logged here, and it sits usefully against UBS's revenue model (07-27: US$5.4bn 2028E on 2.3mn packages) — a ~24-25k/month exit rate in 2027 is ~290-300k packages/yr of run-rate capacity entering 2028, i.e. the 2.3mn-package 2028 figure requires either a further large step or is measured on a different basis. **Flagged as a reconciliation item, not resolved here.** **On the capex: "Intel has already announced its capex increase. Although there is no detailed breakdown of its capex spending, the CFO highlighted that a great portion of the new capex is earmarked for expanding advanced packaging capabilities such as EMIB-T."** That is third-party corroboration of where the >$20B FY26 / "significantly above" CY27 capex is actually going — the page's Signal table has treated the capex raise as a WFE read-through; Fubon says a large slice is packaging, which is the higher-return, lower-risk leg (cf. MS: packaging "reasonably low risk," wafer foundry "a show-me story since 2014"). **Watch item, net-new and corroborating UBS from a second house: "Intel's major supplier Ibiden's upcoming earnings call and potential capex increase in the next two years will be another highlight"** — the same Ibiden substrate-capacity dependency UBS identified on 07-27 ("demand from Google TPU programs alone could largely absorb the planned capacity"), now flagged independently. ⚠️ **Two limits to keep on the record: (i) Fubon still gives NO EMIB-T yield number in this note and does not retract its 60-70%/95%+ framing — it simply stops treating the gate as decisive; (ii) "Intel's supply is a must" is a statement about Google's arithmetic, not a disclosed Intel order or a named commitment, so it does not close this page's standing "still no named anchor customer" bear.** (Fubon "Our advanced packaging, CoWoS forecast adjustments; other updates", 2026-07-28) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Our_advanced_packaging_CoWoS_forecast_adjustments_other_up.html) |

Logged because EDA engagement is one of the few external, non-Intel-sourced scoreboards for foundry viability — the same lens as the SemiAnalysis EDA-primer datapoint already in Debate (Intel Foundry ecosystem mentions in EDA earnings calls rising from ~0-1/quarter to 6/quarter by 2025Q1). | 07-24 | **BofA · Vivek Arya — post-print client call (PRIMARY TRANSCRIPT; the spoken version of the note in the row below, with the 10-Q decomposition and the callback read-outs the note does not carry)** | guidance | **bull** | ⚠️ **Partial duplicate of the 07-24 BofA note below on rating/PO/estimates — those are not repeated here. What is net-new is the decomposition and the price framing.** 🔴 **① THE 10-Q DECOMPOSITION, done live off the filing: "that 59% [DCAI] growth is essentially 9% FROM UNITS AND 48% FROM ASPs" — and on client, "their PC VOLUMES WERE DOWN 8%, whereas ASP WAS UP 27%."** ➤ **The +27% client ASP is the number this page did not have, and it is the direct answer to the channel-stuffing bear: the client beat is price/mix, not units, and Arya reads it as Intel deliberately cutting the low end (Chromebooks/low-end notebooks "basically moved to [[ARM]]") while winning high-end notebooks.** ⚠️ **He still flags it: "usually when you see people SHIP MORE PCs at a time when PC SELL-THROUGH ISN'T THAT GREAT, you just have to be careful" — Intel has acknowledged it is REBUILDING PC INVENTORY into a PC market that may be tough in 2027 too on memory pricing. Called "a YELLOW FLAG, not a red flag."** 🔴 **② THE ASP HEADROOM MAP, the cleanest cross-vendor version on this page: 1Q server ASP "SUB $1,000, like $900 or so" for Intel vs "[[AMD]] OVER $2,000," against [[NVDA]]/[[ARM]] talking "$3 TO $5,000" for agentic CPUs — "Intel potentially has a PATH TO TRIPLING THEIR CPU ASP." On the per-core basis Intel itself prefers: "these guys are getting about $10 A CORE or so. AMD IS GETTING CLOSER TO $20 A CORE. And recently AMD with VENICE suggested THE CORE COUNT COULD CLOSE TO DOUBLE."** ⚠️ **Intel IR would not sign up to $3-5k on the 07-28 call (row above) — "not as open to signing up for those levels" — but did confirm per-core ASPs have stopped declining and are now stable-to-up.** **He also reconciles the two server-CPU TAMs on the wiki: AMD's $220bn-by-2030 (from $120bn) vs NVDA's $200bn — "Nvidia['s] $200 did NOT INCLUDE SOME OF THE GENERIC SERVERS, that was really going after AI and agentic servers… SO THEIR 200, AMD'S 220 MIGHT NOT BE THAT DIFFERENT," with the residual spread in what you assume for CUSTOM-CHIP ASPs (Graviton/Axion at list vs transfer price).** 🔴 **③ THE CAPEX FUNDING ITEM THIS PAGE'S BEAR ANCHOR NEEDS, and it is not in the note: "$20 BILLION IS A GROSS NUMBER FOR THIS YEAR. It's supposed[ly] inflows from BROOKFIELD… FIVE TO SIX-ISH BILLION is the Brookfield contribution, the rest is Intel's. NEXT YEAR, THE BROOKFIELD CONTRIBUTION GOES AWAY. So whatever Intel puts by way of capex is ALL COMING [from Intel]."** ➤ **Read against the FY27 capex step-up: the funding gap widens by the Brookfield delta BEFORE any growth in the headline number. This is the arithmetic behind the "who pays for it" fault line in the synthesis.** **Within the number, tools were growing 25% and "they have now BUMPED IT TO 40%" — Arya's read-through: "if there is one positive takeaway OTHER THAN INTEL from this call, IT'S SEMI CAPS," with WFE "145, 150 BILLION" this year and ASML implying "ALMOST 10% PRICING, 10 TO 15%." He anchors Intel's 2027 capex growth to ASML's litho +30% ("I won't be surprised if that is the ZIP CODE").** **④ VALUATION / THE ASYMMETRY, stated explicitly at a $100 price: "AT $100, I STILL THINK THERE IS MAYBE $10 DOWN AND $50 TO $100 UP FROM HERE." The EPS bridge under rule-of-45 off a 2025 base at ~15% top-line growth: "$1.50 THIS YEAR… and then TWO, THREE, FOUR, FIVE in the next few years… IT'LL GET YOU TO $5-ISH BY THE END OF THE DECADE" — that is the BASELINE, with foundry upside on top. And the condition for the PO to be right is quantified: "FOR US TO BE RIGHT INTEL NEEDS TO BE ABOUT 10% OF THE FOUNDRY MARKET… it's getting those FIRST FEW PERCENT that is the toughest," with the AMD-in-servers analogy as the template.** **⑤ CALLBACK READ-OUTS not in the note: on 14A, "PDK 0.5 out… CUSTOMERS CAN MAKE [A] DECISION BEFORE 0.9, BUT THAT'S WHERE MOST OF THEM WANT TO"; server visibility "ALL THE WAY TILL 2028"; on packaging content, "a FEW THOUSAND [dollars] PER PACKAGE would not be a bad way to think about the content," with [[MEDIATEK]] "the first one" and "a few million units of the TPU next year" (also engagements around Amazon Graviton, and "perhaps looking at packaging with Trainium"); on opex, headcount "about 77,000" with past hints of 70,000 but savings being reinvested, so "opex MAY NOT DECLINE TO THE EXTENT PEOPLE THINK" — "classic Lip-Bu playbook… exactly what he used at [[CDNS]]."** ⚠️ **On the analyst day his read is more cautious than IR's: "MY GUT IS IT'S MORE A Q1 EVENT AT THIS STAGE" (i.e. early 2027, when the CY27 capex number lands), though a customer announcement "can probably come sooner than that."** **⑥ On the equity-raise overhang, from the callback: Intel said "it is FAIR TO THINK WE MIGHT TAKE SOME CAPITAL MARKET ACTION, BUT WE DON'T HAVE TO" — non-core disposals plus customer prepayments; Arya's own read is that a raise would be read two ways at once ("why are they using it?" vs "it must mean a foundry announcement is forthcoming") and notes the alternative funding routes are strategic, not public — [[AAPL]]'s Made-in-America program, SoftBank, the White House.** (BofA · Vivek Arya, client call, 2026-07-24) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_24_vivek_follow_up_on_intc.html) |
| 07-24 | BofA · Vivek Arya ("Core acceleration, with capex boost confirming foundry deals") | guidance | bull | **Buy, PO $160 reiterated**; **raising CY26/27/28E EPS by 20-40%** on server unit/ASP expansion. Frames **three actions as signals of coming external-foundry success**: (1) first firm commitment to develop 14A, (2) raised CY26/27 capex, (3) **hiring former SK Hynix chief Seok-Hee Lee to lead Intel Foundry advanced packaging**. DC +59% y/y = "INTC's best growth rate in the past 15 years." Expects Intel **capex growth at least in line with ASML's ~30%+ annual industry WFE growth** — read-across positive for **both front-end and back-end packaging semicap vendors**. Views US-based leading-edge capacity + White House backing as long-term moats. Risks flagged: **FCF near-zero or negative through CY26-27**, foundry break-even likely pushed out, high capex for a few years; watch for capital-market action, though non-core disposals + customer prepayments are alternatives. |
| 07-24 | MS Europe · van Putten / Simpson ("Implication of Intel's capex raise for ASML and ASMI") | supply | bull | The WFE read-through, quantified. Intel **equipment spend +40% y/y in 2026**, focused on front-end tools for **Intel 3, 18A and 18A-P**, "aggressively locking in tool purchase orders"; space/clean-room already in place from prior years so **the bulk of incremental capex is tooling**. Intel has historically been **>10% of equipment revenue at both ASML and ASMI** (ASMI: ~18% in 2022, MSe ~11% recently). **ASMI:** 14A's addressable market per equivalent 1k-wpm capacity is **~40% higher than Intel 3** (analogous to TSMC 3nm vs A14) → "significant upward pressure" on MS's estimates (currently +15% y/y then +35% in 2027). **ASML:** MS had modelled only **1 Low-NA EUV shipment to Intel in FY26 ramping to 6 in FY27**; with an earlier 14A roll-out it now thinks Intel demand could be **2-4 Low-NA EUV tools this year** — not changing estimates because "this is now pushing against what ASML may be able to deliver," which **reaffirms ASML's pricing power**. Filed to [[ASML]]. |

INTC marked **(+)** in the Bernstein AM Note alongside AMD (+). |

Useful as the counter-narrative marker: the same print reads as a turnaround confirmation or a widening-loss story depending on which segment you anchor on. **Expanded 07-25 with the figures the first pass didn't carry:** GAAP **net loss $10.848B** in Q2 (vs a $3.024B loss in the year-ago quarter) — the GAAP hole behind the non-GAAP $0.42 beat; Intel Foundry **op loss $2.1B on $5.8B of sales** vs $2.4B on $5.4B in Q1, the **10th consecutive quarterly Foundry loss**, taking **cumulative Intel Foundry losses to ~$28B since 1Q24**; external third-party foundry + assembly/test revenue **$293M in Q2 and $467M in 1H26, against $22M and $53M in the comparable 2025 periods** (per the filing) — i.e. external is up >10x y/y off a trivial base but is still ~5% of segment revenue. |
| 07-24 | KeyBanc · John Vinh (quoted in Semiecosystem / LaPedus) | supply | **bull** | **The single most consequential net-new datapoint of the day for the Foundry thesis: KeyBanc says EMIB-T yields "have reached 98%."** If accurate, that **clears the 95%+ bar Google set for TPU V9 production** and directly contradicts Fubon's 60-70% EMIB read (06-05) and its judgement that 95%+ by mid-2027 was "very challenging" — the exact yield gate on which the page's whole EMIB-T-vs-CoWoS debate turns (see the Advanced packaging Signal row and the 07-08 Fubon row). **But the bottleneck simply moves upstream: "INTC is supply constrained on substrates as yields are at 60-70%."** Treat with care — Vinh does not say whether 98% is line/sample yield or packaged-unit yield, and it is second-hand via a trade newsletter, so it does not on its own retire the Fubon/Jefferies bear case; it does mean the bear case must now be argued on substrates rather than on EMIB-T itself. Other KeyBanc content from the same note: **18A yields improved to 85% from 65% q/q** (vs TSMC N2 90%, Samsung SF2 50-60%) — corroborates the 07-14 KeyBanc ">85%" row and now prices the gap to TSMC at ~5 points; **14A "remains on schedule and progressing better than 18A in terms of defect densities," on track to ramp into mass production in 2H28**; **foundry design wins claimed with AAPL (both 18A and 14A, low-end M-series for MacBooks/iPads), plus AMD, NVDA, MRVL, MSFT, MU and OpenAI** — the broadest named list on the page, though Vinh notes it is "unclear if Intel is providing foundry and/or packaging services for these customers"; **EMIB-T packaging business won from Amazon, Google and Microsoft**; **AI PC processor sales +26% in the quarter**; capex to $20B is **+13% y/y**; and, on the ordering side, "**we believe INTC has upwardly revised some of its foundry equipment orders by 30-40%**" on surging agentic-AI server-CPU demand, with plans to significantly expand 18A and Intel 3 (Granite Rapids) capacity — a direct WFE read-through (cf. Also reiterates from the print: "**big beat on ASP**, GMs implied flattish when excluding the HW from inventory writedowns in Q2, capex substantially higher signals confidence for 18A/14A **but no customer announced**." Reads against the Diamond Rapids detail already on the page (up to 4 compute tiles/CPU on Intel 18A, CBBs on 18A-P hybrid-bonded to an Intel 3-PT base die) — so the question is arguably about a *product announcement*, not the architecture. Jefferies' gating condition on the Leading-Edge WFE step-up is explicit: "**Leading Edge will step meaningfully higher if INTC has in fact secured a client for 14A** but official guidance for C27 remains 6 months out" — i.e. the same unresolved 14A-customer question, priced as the swing factor for the whole semicap complex.
