| **08-16** | 🔴🔴 **Mizuho · Vijay Rakesh (w/ Dale Gai Japan, Kevin Wang HK) — "AI Server Call: 2027 VR with HBM and NPO/CPO, Adv. Packaging EMIB-T/CoPoS Ramps." NEUTRAL, PT $109 REITERATED** (px $102.50) | **produto / supply** | **bull (on packaging), neutral (on the stock)** | ★ **THE STRONGEST EXTERNAL PACKAGING-WIN SIGNAL ON THIS PAGE IN MONTHS — AND IT COMES WITH A YIELD NUMBER THAT WOULD CLEAR THE CUSTOMER'S OWN BAR, IF THE BASIS WERE STATED. As printed: "EMIB-T is seeing good yields >97% and real traction," with [[MEDIATEK]] potentially using it for TPUv9 and MTK's TPU the FIRST HIGH-VOLUME EMIB-T CUSTOMER IN 2028E.** (Hedges kept: "potentially" on TPUv9; "first high-volume" is dated 2028E.) 🔴⚠️ **AND IT COLLIDES HEAD-ON WITH FOUR SOURCES ALREADY ON THE WIKI — DELIBERATELY NOT NETTED, BECAUSE MIZUHO DOES NOT SAY WHICH LEVEL THE >97% IS MEASURED AT: Fubon (07-08) had [[GOOG]] demanding 95%+ and called that "very challenging" by mid-2027; DIGITIMES (08-03, the row below) had package yield >90% with "a 50% SUBSTRATE YIELD REMAINS THE BOTTLENECK"; JPM (08-16, on [[MEDIATEK]]) had "~60% at SUBSTRATE level and ~90% at CHIP-ON-SUBSTRATE level"; UBS (07-26) had "packaging validation ~90-92%" and substrate "possibly ~50%." If >97% is the bridge/chip-on-substrate step it is an incremental improvement on the ~90-92% cluster and says nothing about the binding substrate constraint; if it is the substrate step it overturns four sources. Until the basis is disclosed it must NOT be used to declare Google's 95%+ gate cleared.** 🆕 **Also net-new and the most useful line for the foundry model: "2028E CoPoS/EMIB-T adoption unlocks 8–12x reticle size (or a 5–10x increase in ASIC outs) versus today's CoWoS-L at 5.5x"** — the first time the reticle ladder is expressed as **ASIC OUTPUT** rather than area, which is what a packaging customer actually buys. ⚠️ **Widens this house's own 08-09 band downward at the bottom (8x vs the 10–12x it then gave EMIB/EMIB-T; CoPoS was 14x by 2029E). The common 5.5x CoWoS-L base is the reliable anchor.** ✅ **The $109 is a REITERATION of the mark this page adopted on 08-09 — a second dated appearance materially reduces the risk that the un-rationalised 08-09 cut was a stale table entry, which was the caveat logged at the time.** (Mizuho · Vijay Rakesh, 2026-08-16) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/AI_Server_Call__2027_VR_with_HBM_and_NPO_CPO_Adv_Packaging.html) |

### Archived — Q1 FY26 intra-quarter flow (Apr 23 → Jul 22, 2026; rolled at the Q2'26 print)
_The Q1'26 Signal-vs-management table and full log below are retained for the historical record; they cover the window between the Q1'26 (04-23) and Q2'26 (07-23) prints._

**Signal vs management** — what management said on the last call × what the intra-quarter flow is saying (✓ confirms · ⚠ nuances · ✗ contests):

| Theme | Management said (Q1 FY26) | Intra-quarter flow | Signal |
|---|---|---|---|
| **18A / 14A ramp** | Yields ~2-3 months ahead of the internal benchmark; fastest ramps in 5 years; 14A outpacing 18A at the equivalent point | JPM: 18A yields +6-7 pp/month, "extremely impressed" with Lip-Bu Tan · MS bus tour bullish · StoneX corroborates 18A/14A · Bernstein/Dai: Intel the FIRST High-NA adopter, in 2028 at A14 (ahead of Samsung ~'29 / TSMC ~'30) · JPM/Gokul (07-08): TSMC to push back hard vs Intel — Intel+Samsung only ~5-6% of 2nm in the first two waves (2028-29) vs TSMC ~90-95%; TSMC cheaper on an adjusted basis even at a lower sticker · **KeyBanc (07-14): 18A yield >85% (from 65% last qtr), capacity being "significantly expanded"** · **Nova Lake now ~80-90% of tiles pulled in-house onto 18A (from ~60-70%-to-TSMC), a confidence tell (07-15); 18A confirmed US 2nm first-mover + High-NA-first for a volume part (07-13/15)** · **ASML (07-17): Intel the first customer running High-NA EUV in production on 18A, shipping product — "a bit of a validation of where we stand with High-NA"; CPU demand driving more Intel capacity, now visible in ASML's order intake** · **@SemiAnalysis_ (07-20): Kirin 9030 teardown shows a 32.5nm metal pitch, tighter than Intel 18A — a competitive-process nuance (bull rebuttal already on page: 18A supports 32nm M0, PowerVia/full-stack density still ahead)** | **✓ confirms, strengthening** (process execution + High-NA-first tell corroborated by ASML + in-sourcing momentum; but TSMC frames the 2nm foundry-share gap as durable, and the Kirin teardown is a fresh Chinese-node comparison) |
| **Server / DCAI** | DCAI $5.1B (+22% y/y); server CPU double-digit | Redburn (contrarian): x86 server share 50%→55%→~60%, Intel "less capacity-constrained than AMD" · UBS bear: share falls 60%→29% by 2030 (ARM/AMD gain) · Q4'25 DCAI $4.7B (+15% q/q), op margin 26.4% (up from 8.6% Q4'24) — SemiAnalysis calls the late-2025 demand uptick an inflection prompting a 2026 capex guide-up (SemiAnalysis, 2026-02-09) · **JPM (07-12): x86 gets an accelerated ~25% CAGR (server units +20%/yr) but ARM → ~43% of blended server CPU by '28** · **BofA/Core Wars (07-13): INTC server-CPU rev ~$40bn+ by CY30 (~25% of $170bn TAM), value share ~41%→~24% by 2030** · Mizuho (07-11) Neutral $135, "on the sidelines" · **KeyBanc (07-14): INTC-3 capacity for +25-30% server-unit growth this year, >50% next; general-purpose server outlook raised to +30% (from +18.5%); AMD+INTC hope for >50% server-unit growth in 2027** · **BofA lightning round (07-20): expects a beat by ≥$300-500M on server CPU into the 7/23 print ("could sell a hamster-wheel CPU")** | **⚠ nuance** (near-term good/ASP-led + strengthening x86 volume tailwind, but LT share erosion in dispute) |
| **Diamond Rapids competitiveness (SMT gap)** | Management concedes dropped SMT was "a mistake"; real AMD-parity waits on Coral Rapids, no firm date | SemiAnalysis quantifies the damage: 192-core/192-thread Diamond Rapids only ~40% faster than today's 128-core/256-thread Granite Rapids — "exposing Intel for another generation with lower performance than AMD"; gap vs Venice "will widen even more" 2026-2028. Diamond Rapids-SP (mainstream 8ch) cancelled outright, gap to at least 2028 (SemiAnalysis, 2026-02-09) | **✓ confirms, more bearish than management's framing** |
| **Sierra Forest / E-core Xeon line** | Not directly addressed on the Q1 call | SemiAnalysis: Sierra Forest hyperscaler adoption "limited" (AMD + internal ARM instead); Sierra Forest-AP never reached GA; successor line (Rouge River Forest / Clearwater Forest follow-on) confirmed dead, "will end with just 2 generations" (SemiAnalysis, 2026-02-09) | **✗ contests** (realized share loss management doesn't discuss) |
| **Advanced packaging (EMIB)** | Per engagement "billions/year" (up from "hundreds of M"), ramp in 2027 | Fubon: EMIB yield only ~60-70% vs CoWoS 95%+, EMIB-T "not ready for HVM" → MediaTek/Google prioritize TSMC. But MS Amerian: EMIB-T perf/cost edge vs CoWoS >9.7 reticle (higher sample yields, warpage/cost); JPM: MediaTek TPU v9 upside now explicitly gated on Intel's EMIB-T yield progress — packaging tech now load-bearing for an external roadmap; UBS: traction real but resource/capacity-constrained in 2028, internal + Google/MediaTek prioritized first · **Fubon (07-08): Google *planned* EMIB-T for TPU V9 (late-27/28) but asked for 95%+ yield → "very challenging" by mid-2027, likely falls back to TSMC 2028 CoWoS** · **GS (07-09): Intel IMT bumping/assembly for MediaTek "HomoFish" Google-TPU already ~90% yield (substrate ~70%), ~1.5yr to ramp — a distinct, positive IFS packaging read** · **Jefferies/Fubon (07-17): TSMC publicly "welcomes other suppliers" on CoWoS tightness = positive for Intel + MediaTek; but Taiwan rumors of slow IBP learning-curve improvement** · **Kaizad expert (07-16): EMIB 6-7× reticle vs CoWoS 3-4×, mature package yield 98-99%; "Coke-Pepsi 70/30" duopoly with Intel capped 20-30% share absent a wafer-foundry win; Google TPU evaluating EMIB-T** · **JPM TSMC-post-earnings (07-16): TSMC "more accommodating on OSAT" — packaging-overflow door open to Intel, front-end-wafer door not** · **Arete (07-21): Intel AP capacity 14k→60k wspm YE28 (New Mexico 12k→35k + Malaysia 2k→25k); external capacity 0/109k/272k annualized '26/27/28 (45% of total by '28) → $0/$1.5bn/$3.7bn AP revenue at ~$14k/panel; EMIB-T priced ~25% below TSMC CoWoS (~$733/good pkg); Google placed a 3M-unit TPU order with Intel for '28 (~$2.2bn) = first major AP-adoption evidence** · **🆕 KeyBanc/Vinh (07-24): "EMIB-T yields have reached 98%" — above Google's 95% production bar and flatly against Fubon's 60-70%; the constraint moves to substrates, "INTC is supply constrained on substrates as yields are at 60-70%." Also claims EMIB-T packaging wins from Amazon, Google and Microsoft** | **⚠→✓ tilting** (the yield gate that anchored the bear case may just have cleared — KeyBanc's 98% would satisfy Google's 95% requirement and reverse Fubon's "very challenging by mid-2027" call. Not yet decisive: 98% is second-hand via a trade newsletter, the yield basis (line vs packaged-unit) is unspecified, and it is contradicted by Fubon 06-05/07-08 and only loosely consistent with the Kaizad expert's "mature package yield 98-99%" and GS's ~90% IMT figure. **The substrate constraint is the new bottleneck to track** — and it is the same T-glass/substrate gate management named for 2H capacity. Resolve on the next external-foundry disclosure) |
| **Margin / foundry** | Non-GAAP GM 41% (~650bps above guide); capex flat, equipment +25% | Jefferies bear: 40-60% drop-through → exit-2026 GM in the low-40s, IFS break-even pushed to exit-2027 · **BofA lightning round (07-20): watch GM — 18A lower-yielding + pricier TSMC wafers, CFO habit of beating then guiding GM down** | **⚠ nuance** (margin still fragile) |
| **Capital / financing** | FY26 capex guided flat y/y (1st guide-up); buyback of Apollo's stake in Fab 34 ($14.2B) | SemiAnalysis: $20-35B financing hole, should raise ~$40B equity · Arcuri: doesn't think it needs a big raise; "foundry news flow very good" | **⚠ nuance** (need for a raise in debate) |

**Full log** (all intra-quarter flow, by date):

| Date | Source | Theme | Bias | What was said |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 07-26 | UBS · Sunny Lin / Randy Abrams / Nicolas Gaudois / Timothy Arcuri ("Can Intel challenge TSMC's advanced packaging leadership?") | supply | mixed | 🔴 **The first full-length sell-side deep dive dedicated to EMIB-T vs CoPoS — and it supplies a THIRD yield mark that sits between the two already on this page.** UBS: **"Intel must lift EMIB-T packaging from ~90-92% validation yields toward high-volume manufacturing levels,"** and **"EMIB-T 'substrate' fabrication yields may currently be at ~50%, vs >80% for HPC ABF substrates, but capacity expansion at Ibiden could offset lower yields and support selected products like Google's TPU v9."** Put next to what the page already carries — **Fubon 60-70% (06-05), KeyBanc "reached 98%" (07-24), ex-Intel CVP Kaizad "mature yield 98-99%" (07-16)** — the packaging-yield estimates now span **60% to 99%**, and the substrate estimates span **~50% (UBS) to 60-70% (KeyBanc)**. UBS's number is the only one explicitly labelled *validation* rather than production yield, which may reconcile some of the gap; it is not reconciled here. **The market sizing is the other net-new piece and it is big: advanced-packaging TAM US$19bn (2026E) → US$122bn (2030E), a 59% CAGR.** UBS's stress test — deliberately framed as a *bull* case for Intel — has **Intel taking a "meaningful" 13% industry share, or US$16bn of sales by 2030, equivalent to packages for ~24mn accelerator dies**; even then TSMC compounds advanced-packaging sales at a 50% CAGR 2026-30 and holds 65-70% share. **EMIB-T's structural case, stated by UBS:** a simpler bridge-based architecture, **competitive structural cost through elimination of the interposer**, and **greater scalability to ultra-large package sizes by building directly on large substrates** — with **mass production targeted H2'27-28, slightly AHEAD of TSMC's CoPoS in 2028**. **The two risks UBS names that are specific to Intel and new to this page: "integration between the foundry (TSMC) and back-end (Intel) processes, as well as yield responsibility"** — i.e. the commercial problem of who owns a yield loss when the die is TSMC's and the package is Intel's, which is a contracting obstacle rather than a technical one. **Rating unchanged and explicitly valuation-based: "While a successful EMIB-T ramp could lift Intel's foundry sales and profitability, we remain Neutral due to valuation considerations."** (UBS, 2026-07-26) |
| 07-26 | MS · Derrick Yang / Vivi Huang / Andy Meng ("Progress for Glass in Advanced Packaging") | supply | bull | **Intel named as the partner in Lens Tech's glass-core-substrate program, with a dated ramp: "Lens Tech (300433.SZ) announced it will work with Intel on the glass core with TGVs… Our checks indicate it will have a pilot line ready by end-2026, and more meaningful volume shipments might start from 2H27"** — and MS "think[s] it may be working with other potential Korean players on this as well." Read against the row above: **glass core is the structural answer to exactly the ~50%-substrate-yield constraint UBS flags**, since glass offers lower signal loss, better CTE matching (mitigating warpage) and greater mechanical strength at large panel sizes. The competitive timing matters — **Innolux is on a TSMC + Ibiden glass-core project with mass production only in 2H28 or 2029**, and **BOE plans capacity in 2027 for 2028 MP** — so on MS's checks **Intel/Lens Tech is ~12-18 months ahead of the TSMC/Ibiden path on glass**, which is the same ordering UBS gives for EMIB-T vs CoPoS. MS's caveat is the honest one: "we believe this could help with sentiment, but **more meaningful revenue contribution should take time**," and the hurdles are "more related to process control instead of pure equipment capability." (MS, 2026-07-26) |
| 07-26 | Jefferies EU Semis · Janardan Menon (Global SEMIS Daily) | capital | bull | Clean restatement of the 14A commitment with two details that sharpen it: **"Defect density and transistor performance are outpacing 18A development, and Intel is positioning the node for broad-based adoption across a wide range of customers"** — the first time on this page that 14A is described as *ahead of where 18A was at the same stage* — and **"the company is aggressively locking in tool purchase orders and accelerating cleanroom buildouts."** Jefferies adds that **14A "is also likely to be a material user of high-NA systems,"** and that **ASML's revenue from Intel "continues to be subdued this year, but is likely to accelerate in 2027"** — the WFE read-through ([[ASML]], ASM, VAT). Blayne Curtis's US-side framing is the constraint: **"Leading Edge will step meaningfully higher IF INTC has in fact secured a client for 14A, but official guidance for C27 remains 6 months out"** — i.e. the WFE upside is gated on a customer announcement that will not arrive at this print. |
| 07-26 | JPM · Matthew See / Harlan Sur (APAC Thematics) | valuation | bear | **The cleanest statement of the tape problem: Intel "was the latest victim of 'good earnings, bad reaction'… the stock closing -8% on Fri having initially rallied +5% pre-market post strong results & guidance that Harlan said 'cleared expectations by wide margins.'"** Context: the **SOX closed -4.3% Friday and is now -19% from the mid-June high**, with Nokia, ASML and TSMC as the earlier examples of the same pattern. Jefferies' own derating table has **CPUs down -21%** since the June 22 SOX peak, and INTC **-34% since the Tech-Momentum ATH** — i.e. the beat-and-raise has not been paid for. Positioning explains part of it: JPM's Schlegel has aggregate equity positioning light (**US TPM 19th %ile**) but **global semis still crowded on long-term metrics at the 98th %ile on both net exposure and L/S ratios** (hardware 99th/86th). | 07-27 | UBS · Sunny Lin / Randy Abrams / Nicolas Gaudois / Timothy Arcuri / Shingo Hirata / Jerry Su / Diana Chang ("Can Intel challenge TSMC's advanced packaging leadership? — Intel's EMIB-T vs TSMC's CoPoS") | supply | mixed | **The full note (the 07-26 Changelog entry covered the same piece via the Outlook preview; only net-new is logged here). Rating unchanged — Neutral, PT US$121**, px US$100.23 priced 23 Jul 2026, mkt cap US$521,617m, 2026/27E P/E 70.4x/51.1x, P/BV 4.3x/3.6x, ROE 6.2%/7.6% — and the Neutral is **explicitly a valuation call, not an execution call**: "while a successful EMIB-T ramp could lift Intel's foundry sales and profitability, we remain Neutral due to valuation considerations." **Net-new #1 — the first EMIB-T revenue model on this page: base case US$5,438m (2028E) / US$9,570m (2029E) / US$15,791m (2030E) = 9%/11%/13% industry share**, built as **Google TPU US$5,003/8,374/13,159m on 2.3/3.5/5.0mn packages** + **Others US$435/1,196/2,632m on 0.2/0.5/1.0mn**, at **EMIB-T content US$2,175/2,393/2,632 per package** (~4 XPU dies per package). **Upside case US$5,438/11,963/21,054m = 9%/14%/18% share** on Google TPU units 2.3/4.5/6.5mn. Note UBS books **zero external EMIB-T revenue before 2028** — not the same picture as Pitzer's "meaningful contribution in 2H26." **Net-new #2 — the yield spread on this page is mostly definitional, and the full note separates the four numbers: standard-EMIB PACKAGING ">90%"; standard-EMIB SUBSTRATE "close to 60%"; EMIB-T SUBSTRATE "around 50% for the leading Japan suppliers"; EMIB-T PACKAGING "about 90%, based on trial production runs of MediaTek's TPU v9 prototype."** So Fubon's 60-70% and UBS's ~50% are substrate marks; KeyBanc's 98% and Kaizad's 98-99% are packaging marks; they were never in conflict. **What is still unresolved is the bar: "for HPC products… packaging yields ideally should improve to the high 90% range."** **Net-new #3 — the substrate constraint has a dated workaround and a dated ceiling: Ibiden's ¥220.0bn Gama-plant investment (announced Feb-2026), EMIB-T MP from late 2027 with a meaningful ramp in 2028 — but "demand from Google TPU programs alone could largely absorb the planned capacity,"** so 2029-30 volume depends on Ibiden's *next* capex round. **Unimicron** is developing EMIB-T "although yields remain below optimal levels" and is positioned as **second source if volumes exceed expectations**. **Net-new #4 — manufacturing specifics:** EMIB-T substrates are **"5x or even larger"** than today's EMIB, potentially **>9-10x reticle** (vs 2-3x now) at **20+ layers** with more bridges; **TC NCF replaces standard die attach** for bridge placement into panel-level (510×515mm²) cavities; but the overall **equipment set is SIMPLER than CoPoS's** because EMIB-T has no separate chip-on-panel stage — hence **"a key leading indicator of EMIB-T capacity expansion is likely to be thermo-compression bonding (TCB) tool demand"** (suppliers **K&S, ASMPT**), and Intel **"may rely more on US-based equipment suppliers"** where TSMC's chain is Asia-localised. **Net-new #5 — the assembly footprint: Intel vertically integrating EMIB-T for external customers at its expanding Penang (Malaysia) plant, with the 2025 Amkor partnership opening Amkor's Korea/Portugal/US lines** — UBS expects Intel to keep Google TPUs in-house for delivery certainty (2028-29) and outsource **mature in-house CPU on-substrate packaging to Amkor**, with **C4 wafer bumping** a further outsourcing candidate. **Amkor rated Neutral, PT US$80** (px US$65). **Net-new #6 — UBS's head-to-head scorecard**, which is more even-handed than either side's marketing: **EMIB-T wins architecture simplicity, package complexity, scalability >12x reticle, cost potential (assuming reasonable yield) and time-to-market (H2'27 vs 2028); CoPoS wins performance, power integrity, yield confidence and ecosystem strength; manufacturing risk is a TIE.** Plus a rarely-cited Intel edge: it can **leverage its SMT expertise for board-level integration** to help customers manage warpage. (UBS, 2026-07-27) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/UBS_on_adv_packaging.html) |

Wei — "EMIB, Intel… IT LOOKS GOOD ON PAPER. Because everything looks good on paper. The trick is you need to deliver it in real world production, but it doesn't matter — [if] they can do it, WE WELCOME IT. Because that helps our customers out on the packaging side, AND THAT'S GONNA MEAN MORE WAFER BUSINESS FOR US."** **Why TSMC can afford to say that: it frames advanced packaging as "STRATEGIC, NOT FINANCIAL" — "where do we make our money? Packaging? No. Manufacturing semiconductor logic wafers. That's our bread and butter" — so every wafer Intel packages is, in TSMC's model, a wafer TSMC still fabricates.** 🔴 **THE REBUTTAL, which is the part that matters for the IFS thesis: "The part we disagree is, he also said on the call, that PACKAGING WILL LEAD TO OR BE THE GATEWAY TO FOUNDRY BUSINESS. NO. The complexity, difficulty, nature of packaging versus logic wafer… are 2 completely separate issues."** Two analogies offered: C.C. Wei's — *"that's why ASE, who does a lot of packaging — why can't they do foundry?"* — and Jeff Su's own, which he calls *"even more direct"*: **"we have had a competitor in South Korea, [[SAMSUNG]] Electronics, who is the king, the leader, UNDISPUTED in memory. And for the last 15 PLUS YEARS, they have been trying to leverage memory to be the gateway for their foundry business. But look at the result so far. TELLS YOU IT'S NOT SO EASY."** ⚠️ **This is the incumbent monopolist arguing against a challenger's go-to-market, so discount it accordingly — but note WHAT it concedes and what it does not. It concedes EMIB may work as packaging; it denies the funnel. The falsifier is specific and testable: a customer that starts at Intel on EMIB and subsequently moves front-end wafers to Intel 14A/18A. Absent that, TSMC's read is that EMIB volume is a packaging annuity for Intel and a wafer annuity for TSMC.** **Separately from the same call, relevant to the tool-competition question on this page: TSMC says memory makers and *"someone in Texas"* are paying premiums for critical tools but that it will still get its allocation on multi-decade counterparty-durability grounds — see the [[ASML]] page.** |
| **08-24** | **@DylanOnChips** (second-hand share reporting) | **competicao** | **bear** | 🔴 *"**Intel's share in the x86 CPU market for PCs and servers fell under 70 percent for the first time since 1995, marking a 31-year low** … even as **AI spending fueled historic growth for its data center business**."* ➜ **Both halves are the Intel debate: the segment is growing fast enough that DC revenue can set records while share keeps falling to [[AMD]].** ⚠️ Single relay, no source house, no exact figure, no client/server split. |
| **08-24** | **Hot Chips 2026 — live coverage (@firstadopter)** | **produto** | **mixed** | Three independent Intel disclosures in one day: **a new FAN-OUT FABRIC architecture** (relevant to the EMIB thread already on this page), **"Scalable Compute Building Blocks"**, **Diamond Rapids specs + an agentic-AI case**, and **18A-P benefits**, alongside an exec on *"what a data center CPU needs today."* ⚠️ **Conference tweets with slide photos, no talk text — establishes WHAT and WHEN, not merit. No perf number, yield or customer was legible.** |
| **08-24** | **The Information · Martin Peers** (citing NVDA filings) | **capital** | **bull** | [[NVDA]]'s public-equity stakes were marked **$18.4bn (Mar-31) → $63.0bn (Jun-30)**, with the step-up attributed partly to *"increases in the value of stakes in companies such as **Intel** and [[CRWV]]."* ➜ **Intel is now a marked-up position on its largest customer-competitor's balance sheet.** |

**Quarter synthesis:** the sell-side warmed up (Bernstein PT $100, Apple deal +8-9%, JPM impressed with 18A) and the process narrative became the bull side — but the debate shifted to the *execution holes*: EMIB yield well below CoWoS, long-term server-CPU share erosion (UBS) against Redburn's contrarian read (60% in 2026), and the size of the capital hole (SemiAnalysis sees $20-35B; Arcuri disagrees). On Jun-24, the disclosure of the Pelosi trade ($1M-$5M in INTC calls expiring March 2027, executed May 29) was read by the market as a signal of external conviction in a potential 2H-2026 catalyst (14A customer announcements). **06-30:** the packaging debate crystallized into two opposing expert reads — MS Amir Amerian relays the EMIB-T bull case (perf/cost edge >9.7 reticle, higher sample yields), while a former-AMD-director expert call calls CoWoS the 'gold standard' and doubts Intel can scale packaging without major external wins; DIGITIMES also poured cold water on the Intel–UMC (12nm→Intel 3) tie-up rumor on process-gap/cash grounds. A separate Capstone expert call (former Samsung EVP, now a photonics-startup CEO) sharpened the foundry-gap bear case further, seeing Intel as having "no chance" to catch TSMC's leading edge within 2-3 years and flagging that internal Intel products are increasingly shifting to TSMC. Backfilling the pre-quarter record, SemiAnalysis's Feb-2026 "CPUs are Back" deep-dive (Wong/Patel) supplies the single most detailed and most bearish Diamond Rapids product read on file: the quantified ~40%-faster-than-Granite-Rapids-only claim (SMT removal), the outright cancellation of mainstream Diamond Rapids-SP, and confirmed dead-ends for Sierra Forest/Clearwater Forest's successor — balanced by the Q4'25 DCAI beat/capex-guide-up inflection and Coral Rapids' return of SMT (though not before late-2028/early-2029). Barron's comparators (AMD data-center crossover in 2026E; Nvidia's stand-alone CPU business already "roughly the size" of Intel's DCAI; Intel now profiting from OpenAI/Mistral stakes) round out the quarter's cross-broker context. **07-01:** the EMIB-T packaging debate moved from "telegraphed advantage" to a load-bearing external dependency — JPMorgan's MediaTek note now gates part of MediaTek's TPU v9 ("Humufish") unit upside on Intel's EMIB-T yield progress, and UBS corroborates real traction but flags 2028 resource/capacity constraints that push Intel to prioritize internal + Google/MediaTek first, i.e. genuine demand but a rationing problem, not unlimited capacity. Cutting the other way, The Information reports Terafab (Musk's Tesla/SpaceX fab JV) poached 18-year Intel veteran and former Intel Arizona fab manager Gary Jiang as its first major hire — a concrete talent-departure signal alongside Semiecosystem's report that Intel broke ground on a new Santa Clara photomask facility (Intel Mask Operations expansion), a smaller but tangible leading-edge supply-chain investment. **07-02:** SemiAnalysis's ECTC 2026 roundup is the strongest technical validation yet for the EMIB-T bull case — Intel was the largest presenter (12 papers), and EMIB-T (EMIB + TSVs) is called "the most credible alternative to TSMC's CoWoS," with the disclosures explaining why it's expected in Google's TPU v9 (36/35 µm→25 µm bump pitch, quarter-panel packages, TSVs cutting DC voltage drop 68-80%, HBM4E @12 Gb/s), plus an industry-first 510×515 mm glass-core panel — though SemiAnalysis is careful to add EMIB-T is "still behind CoWoS" on DTC/eDTC/IVR/active LSI and "still catching up to an ecosystem executing in volume for years." UBS's Sector Keys reiterates the bear-side server-CPU erosion (Intel 60%→29% by 2030) while noting Intel outsourcing to TSMC "could stay for longer." **New from 07-05/07-06:** two fresh sell-side marks bracket the setup into the Q2 print — **Goldman Sachs (Schneider) opens 2Q with Neutral / PT $150** (30x $5.00 normalized EPS), expecting modest 2Q upside but calling the structural-turnaround thesis "largely priced in"; and, more constructively, **Bernstein (Dai) upgrades the framing to Outperform / PT $100** inside its ASML High-NA note, singling Intel out as the **FIRST to adopt High-NA EUV (2028, A14)** ahead of Samsung/TSMC — a process-leadership read-through that reinforces the bull side's 18A/14A-execution pillar, even as the valuation debate (BofA UP $96, GS Neutral $150) stays wide. **07-07:** UBS (Arcuri), initiating on SpaceX/SPCX, sharply **raises its INTC PT to $122 (Neutral, from $40)** on a five-segment SOTP and adds the quarter's most concrete Terafab-to-INTC read: INTC has *already engaged* with Terafab and may be helping it secure tool slots, with UBS framing a potential **"knowledge owner"/tech-licensing** structure (IBM-AMD→GFS analogue) or a JV site ("Ohio One") — a genuine foundry/licensing optionality that cuts *against* the pure-competitor framing of the 07-01 Gary Jiang talent-poaching story, though both can be true (INTC monetizes know-how while a well-funded new fab rises). The Terafab thesis (a potential >$50B/yr WFE spender, ~$135B WFE over 5yrs) also reinforces the semicap-WFE-to-~$300B-by-C2029 backdrop relevant to INTC's own tool suppliers. **07-08/07-09 (TSMC-primary read-throughs):** two cross-reads from the JPM/Gokul and GS TSMC previews cut opposite ways on Intel. On foundry share, **JPM/Gokul frames the leading-edge gap as durable** — even on execution, Intel+Samsung take only **~5-6% of 2nm in the first two waves (2028-29) vs TSMC ~90-95%**, and TSMC stays cheaper on an adjusted basis despite a lower sticker (price isn't the lever that wins Intel share). But on packaging, **GS delivers a genuinely positive IFS datapoint** — MediaTek's "HomoFish" Google-TPU builds on **Intel IMT**, with bumping/assembly yield already **~90% (substrate ~70%)** and ~1.5yr to ramp — a concrete advanced-packaging win read that sits alongside (and is distinct from) the still-contested EMIB-T interposer story. **07-11 (Apple foundry win):** WSJ reported [[AAPL]] secured a **semiconductor-tariff exemption for committing to Intel's fabs**, extending to **iPhone chips (not just Mac)** — the biggest external-foundry-commitment datapoint yet in the Apple-Intel thread (building on the mid-June deal that drove INTC +8-9% on 06-18), and, if it holds at scale, the marquee external-customer validation the whole foundry thesis hinges on. Node/volume/timing are unspecified and prior Apple-Intel reports drew TSMC-leverage skepticism (Fubon), so the Street will want substance at the 2H-2026 analyst day (WSJ / @jukan05, 07-11). **07-11/12/13 (the CPU-preview cluster):** the setup into the print turns more constructive on the DCAI/server-CPU side. **JPM's proprietary server-CPU model (07-12)** cuts mixed for Intel — a **~25% x86 CAGR** (server units +20%/yr, vs flattish-to-LSD prior cycle) is a genuine volume tailwind for Xeon, but **ARM rising to ~43% of the blended server-CPU market by 2028** (from ~22%) keeps the structural share-loss overhang intact. **Mizuho (Rakesh, 07-11) initiates the page Neutral $135**, framing Intel a technology-enabler of the >$1.4T-2028E capex ramp but "on the sidelines" on the foundry roadmap + AI-accelerator lag + newly-flagged wafer/memory-supply tightness. The single biggest new mark: **BofA (Arya, 07-13) carries INTC at Buy $160** (per the note's ratings table) — a two-notch step up from the page's prior BofA Underperform $96 — on stronger server-CPU pricing/demand offsetting the PC headwind, ~$40bn+ CY30 server-CPU sales (~25% of a $170bn TAM), Products OpM +~600bps in Q1, and the external-foundry option (Apple/MediaTek/Terafab/ARM). Net: the server-CPU $ story (ASP-led growth + agentic-AI TAM) is now a broad bull thread even as long-term *share* erosion to AMD/ARM stays the unresolved bear axis — and BofA's flip to Buy meaningfully widens the house dispersion (BofA Buy $160 / GS Neutral $150 / Mizuho Neutral $135 / UBS Neutral $122 / Bernstein OP $100). **07-13→15 (Twitter/X + Outlook flow):** the pre-print signal turned decisively bullish on *execution*, with the first tape-negative of the run appearing on governance. On process/yield, **KeyBanc (via @firstadopter, 07-14) put 18A yields at >85% (from 65% last quarter)** — the largest single step-up on the page's most-watched metric — plus INTC-3 capacity for +25-30% server-unit growth this year (>50% next), a general-purpose server-outlook raise to +30% (from +18.5%), and an aggressive (Intel-unconfirmed) claimed design-win list spanning AMD/NVDA/MRVL/MSFT/MU/OpenAI beyond Apple. Two concrete in-sourcing tells corroborate the yield story: Intel's **€5B Leixlip Fab 34 expansion** for Xeon on Intel 3 (>$24B total; @IntelProMUltra reads it as captive, not external, capacity), and reports that **Nova Lake-S is being pulled ~80-90% in-house onto 18A** (from ~60-70%-to-TSMC) — the latter directly cutting against the June MS bus-tour read that Nova Lake desktop would stay at TSMC. 18A was also reaffirmed as the **US 2nm first-mover** (Samsung's Tesla A15 tape-out "just behind Intel's 18A") and **first with High-NA EUV for a volume part** (Moorhead), reinforcing Bernstein's High-NA-first thesis. The one clear negative: **INTC fell ~6% on 07-14 on unease over "Trump's heavy hand" in the turnaround** — the government-equity overhang moving from theoretical risk to a realized tape event. **07-17:** two corporate-access read-throughs cap the window — ASML's London roadshow calls Intel the **first customer running High-NA EUV in production on 18A** ("a bit of a validation of where we stand with High-NA") and flags **CPU demand now driving incremental Intel capacity, visible in ASML's order intake** (a fresh demand/execution tell reinforcing the 18A-first + in-sourcing threads); and Jefferies (with Fubon, post-TSMC 2Q) reads TSMC's public "welcome" to other advanced-packaging suppliers as **positive for Intel + MediaTek**, tempered by Taiwan rumors of a slow learning-curve on Intel's IBP packaging. **07-15→20 (CPU-Renaissance cluster + the 7/23-print setup):** BofA's "Big Tech setups lightning round" (Arya, 07-20) expects a **server-CPU beat by ≥$300-500M** but flags **gross margin** as the swing item (18A lower-yielding + pricier TSMC wafers + a CFO habit of beating then guiding GM down), calling the stock — off 30-40% from peak — "worth engaging." A Kaizad ex-Intel-VP expert call (07-16) frames advanced packaging as a **"Coke-Pepsi 70/30" duopoly** where Intel (the Pepsi) is **capped at 20-30% packaging share without also winning wafer-foundry share**, its edge being **known-good-die die-level test** and being the only credible 2nd source (Google TPU evaluating EMIB-T); and JPM's TSMC-post-earnings read has TSMC **accommodating Intel on packaging overflow but "not leaving revenue on the table" on front-end wafers** — the two-door framing (packaging open, wafers closed). On the fund side, **Octahedron's 2Q26 LP call (07-15)** escalates its variant bull case (long "vindicated," ~$40→~$100, FY-next-yr EPS lifted to $5-6.50, path to $150/$100+). Backfilled: the actual **Bernstein "CPU Renaissance? $223bn TAM"** report (06-17, PT $100 already logged) adds EPS 2026E/27E/28E $1.07/$1.50/$1.96 and the Coral-Rapids-closes-the-gap framing.
